- **ALL EIGHT DASHBOARD PROFILES ARE ONE CODEBASE WITH AN ATYPICAL *SHAPE* — tsc's OWN SOURCES, **78 FILES AVERAGING 128 KB** — SO A COST THAT IS PER-**FILE** RATHER THAN PER-**BYTE** IS STRUCTURALLY INEXPRESSIBLE ON EVERY ONE OF THEM (2026-08-30, (INC.57): a QUADRATIC front-end row survived ~950 rounds).** `extractRelativeImports` rebuilt a set of every program file name on entry and is called twice per file: `2 x 78^2` is twelve thousand probes and vanishes, where the same code on an application-shaped project (thousands of ~1 KB files) read **18.9 / 76.3 / 331.6 ms at 601 / 1201 / 2401 files — 4x the cost for 2x the files**, 20% of the incremental floor. **THREE INDEPENDENT QUADRATICS WERE FOUND THIS WAY IN ONE SESSION, IN THREE SUBSYSTEMS, TAKING A 2,401-FILE PROJECT'S PER-KEYSTROKE FLOOR 1,653 -> 279 ms**: the emit-order import scan ((INC.57)), `checkJsxImportResolutions` ((INC.58)) — **709.7 ms of a 774.7 ms floor pass table (92%) on a project with NO JSX in it**, where (INC.54)(a) had ranked it at **1.2 ms** from the tsc profile, i.e. **600x** — and `parsedSourceFiles.filter { it.key !in transformOrder.toSet() }` ((INC.59)), whose `.toSet()` sat INSIDE the lambda and which runs in the **`--noEmit`** path, i.e. 175 ms per keystroke preparing an emit order that is never used. **THE SECOND AND THIRD WERE FOUND BY RE-READING THE FLOOR AFTER THE ROUND BEFORE THEM, NOT FROM THE QUEUE: the ranking changed three times in one session and each new top row was one no queue item had named**, so re-decompose after every round that moves the floor rather than inheriting an ordering. **So the law bites RANKINGS, not just prices: any per-pass ordering taken on a dashboard profile must be re-taken on the many-small shape before one of its rows is opened.** **Before pricing ANY front-end or floor row, ask whether the profile's shape can express the cost at all**; generate the other shape with `scripts/gen-many-small-project.py` and read it with `scripts/floor-decomposition.sh` — the instrument that finds this class is to **divide the floor pass table by FILE COUNT at two program sizes** (an honestly O(program) pass reads a constant µs/file; a quadratic one doubles). Same law as (INC.9)'s "a cost prior does not transfer across REGIMES", on a new axis. Both fixes together took that project's per-keystroke floor **1,653 -> 366 ms**.
- **THE INTELLIJ HOST RUNS *ONE COMPILER THREAD PER `tsconfig.json`* IN ONE JVM, SO EVERY PROCESS-GLOBAL CACHE HERE IS REACHED CONCURRENTLY — A SHAPE NO FIXTURE, PROFILE OR CORPUS BASELINE PRODUCES (2026-08-31, (INC.67), found by reading `xemantic/xtsc-intellij-plugin`).** Its `XtscService` keeps a `ConcurrentHashMap` of `XtscSession`s, each with its own single-thread executor. `RealLibSnapshots.parseCache`/`declIndexCache` and `RealLibResolver.resolutionCache` therefore publish **copy-on-write behind `@Volatile`** — a reader sees the old complete map or the new complete one — and a NEW process-global cache must do the same; `prewarmParsedLibFiles` is NOT the mitigation (it covers `--workers` inside ONE compile and says nothing about two sessions). A lost race costs a RECOMPUTATION, which was always possible (`getOrPut` on a `HashMap` is not atomic either) and is benign **for the mirror of round 471's reason**: the identity sets these feed compare `Node`s STRUCTURALLY, so two parses of the same lib text are interchangeable to every consumer. A per-INSTANCE cache (`ModuleResolver`'s) needs none of this.
- **A *BLOCKED* PAIRED RUN MANUFACTURES A REPRODUCIBLE DELTA IN A REGION WITH NO CAUSAL PATH TO THE CHANGE, AND A LARGER PER-ARM DRAW COUNT DOES NOT FIX IT — 12 DRAWS PER ARM READ **+2.70 ms** ON `include/exclude regex match` (which calls no `PathUtil.normalize`) AND **+3.39** ON ITS CONTAINING ROW, BOTH OF WHICH *INVERTED* TO -0.24 / -4.51 UNDER ABBA ROTATION (2026-08-31, (INC.68)).** Three `before` processes run before three `after` ones is enough: the box drifts, and the drift lands entirely on whichever arm went first. **The tell is a stable delta in a region you cannot connect to the edit** — treat that as evidence the ORDER is the variable, never as a mechanism to hunt. Rotate arms across PROCESSES (round 867: each arm needs its own JVM) even when both arms are separate class directories rather than two flags.
- **`PathUtil.normalize` IS A FIXED-POINT FUNCTION FOR ~80% OF WHAT THIS COMPILER ASKS IT — 9,584 OF 11,935 CALLS PER FLOOR BUILD — SO ITS FAST PATH IS LOAD-BEARING AND ITS PREDICATE (`PathUtil.isNormalized`) MUST STAY CONSERVATIVE IN ONE DIRECTION ONLY (2026-08-31, (INC.68)).** A false NEGATIVE costs the old allocating path; a false POSITIVE returns an un-normalized path, which resolves to a DIFFERENT FILE and per (CFG.1) is invisible to every diagnostic channel here. **The corpus cannot see this at all** (it materialises no directory), so the instruments are the 8-profile grid, `output.programFiles` in `cost_gate.py`, and `PathNormalizeFastPathTest`'s value pins over the ACCEPTED population against a transcribed reference — a second implementation, because a differential whose two arms are one function cannot see a fast path.
- **A PIN'S *ENVIRONMENT* IS PART OF ITS SPECIFICATION, AND TWO WAYS OF FORGETTING THAT BOTH BIT IN ONE ROUND (2026-08-31, (INC.67)).** A pin that compares two successive reads of a process-global by IDENTITY assumes a QUIESCENT process — the suite publishes into these maps from elsewhere, so it passed in isolation and failed in the full run; state the property that survives concurrency instead (a map already handed out never grows). And CLAUDE.md's "never put a `SourceFile` subexpression in a power-assert assertion" is not theoretical: a cache map holding `SourceFile`s inside `assert(...)` turned the failure into an **`OutOfMemoryError` in the diagram builder**, masking the real cause. Compute booleans into locals first.
- **A ROTATED BATCH OF A *COMPOSITE* QUANTITY STILL CANNOT SEPARATE TWO OF ITS TERMS — THE INCREMENTAL FLOOR WALL CARRIES A ±20 ms CONCURRENT TERM, SO IT CANNOT RESOLVE A 7 ms SEQUENTIAL CHANGE HOWEVER MANY DRAWS IT IS GIVEN (2026-08-31, (INC.72), which RETRACTS two wall figures from its own session).** Two rounds read the rotated floor wall at ~3x what their pass row explained and the gap was queued as a mechanism; the per-PHASE instrument over the SAME two binaries then attributed **init-block pass dispatch -14.81 ms** — exactly what the rows said — while the UNTOUCHED crawl swung **+18.01** in the same run (its `read+decode` sum, elapsed-with-suspension across `Dispatchers.Default` workers, moved 147.8 -> 249.9). **For a checker-side floor change the receipt is `FrontEnd`'s phase row plus the deterministic population count; the floor wall is a sanity check.** (INC.68)'s law is the step before this one: a BLOCKED batch invents a delta and rotation removes it; rotation does NOT remove a real, large, unrelated phase. `FloorAbMain`'s `fe` mode is the two-binary phase decomposition (`scripts/inc69-floor-ab.sh` runs the wall arms).

- **`FrontEnd.READ` PRICES THE CRAWL'S *THREAD HANDOFF*, NOT THE READ — IT BRACKETS A `withContext`, SO IT IS ELAPSED-WITH-SUSPENSION, AND SERVING **33,350 READS FROM MEMORY** MOVED THE CRAWL'S WALL BY **NOTHING** ON A 2,401-FILE PROJECT WHILE HALVING IT ON tsc's 78 HUGE SOURCES (2026-08-31, (INC.56)).** The read is a **BYTE** cost (10 MB of UTF-8 decode is real; 2,401 reads of ~1 KB over 16 workers are ~1-2 ms), and the row's per-FILE call count is what makes it read as a per-file one. So a crawl candidate priced off that row is priced off a LOCATION: the fix that pays on BOTH shapes removes the HANDOFF (`Vfs.readTextIfResident`, answered for content already in memory), not the read. (INC.64)'s law one hop later.
- **`Vfs.retainRead` IS CALLED FROM THE CRAWL'S SINGLE-THREADED FOLD AND `readText`/`readTextIfResident` MUST STAY READ-ONLY — populating a retention map from `readText` is round 825's data race, because the crawl reads from N concurrent workers (2026-08-31, (INC.56)).** Same discipline and same fold as `CrawlParseCache.store`; the natural design (retain what you just read, where you read it) is the wrong one. `OverlayVfs`'s `retained` and `contents` are additionally DISJOINT by construction (`put` drops the retained entry, `retainRead` refuses an overlaid path), which makes the lookup ORDER between them a measured REDUNDANT guard — round 927's pair, recorded rather than claimed.
- **A PASS ROW IS NOT A GATEABLE POOL: (INC.74)'s ~10 ms OF "WHOLE-PROGRAM WALKERS" IS **~1.2 ms** ONCE READ — TWO ARE ALREADY PARTITION-SCOPED AND WHAT REMAINS IN THEIR ROW IS THE COLLECTOR ROUND 609 FORBIDS GATING, ONE IS PURE COLLECTOR, AND THREE WERE REFUSED BY EARLIER ROUNDS (2026-08-31, (INC.77)).** An (INC.20) MIXED pass keeps its `binderResults` collector loop and shows its FULL cost in the row, so ranking gating candidates off `--passTiming` over-counts the pool by ~8x. **And narrowing the wrong loop is silent: `checkCrossFileUseBeforeDeclaration`'s `fileIdx` comes from `binderResults.withIndex()` and is compared against the indices its COLLECTOR recorded, so a mechanical rewrite to `checkedResults.withIndex()` renumbers it and makes a use-before-declaration verdict a function of the PARTITION.** Read what an index MEANS before narrowing the loop that produces it — the sibling of CLAUDE.md's "a `for (result in binderResults)` loop is not evidence of a tail walker". **RESOLVED 2026-09-01 ((INC.89)(b)), AND THE FIX IS THE GENERAL RULE: WHEN A PASS'S VERDICT COMPARES ORDINALS OF ITS OWN ENUMERATION, THE EMITTER IS NARROWED BY A `continue` *AFTER* `binderResults.withIndex()` — NEVER BY CHANGING THE LOOP HEAD**, because filtering after the enumeration cannot renumber while re-heading always does (ablated: re-heading invents a TS2448 no unpartitioned build produces, and only ONE of seven pins can see it). What licenses the narrowing at all is that the row is anchored at the USE file and non-null, so `getDiagnostics()` was already discarding it — check both of those before narrowing any emitter, since a `fileName = null` row SURVIVES the partition filter.
- **THE CRAWL'S FAST DRAIN SERVES A FILE FROM THE PARSE CACHE, SO ITS GATE MUST STAY THE CONTENT-AND-FLAGS `lookup` — A PATH-KEYED `peek` SERVES THE **PREVIOUS KEYSTROKE'S TREE** AND NO COUNTER, ORDER PIN OR CORPUS BASELINE NOTICES (2026-09-01, (INC.85)).** The one instrument that sees it is a fixture with an UNSAVED BUFFER — resident, carrying NEW bytes, over a stale cached tree — and the obvious fixture does not have that shape: an `edit()` that drops retention makes the file non-resident, the fast drain is never entered, and the staleness arm reads 0 RED as a DEAD arm. `CrawlAdaptiveDrainTest`; `Vfs.hasResidentContent()` (defaulted `false`, so an un-opted-in `Vfs` takes the old path and probes nothing) gates the classification, and `OverlayVfs` answers it from `retained` and NOT from `contents` — O(open editors) against a wave's O(program) can never pay, which its KDoc records so it is not "fixed" back.
- **THE WHOLE-PROGRAM REGEX LAW EXTENDS FROM SOURCE *TEXT* TO FILE *NAMES*, AND `docs/perf/whole-program-regex-census.md` CENSUSES ONLY THE TEXT SCANNERS — SO A NAME-SCANNING REGEX IS OUTSIDE IT AND UNCENSUSED: `checkMissingTypesReferenceExports`' `(?:^|/)node_modules/…` IS ALTERNATION-ROOTED, CARRIES NO BOYER-MOORE LITERAL, AND WAS ATTEMPTED AT EVERY POSITION OF ALL 2,401 FILE NAMES FOR **3.30 ms OF A 4.51 ms `FrontEnd.POST_DIAGS`** ON A PROJECT WITH NO `node_modules` AT ALL (2026-09-01, (INC.87)(a)).** The fix is the usual one — an EXACT literal pre-gate (`endsWith("/package.json")`, implied by the pattern's own `$`-anchored tail) with the regex kept LIVE as the decider — and it generalises: hoist any `Regex` out of a per-compile body, where it is recompiled every call. **AND A `package.json` IS NEVER A PROGRAM INPUT UNDER `ProjectCompiler`** (its own comment, `ProjectCompiler.kt:353`), so any pin for a mechanism reading one out of `parsed.files` must use the multi-file `@Filename` harness — through `ProjectCompiler` it reads an empty diagnostic list and, written as an ABSENCE assertion, is green forever.
- **A KOTLIN *PACKAGE* IS NOT A SCOPE, AND MODELLING IT AS ONE IS SILENT AND TOTAL: `resolveSpelling`'s innermost-first walk STOPS at the first scope declaring a dotted name's head, so putting a package's segments in the spelling set makes `node.node.console` (the `node:console` twin under the root `node`) declare the name `node` in the scope `node` — and `node.net.Socket` read from `node.http` then resolves to NOTHING (2026-09-04, (EXT.24)).** It emptied the whole cross-module override attribution of the externals generator with no error anywhere; **it is invisible at three modules and total at 51**, so a per-module design must be exercised at the real fixture's width before it is believed. `Inheritance.resolveHere` separates the package half (absolute, matched against the known package prefixes) from the declaration half, and `spellHere` refuses a partial package prefix as a spelling — Kotlin has no relative package resolution, so `alpha.Socket` names nothing from `node.beta` however `node.alpha.Socket` resolves.
- **A CROSS-PACKAGE `override` NEEDS AN `open` THE OWNING GENERATION CANNOT SEE — its own declarations are spelled locally while the foreign subclass's supertype names its package, and no single table holds both spellings (2026-09-04, (EXT.24); 115 `is final and cannot be overridden` on `@types/node` before it).** Compute such an attribution ONCE over a uniformly-lifted view of the whole set and restate it per generation; the same shape applies to any per-file rule whose input lives in another file of the same compilation.
- **A MULTI-FILE COMPILE GATE MUST PUT THE *FILE* IN ITS ERROR STRING — a line number over 51 generated sources names nothing (2026-09-04).** Same round: a census regex that counts `public fun` but not `public override fun` read a rung that ADDS supertypes as a loss of 221 declarations, i.e. a counting artefact indistinguishable from a regression.
- **A `Vfs` WRAPPER THAT DOES NOT OVERRIDE `listEntries` INHERITS THE INTERFACE DEFAULT — `list(path).map { VfsEntry(it, isDirectory(it)) }` — AND SO HANDS (INC.60)'s WHOLE SAVING BACK: `OverlayVfs` DID EXACTLY THAT ON THE SHIPPED PATH OF EVERY `Project` BUILD, AT **19.54 ms AGAINST 6.34** OVER 50 DIRECTORIES / 2,451 ENTRIES (2026-08-31, (INC.76)).** CLAUDE.md already said this about a COUNTING Vfs in a test; it is the same trap in production and it is SILENT, because the two paths agree on every answer and differ only in syscalls (`SystemVfs.isDirectory` is kotlinx-io's `metadataOrNull`, up to five `stat`s). **Any new wrapping `Vfs` must override `listEntries` or use `Vfs by delegate`** — and the general law: a defaulted interface member added for SPEED is a silent regression waiting for the next wrapper. The instrument that finds it is a row measured STANDALONE against the same row measured IN THE BUILD, with the gap treated as a fact to explain (`GlobListProbeMain`); the pin is a DIFFERENTIAL against the default body, since a wrong entry KIND drops a file from the program or adopts a directory as a root and (CFG.1) says nothing here notices. Note `isDirectoryCalls == 0` after a build is FALSE and correctly so — a build asks about specific PATHS — so that cost pin is a complexity claim at two program sizes.
- **A ROW WHOSE KEY IS AN AST *DATA CLASS* IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY ROUND 471 — `enclosingImportIndex`'s STRUCTURAL `hashCode` IS **76.5 ns, 0.72 ms, 14%** OF ITS 4.7 ms ROW, WHILE **~3.4 ms IS THE INSERT PLUS A `MutableList` AND A `Pair` ALLOCATED PER KEY** — because the census says every `getOrPut` MISSES (9,401 specifiers, 9,401 distinct keys) (2026-08-31, (INC.81)).** Price the hash in ONE timestamp pair over a second pass of the same population before touching a key whose structural semantics are load-bearing. **And `multiFileKeys=0` — not one key reached from two files — is the statement that NO REAL PROJECT EXERCISES THE MULTI-ENTRY PATH**, so a one-entry representation needs a fixture to pin its promotion: `ImportSpecifier`'s data-class components include `pos`/`end`, so two BYTE-IDENTICAL importers spell one key. Same law as (INC.57): a row no queue item names is found by re-taking the ranking, not by reading the queue.
- **A TWO-DRAW READ OF AN INSTRUMENTED ROW CANNOT SEE A **23%** CHANGE IN THAT VERY ROW, AND IT FAILS IN THE *REFUTING* DIRECTION — (INC.80) read 6.26/7.22 before against 6.22/7.45 after and was being written up as refuted when six draws per arm ROTATED ACROSS PROCESSES read **6.41 -> 4.95 ms, 3/3 batches, both rotation directions** (2026-08-31).** (INC.68)'s law is usually quoted about a blocked batch INVENTING a delta; it hides a real one just as easily. Two class dirs differing in one file plus an ABBA loop is the instrument, and it costs one extra build. **And the tempting explanation for "the microbenchmark says 6x and the build says nothing" — that the allocating arm pays GC the build's population never pays (round 801) — was MEASURED AND REFUTED here: a 2 GB young gen made BOTH arms slower.** Corollary for `PathUtil.join`: a module specifier's `..` is exactly what (INC.68)'s `isNormalized` must refuse, so a fast path for the already-normalized case never covered the crawl at all.
- **A PER-COMPONENT REFUSAL CAN BE RIGHT ABOUT ITS COMPONENT AND WRONG ABOUT THE PROGRAM — (INC.73)(a) CALLED THE CRAWL'S `exists` PROBES IRREDUCIBLE ("one per distinct resolution") AND **THE ROOT-FILE GLOB HAD ALREADY LISTED EVERY ONE OF THOSE FILES, OFF THE SAME `Vfs`, ~20 ms EARLIER IN THE SAME BUILD**: 2,351 syscalls -> 0 (2026-08-31, (INC.79)).** Before inheriting a floor refusal, ask WHO ELSE IN THE BUILD ALREADY KNOWS THE ANSWER. `ModuleResolver` now memoizes `exists`/`isDirectory` per build (no new assumption — (INC.65)'s answer memo is strictly stronger and has the same one-build lifetime) and is seeded from the glob; **the seed may only say YES**, because a file can exist and be EXCLUDED from the program, and a seed read as an authoritative file list resolves a real import to nothing. **And keying such a memo by anything less than the WHOLE path is a wrong PROGRAM that every count pin passes** — the basename-keyed ablation reddened only the counters until a pin asking for a file that exists in ONE of two directories was added.
- **AN *ACCEPTING* REGEX CANNOT BE SHORT-CIRCUITED BY A REFUSAL FILTER, SO A PER-ITEM REGEX ROW DOMINATED BY THE *MATCHING* PATTERN NEEDS AN EXACT SHORTCUT AND NOT A PRE-GATE — THE ROOT-FILE GLOB'S EXCLUDE HALF WAS **191 ns/candidate** AND ITS INCLUDE HALF **2,239**, AND THE QUEUE'S OWN "refuse most candidates cheaply first" WAS AIMED AT THE HALF THAT WAS ALREADY CHEAP (2026-08-31, (INC.78)).** `GlobMatcher` answers the `<literal>` + `**` segment + bare `*` leaf + literal tail shape from the head and the tail, keeping the regex as the ORACLE (round 792's shape) — and the equivalence is exact ONLY for a boundary-aligned head and a path with no doubled separator, because `(?:[^/]+/)*[^/]*` matches every remainder EXCEPT one with an EMPTY SEGMENT; both cases fall back to the regex rather than to a second rule guessing at them. **Gate it with a DIFFERENTIAL against that regex, never a green suite** — (CFG.1): a wrong root-file set has no diagnostic channel here. **And the perf receipt is a COUNT (`FrontEnd.globRegexEvals`, 4,802 -> 0), because the same one-process wall ratio read 12x / 5x / 3x over four processes of one binary.**
- **A PIN ABOUT A FILE CHANGING ON DISK CANNOT GO THROUGH `Project.diagnostics()` — (INC.46) NARROWS THE CHECK TO THE FILES THE HOST *REPORTED* AS DIRTY AND REUSES THE PREVIOUS ROWS FOR THE REST, SO AN UNREPORTED CHANGE IS INVISIBLE THROUGH IT WHATEVER THE `Vfs` DID (2026-08-31, (INC.56), two pins re-written after their first run).** It fails as a defect in whichever arm ran first, which is the confusing direction. Force the whole-program build (`Project.files`) so the only variable left is the read.

- **A FAMILY CAN HAVE *ZERO* CORPUS COVERAGE AND STILL BE LOAD-BEARING — `moduleInterfaceNames` / `multiFileModuleTypeNames` (rounds 471/513) ABLATE TO **0 RED** OVER THE ~13k BASELINES AND REDDEN **3 OF THE 8 PROFILES** (+2 rows each: harness, server, services) (2026-08-31, (INC.73)).** That is where their evidence came from in the first place — round 471's was the SERVICES self-compile (an un-memoized `isLibPhantomMemberOfModuleInterface` doubled it, 39 s -> 77 s) and round 513's is tsc's private-codefix `Info` shape, a name declared as an interface in two module files. **So a green corpus under an ablation is not "this is dead"; ablate AND grid before concluding anything about a mechanism's coverage**, and expect the answer to be somewhere for a reason its own round note usually records.

- **`SystemVfs.exists` IS *ONE* SYSCALL — 1.02x `java.io.File.exists`, MEASURED — SO (INC.60)'s FIVE-STAT FINDING IS SPECIFIC TO `metadataOrNull` AND DOES NOT GENERALISE (2026-08-31, (INC.73); `ExistsProbeMain` re-takes it).** And `ModuleResolver`'s probe ladder is already optimal for the ordinary shape: **2,351 `exists` + 10 `isDirectory` per build for 2,351 distinct resolutions**, i.e. exactly one probe each, because `.ts` is first in `allExtensions`. There is no syscall lever in the crawl's specifier-resolution row; what is unexamined there is the non-syscall remainder.

- **A PASS IS NOT DEFERRABLE BECAUSE ITS PRODUCT'S READERS ARE ALL "CHECKING" — ASK WHEN THOSE READERS *RUN* (2026-08-31, (INC.73)(b)).** `init:collectUmdGlobalsAndModuleFiles` and `init:mergeFileLocalsIntoGlobals` look exactly like (INC.70)/(INC.71)'s shape and are not: `umdGlobalNames` is read by `moduleLocalContributesGlobally`, which the MERGE itself calls, and `moduleFiles` by `collectModuleAugmentations`, which `init:mergeModuleAugmentations` dispatches — both LATER INIT PASSES that run unconditionally, so a floor build forces the index whatever the checking path does. The census that decides a deferral must be taken at the readers, and a reader inside `init` answers on every build.

- **FOR ANYTHING IN THE INV.3 VISIBILITY MODEL THE `-project` PINS GATE THE *REGIME* AND THE CORPUS GATES THE *ANSWER* — TWO ROUNDS RUNNING A HAND-WRITTEN VALUE PIN COULD NOT DISCRIMINATE THE MECHANISM WHILE AN ABLATION REDDENED THE CORPUS IN THE HUNDREDS (2026-08-31, (INC.70) 503 tests, (INC.71) 492).** Both fixtures asserted the thing the mechanism is *for* — a module file's local not leaking into another file — and both stayed GREEN with the mechanism disabled, because on a hand-written shape the name is not in the merged `globals` either, so the set has nothing to add. **Write the `-project` pin as a COUNT** (`EagerIndexCensus.perFileScopeBuilds` / `perFileVisibilityBuilds`: 0 on a floor build, 1 or `binderResults.size` on a checking one) **and take the value receipt by ABLATING against the corpus**, which is a measurement and costs one suite run.

- **A DEFERRAL THAT CHANGES *WHEN* A PROBE'S INPUT IS BUILT MUST KEEP THE PROBE'S INSTALL WHERE IT WAS AND FORCE FROM INSIDE IT, OR `globals.lookups` MOVES (2026-08-31, (INC.71)).** `InstrumentedSymbolTable.onLookup` counts NOTHING while it is null, so installing the INV.3(a) classifier lazily would have silently dropped every lookup before the first ask — a `cost_gate.py` counter, not a diagnostic. Installed at the pass's own moment with `ensurePerFileVisibility()` as its first statement, the counters read +0.00%. **The stated cost of that choice: under FULL `--passTiming` the work lands on whichever pass performs the first `globals` lookup rather than on its own row** (at the `rows` tier `globals` is not instrumented, so the floor table is honest).

- **THE EXACTNESS OF A DEFERRED PER-FILE TABLE IS AN *INIT-ORDER* FACT, AND `buildPerFileScopes`' SNAPSHOT EXISTED PRECISELY TO SURVIVE A LATER MUTATION — THE ONE WRITER OF A `BinderResult.locals` IS `collectModuleAugmentations`, DISPATCHED BY `init:mergeModuleAugmentations`, WHICH RUNS EARLIER (2026-08-31, (INC.70)).** So the eager and lazy snapshots are the same table; **a writer scheduled AFTER that pass would make them disagree silently, and nothing here would print it.** Per-file scopes are now built on first ask (`buildPerFileScopeFor`, pinned by `EagerIndexCensus.perFileScopeBuilds` — 0 on a floor build, `binderResults.size` on a full one). Two corollaries measured in the same round: **`perFileScopeOf`'s one-entry IDENTITY memo absorbs every repeated ask for one file, so the `perFileScope` MAP's memoization is pinned by nothing** (an arm that rebuilds per ask reads 0 RED even on a fixture with several unresolved names in one file); and **a value pin on module-local visibility does NOT discriminate `perFileScope` at all** — `moduleOnlyGlobalNames` decides that upstream — so the receipt that the table is load-bearing is the CORPUS, where never building one reddens **503** tests.

- **A CROSS-BINARY A/B RUNNER MAY READ NO COUNTER THAT DOES NOT EXIST IN *BOTH* ARMS — the two arms are loaded by the SAME runner class, so the older one dies with `NoSuchMethodError` and the batch prints ONE arm's medians as if they were both, which reads exactly like a successful run (2026-08-31, (INC.70)).** Populations belong in the pins, which run against one binary at a time; `scripts/inc69-floor-ab.sh` + `FloorAbMain` are the floor-only ABBA harness and deliberately read none.

- **A *PLATEAU* OF NEAR-IDENTICAL PASS PRICES IS A SHARED PER-FILE COST, NOT A COINCIDENCE OF WHAT THE PASSES DO — AND A TOP-N RANKING CANNOT SHOW A PLATEAU, ONLY A DISTRIBUTION CAN (2026-08-31, (INC.69)).** The init-block dispatch had been recorded as "FLAT across ~400 passes, so there is no row to make cheaper" ((INC.66)); the HISTOGRAM says **418 rows, 44 carrying 94% and 367 carrying 0.82 ms**, with 21 of the 44 at an identical 0.39-0.55 ms — 21 corpus PIN walkers sharing one line, a whole-program loop whose first act is `fileName.substringAfterLast('/') != "<one literal>"`. `Checker.filesNamed` is now the one basename index; **the redundant `!=` guard stays VERBATIM in every body** (a round-927 pair: the index buys the speed, the guard keeps the correctness — an ablation widening the index alone reddens nothing). **The corpus CANNOT gate this class**: its harness materialises no directory, so its file names are FLAT and `substringAfterLast('/')` returns the whole string, so an index keyed by the full path passes all ~13k baselines and silently stops pinning a real project's `src/dates/temporal.ts` — a MISSING diagnostic, which nothing in this repo prints. Any future file-name-keyed lookup needs a NESTED-path pin.

- **BEFORE PRICING A ROW, CHECK IT *HAS* A SPLIT — (INC.65)'s ENTIRE FINDING LIVED IN A RESIDUE NO SUB-ROW NAMED, AND THE ROUND BEFORE IT HAD JUST DECLARED THE FRONT END DONE (2026-08-30).** `FrontEnd.CRAWL` decomposed into `read+decode` and `pre-parse`, BOTH elapsed-with-suspension (i.e. locations, not prices), so the gap between them and the WALL was unattributed — and it was **half the row**: bracketing the crawl's sequential half (`FrontEnd.CRAWL_RESOLVE`) read **20.6-28.6 ms of a 44-60 ms wall** at 2,401 files. That is (INC.53)'s "ask what runs OUTSIDE a pass" with a sub-row-shaped twin, and the third time in this arc that ADDING an instrument — not reading one — is what found the cost. Corollary for any "the era of finding a stray quadratic is over" claim: it is a statement about the instruments, not the code.
- **MODULE RESOLUTION IS A PURE FUNCTION OF `(importerDir, specifier)` — `ModuleResolver.resolve` READS `importerPath` ONCE, FOR ITS `dirname`, AND NEVER AGAIN — SO ITS PER-BUILD MEMO'S KEY IS EXACT AND NOT A HEURISTIC (2026-08-30, (INC.65)).** Duplication on an application-shaped project is real (**4,701 resolutions over 2,351 distinct pairs**, factor 2.0; barrels raise it). Three things a future edit must keep: the memo is per `ModuleResolver`, i.e. per `ProjectCompiler.build`, which is what makes an ADDED file visible next build ((INC.48)) and is why it must NEVER become process-global; `null` is a real answer and must be memoized or every unresolved specifier re-probes the filesystem; and **DROPPING THE DIRECTORY FROM THE KEY IS SILENT AND CATASTROPHIC** — it resolves `./dep` in one directory to another's file, a wrong PROGRAM that (CFG.1) says this repo has no diagnostic channel to notice, so the pin is a VALUE one over two directories, never a count.
- **A CONCURRENCY SHAPE DESIGNED FOR THE *COLD* PATH IS PURE OVERHEAD ON THE WARM ONE, AND THE CRAWL PAID **TWO DISPATCHER HOPS PER FILE** TO SCHEDULE A ~1 us MAP PROBE (2026-08-30, (INC.64)).** `readAndScanBatch` hopped to `Dispatchers.Default` for EVERY file so a parse would never run on an IO thread — but on a warm incremental build every parse is a `CrawlParseCache` HIT. Measured over 2,401 files: sequential read **14.4 ms**, `flatMapMerge(16)` alone 17.2, one hop 18.5, the shipped two hops **32.1**, against a crawl WALL of 51-57 and 13-21 ms of actual reading. **The floor's `read+decode`/`pre-parse` CPU sums are elapsed-WITH-SUSPENSION and are LOCATIONS, not prices** — only the WALL is a price, and here it could not resolve the fix (overlapping ranges, and that run's own `full` median 9% slower), so the claim rests on the mechanism plus a synthetic ABBA arm and the pin is a COUNT (`CrawlParseCache.parseDispatches`, at two program sizes). Any new counter folded from those workers goes in the SINGLE-THREADED fold beside `hits`/`misses` — a `++` from the flow is round 825's race.
- **THE EMIT-ORDER REGION RUNS IN THE `--noEmit` PATH, AND THE CORPUS IS A *CONTROL* FOR ANYTHING GATED ON `skipEmitOutputs` — ALL ~13k BASELINES RUN WITH THE BRANCH TAKEN, BECAUSE ONLY `ProjectCompiler` SETS THAT FLAG (2026-08-30, (INC.64)(b), (INC.59)'s finding one call deeper).** `extractRelativeImports` ran twice per file to build a dependency ORDER whose every consumer emits — 15-23 ms of a ~170 ms incremental floor. What GATES such a change is the 8-profile `--noEmit` grid plus a `-project` pin, with an `--outDir` `diff -r` as the separate control that the emitting path is inert. **Two traps: the obvious edit is a `continue`, and `tsFileNames.add` sits BELOW the extraction where every later phase reads it; and a pin over an ORDER needs a fixture whose expected order differs from every order the system produces BY ACCIDENT** — named the obvious way round (`dep` imported by `main`) dependency order equals ALPHABETICAL order, and the value pin stayed green against a binary whose dependency edges had been emptied (`zdep`/`amain` is what discriminates).
- **A REFUSAL THAT NAMES A BLOCKER IS ONLY AS GOOD AS ITS SPLIT, AND (INC.54)(c) REFUSED A 46-50 ms ROW FOR SEVEN ROUNDS ON A MECHANISM THAT OWNS **3%** OF IT (2026-08-30, (INC.63)).** `parseBuiltinLib` was "BLOCKED on round 884's `mergedSymbols` clone-on-write" — true of the BIND, which measures **1.4 ms**; the other 97% was `RealLibResolver.resolve` (a `/// <reference lib=…/>` regex over ~3.7 MB of lib text, called **TWICE** per checker construction) and the decl-set walk (~30k puts into containers keyed by **data-class AST nodes**, i.e. round 471's deep `hashCode`). The recorded split mis-attributed the resolve because it sits INSIDE the `bindLibFiles` section, so `bindLibFiles` 17.4 -> 1.4 ms is that regex and not a bind. **Before inheriting any refusal here, re-derive its split and divide each part by its own population** — ~500 ns per `HashMap` put with a `String` value is 20-40x impossible and names the mechanism with no build. Both fixed halves are pure functions of the SHARED parses; `RealLibSnapshots` is their one home, and the walk is kept VERBATIM there because a `Set<Node>` dedupes STRUCTURALLY (a different container silently changes which declarations the sets hold).
- **AND THAT ROW IS `O(1)` IN PROGRAM SIZE, WHICH MAKES IT A *BIGGER* SHARE THE SMALLER THE PROJECT — THE OPPOSITE OF EVERY OTHER FLOOR ROW THIS ARC HAS PRICED (2026-08-30).** Anything per-CHECKER rather than per-FILE (lib parse/bind/merge, the ~494 field initializers of (INC.53)) is a CONSTANT an IDE pays on every keystroke, so a fixture's FILE COUNT hides it exactly as its `lib` setting does ((INC.61)). Its tell in a floor table is a row stable to ~1% across draws where every other row swings 40%.
- **A FLOOR/FRONT-END FIGURE IS A CLAIM ABOUT THE FIXTURE'S `compilerOptions` AS WELL AS ITS FILE SHAPE — THE GENERATED many-small FIXTURE PINS `"lib": ["es2020"]` (~185 GLOBALS) WHERE AN UNSET `lib` MEANS `dom` (~2,242), AND CHANGING THAT ONE LINE TOOK THE FLOOR'S LARGEST PASS FROM **13.5 ms TO 175.6 ms** ON THE SAME 2,401 FILES (2026-08-30, (INC.61)).** So the whole (INC.\*) arc had been measuring the cheap case; `build/bench/many-small-2400-dom` is the shape to use, and (INC.57)'s "ask whether the profile can express the cost at all" applies to the OPTIONS, not only to the file count. Anything O(files x globals) is invisible on the other fixture.
- **AND THE `Vfs`/SCOPE COROLLARY: A PER-FILE TABLE SEEDED FROM A PROGRAM-WIDE ONE IS `files x globals` AND MUST BE AN OVERLAY, NOT A COPY — `LayeredSymbolTable` IS THE ONE HOME (2026-08-30).** Its iteration ORDER is load-bearing and non-obvious: a `LinkedHashMap` keeps a key's ORIGINAL position when a later `put` overwrites the value, so a shadowing local appears at the BASE's position carrying the OWN value and is NEVER appended — three consumers iterate a per-file scope, and round 776 says an order that stops being a function of the program turns a cost counter into a property of the box. **The corpus cannot see that mistake** (order reaches only cost counters and spelling-suggestion order), so pin such a view against the reference CONSTRUCTION, never a hand-written expectation.
- **A tsconfig's DEFAULT `exclude` IS `[outDir, declarationDir]`, NOT THE PACKAGE FOLDERS — tsc PRUNES `node_modules`/`bower_components`/`jspm_packages` FROM EVERY WILDCARD MATCH SEPARATELY (`commonPackageFolders`, which our `walk`'s own `pruned` set mirrors), SO THE ONLY `exclude` DEFAULT THAT DOES ANY WORK IS THE ONE WE DID NOT HAVE (2026-08-30, (CFG.1), `commandLineParser.ts`).** Without it a project that has ever run a declaration-emitting build reads its own `dist` back in as ROOT FILES — measured against tsgo 7.0.2, its program is 1 file where ours was 2 — and re-checks the emitted tree on every keystroke. An EXPLICIT `exclude` REPLACES it; it is not additive.
- **AND THE REASON THAT SURVIVED IS A THIRD AXIS OF (INC.57)'s LAW: THE CORPUS HARNESS MATERIALISES **NO DIRECTORY AT ALL** (so it has no glob to get wrong) AND ALL EIGHT DASHBOARD PROFILES SCOPE `include` TO A `src` SUBTREE (so `dist` never matched) — a green grid on anything touching ROOT-FILE SELECTION is a CONTROL, never coverage.** The instrument for that whole class is a `-project` fixture through `ProjectCompiler` + a `Vfs`, exactly as for (CHK.29)'s `package.json` scopes. **And do not expect a DIAGNOSTIC to notice: a wrongly-adopted output tree reports TS2451 x2 and TS5011 in tsc and NOTHING here ((CHK.74)/(CFG.2)), so the only observable is the program's own file COUNT** — a value pin written on those codes passes on a broken binary too.
- **ASK A `Vfs` FOR A DIRECTORY LISTING WITH `listEntries`, NEVER `list` + `isDirectory` PER ENTRY — `SystemFileSystem.metadataOrNull` ANSWERS THAT ONE BOOLEAN WITH **UP TO FIVE `stat` SYSCALLS** (`exists` + `isFile` + `isDirectory` + `isFile` + `length`, kotlinx-io 0.9.1), MEASURED AT **7.3-8.6 us PER ENTRY** AND 60-70% OF THE ROOT-FILE GLOB (2026-08-30, (INC.60)).** `Vfs.listEntries`'s DEFAULT body is exactly the pair it replaces, so a counting/wrapping `Vfs` written for a test **must override it** or the pin is vacuous against every binary — and `SystemVfs`'s override is a SEPARATE implementation (`expect fun systemListEntries`, JVM = `File.listFiles` + one `File.isDirectory`), whose divergence is silent: a wrong kind drops a file from the program or adopts a directory as a root, with no diagnostic. `listEntries` promises NO order — round 776's sort at the call site is what fixes program order.
- **AND THE INSTRUMENT THAT FOUND IT IS THE ONE TO REACH FOR IN THE FRONT END: A `--frontEnd` ROW DIVIDED BY ITS OWN POPULATION, WITH THE IMPLIED PER-OP COST REFUSED WHEN IT IS PHYSICALLY IMPOSSIBLE (2026-08-30).** The wall could not see this fix at all — uninstrumented floor medians read **216 ms before and 222 after** for a real 16 ms saving, i.e. inside (INC.52)'s +-40% single-draw band and with the WRONG SIGN. Build the split BEFORE the fix, and keep a POPULATION census beside the nanos: identical counts across the change (`50 dirs / 2451 entries / 2401 candidates / 2401 roots`) is the receipt that nothing was skipped to buy the time.
- **A LOOP-INVARIANT REBUILT INSIDE A PER-FILE LOOP IS A *HOIST*, NOT A CACHE, AND MUST NOT BE ARGUED ABOUT AS ONE — there is no invalidation story when the source is a `val` on a data class nobody reassigns (2026-08-30, (INC.57)).** Two rules that kept the hoist free: keep `.toSet()` VERBATIM rather than "improving" it to a `HashSet`, so the container and any iteration order stay bit-for-bit what the per-call expression produced and CLAUDE.md's "PROVE order is unused" rule is never engaged; and **pin it with a COUNT AT TWO PROGRAM SIZES**, because the claim is COMPLEXITY and no wall assertion can state one (`EagerIndexCensus.programNameSetBuilds == 1` at 10 files and at 100 — the ablated binary reads `2 x files`). (INC.55)'s "a pin comparing two different programs cannot attribute" does NOT apply to an absolute quantity that is 1 by construction; it bit a DIFFERENCE that a varying poll count swamped. **AND THE VALUE HALF NEEDS ONE PIN PER RESOLUTION PATH: (INC.58)'s first value pin went RED on a WORKING binary** because the diagnostic it asserted fired through an O(1) direct probe rather than the scan being narrowed — **an assertion about WHICH path served an answer is not implied by the answer being right.**
- **A SINGLE-DRAW `--passTiming` ROW IS NOT A MEASUREMENT AT FLOOR SCALE: THE SAME BINARY READS **13.16 ms AND 8.42 ms** FOR ONE PASS IN TWO DRAWS OF ONE RUN (2026-08-29, (INC.52)).** The incremental FLOOR is ~68 ms with draws spanning **57-86 ms**, so its per-pass rows swing ~40% and a 6 ms lever is invisible to BOTH the row and the wall. Price a floor candidate by POPULATION (a count the verify arm can produce from the same binary — round 790's control shape), or amplify it (round 759); never by the row that made it look big. Floor composition for whoever prices the next one, shares not milliseconds: config+glob 13%, crawl 16% (80% of it read+decode), bind 7%, **checker construct+getDiagnostics 67%**, post-checker 3%.
- **…AND THAT 67% IS **NOT** THE PASS TABLE: `getDiagnostics()` IS **2-3 us** ON A FLOOR BUILD AND THE WHOLE PHASE IS THE `Checker` CONSTRUCTOR, OF WHICH ~20 ms IS ITS ~494 **PROPERTY INITIALIZERS** — A CONSTANT, IDENTICAL ON A 63 ms FLOOR AND A 5.2 s FULL BUILD (2026-08-29, (INC.53)).** So it is 0.4% of a full compile — invisible for ~950 rounds — and **~30% of what an editor pays per keystroke**. **A FIELD INITIALIZER IS NOT A `pass("…")`, SO IT CONTRIBUTES TO NO `--passTiming` ROW, NO `cost_gate.py` COUNTER AND NO DIAGNOSTIC**: the whole pass-gating arc ((INC.7)/(INC.20)/(INC.21), 189 walkers) swept loop headers and could not have seen it. Four initializers were essentially all of it and the other ~490 are 0.2-1.2 ms between them, so **divide before believing an aggregate here** — 494 allocations cannot be 20 ms, which is what said a handful were doing whole-program work. Three are now built on FIRST ASK; the instrument that decomposes the rest is `FrontEnd.CHK_CTOR`/`CHK_INIT` plus a row per field (`scripts/floor-decomposition.sh`). **Before pricing anything else in the floor, ask what runs OUTSIDE a pass.**
- **A COOPERATIVE CANCELLATION MUST BE AN `Error`, NOT A `RuntimeException` — THE CHECKER, THE CRAWL AND THE `Vfs` CARRY DEFENSIVE `catch (Exception)` GUARDS THAT WOULD SWALLOW IT AND LET THE BUILD CARRY ON WITH A MISSING FILE (2026-08-30, (INC.55)).** Silently wrong is worse than not cancelling. `Error` is safe for a REASON that must be preserved: the 2026-07-04 sweep left NO `catch (Throwable)` and NO `catch (Error)` in `commonMain`, and `Checker`'s one boundary guard catches `StackOverflowError` alone — so **adding a `catch (Throwable)` anywhere on the build path silently re-opens this**, which is why `ProjectCancellationTest` pins the TYPE rather than trusting the KDoc. `runWithDeepStack` transfers it faithfully because it uses `runCatching`, which captures `Throwable`.
- **THE `pass("…")` POLL COUNT IS NOT CONSTANT ACROSS PROGRAMS, SO A PIN THAT COMPARES TWO *DIFFERENT* PROGRAMS CANNOT ATTRIBUTE A PER-NODE EFFECT — 405 against 418 ON TWO FIXTURES OF THE SAME PROJECT SHAPE, WHICH SWAMPED THE ~12 THE SPINE CONTRIBUTED (2026-08-30, (INC.55)).** The pin read as a dead mechanism when the mechanism was fine. Hold the FILE COUNT and the shapes fixed and vary only SIZE — the same one-file program with 400 and 4,000 functions reads ~417 against ~526 — so the per-node site is the only variable. General form of round 902's dead-arm law for any "does the fine-grained hook fire" question.
- **A DATA-CLASS-KEYED NODE COLLECTION IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY THE COST, AND (INC.53) MEASURED THE ROUND-471 HYPOTHESIS **WRONG** ON ITS MOST TEMPTING INSTANCE (2026-08-29).** `builtinLibDecls`/`builtinLibMemberDecls`/`realLibDeclFile` are `Set<Node>`/`Map<Node,·>` filled from every lib statement AND every interface/class member — exactly the shape CLAUDE.md flags as safe only "because lib decl subtrees are small" — and the walk that fills them is **1.9-2.8 ms** of an 8-11 ms `parseBuiltinLib`, against 3.2-5.3 for the binds and 3.1-5.3 for the lib-set resolution plus 45 `mergeSymbolTable` calls. Split the row before acting on the mechanism you expect to find.
- **A CROSS-PROCESS CACHE IS VALIDATED BY *CONTENT*, AND ITS `.json` INPUTS ARE PART OF THE CONTENT — A `tsconfig`'s `extends` TARGET OR A `package.json`'s `type` DECIDES WHAT THE PROGRAM *IS*, SO A CHANGE THERE INVALIDATES EVERY STORED ROW RATHER THAN ONE FILE'S (2026-08-29, (INC.48)).** Which `.json` files a build depends on is NOT a function of the project path, so they must be RECORDED as the build reads them (`OverlayVfs.jsonReads`, mirroring `TsBuildInfo`'s `RecordingVfs`) — a snapshot that hashed only `tsconfig.json` is validated against a fraction of what decided its answer. Two more that are not optional: the compiler BUILD ID (and never a `.dirty`/`unknown` one — two dirty dev trees share an id without sharing behaviour), and the rule that **a content hash cannot see an ADDED file**, so a restored state must stay untrusted until one build has re-crawled and found the same program. The last is the only one an ablation catches: the naive "trust the snapshot when nothing changed" version passes every other pin.
- **A MODE EVERY PIN INSTALLS IS A DEFAULT PINNED BY NOTHING — AND A PIN THAT IS VACUOUS IN A DEV TREE BUT LIVE IN CI IS WORSE THAN NO PIN (2026-08-29, (INC.48)).** `XTSC_BUILD_ID` ends in `.dirty` for every local build, so anything gated on build-id reusability silently does nothing locally and exercises the real path only in CI — i.e. the pin passes for opposite reasons in the two places. The fix is a NAMED test seam installed and restored per test (`ProjectStateSnapshot.allowUnstableBuildIdForTesting`) plus one pin asserting the shipped DEFAULT, which is (INC.16)'s law: without that pin an ablation restoring the old policy reads 0 RED.
- **AN ESCAPE IS A LABEL ON A FILE THAT IS *CHANGING A LOT*, NOT A CAUSE OF ANYTHING — REMOVING ONE BUYS NOTHING, MEASURED ON THREE CORPORA (2026-08-29, (INC.47)/(INC.51)).** Removing `types.ts`'s escape left tsc's incremental stability rate at **67% with all 40 per-case verdicts identical**; removing `marked.ts`'s left that library's at **72%**. Both times the file that could not be summarised was also one whose exported surface genuinely moved, which is why an escape LOOKS causal in a per-case table. Before pricing "make this file summarisable", check whether the cases it appears in have another mover — the count that answers it is "cases whose EVERY moved file is an escape", never "cases that touched one".
- **A NAME IN AN `export { }` CLAUSE IS THE *EXPORTED* NAME AND `locals` IS KEYED BY THE *DECLARED* ONE, SO `export { impl as api }` MISSES EVERY LOOKUP WRITTEN THE OBVIOUS WAY (2026-08-29, (INC.51)).** `ExportSpecifier.propertyName` is the local and `.name` is what an importer sees; they differ exactly for `x as y`. The (INC.46) fingerprint conflated them, read the miss as "an exported name with no file-level symbol" and escaped the WHOLE file — every edit to it rebuilding the whole program, and the export's type never hashed. **tsc's own 78 sources never use the shape**, so the eight dashboard profiles, the corpus baselines and every cost counter are structurally blind to it; a 13-file real library had it on line 164. Same two-spellings-one-symbol trap as (INC.44)'s `import { p as q }`, in the other direction.
- **THE THREE RATES IN THE ENTRY BELOW WERE TAKEN AT `target: ES5` (`scripts/inc50-stability-lib.sh:105`) AND (P18.113) CHANGED WHAT THAT FIXTURE'S DEFAULT LIB CONTAINS — it now reaches es2015, as tsgo's does — so RE-MEASURE before quoting cronstrue 50% / tsc 67% / marked 72% again (2026-09-16).** The script was deliberately NOT edited: it is a committed measurement instrument, and bumping its target silently would have made the recorded figures unattributable.
- **THE INCREMENTAL STABILITY RATE IS A PROPERTY OF WHAT A CODEBASE'S COMMITS *TOUCH*, NOT OF HOW LAYERED IT IS — cronstrue **50%**, tsc **67%**, marked **72%** (2026-08-29, (INC.50), 40 real commits each).** The standing hypothesis was that tsc's barrels depressed the rate and layered code would do better; the two libraries BRACKET tsc instead, and the higher one is biased TOWARD stability because 18 ours-only diagnostics degrade some of its types to `any`. **A library this checker reports errors on is the wrong control for any measurement that reads a type**: a degraded type is artificially stable. `cronstrue` is the one library measured to agree with tsgo 7.0.2 exactly, which is why it is the control arm in `scripts/inc50-stability-lib.sh`.
- **AN ATTRIBUTION AN INSTRUMENT *PRINTS* IS A CLAIM ITS CODE MAY NOT MAKE — RE-DERIVE IT FROM THE INSTRUMENT'S OWN DETAIL BEFORE IT BECOMES A THRESHOLD (2026-08-29, (INC.47)).** `Inc46StabilityMain` printed `"N were moved only because a touched file ESCAPES"` over `if (escaped) movedBecauseEscaped.add(...)`, which counts every case that TOUCHED an escaping file whether or not it moved on its own — and its own detail lines showed four other movers in the same case, in the same run. That one line became (INC.46)(2)'s **87.5% ceiling**, then the queue's named successor, then a round of work; re-derived, exactly ONE of the 8 cases qualified (ceiling **70%**), and removing every escape moved the measured rate by **nothing** (67% on both arms, all 40 per-case verdicts identical). A summary line is a place a hypothesis gets laundered into a measurement: the detail is the measurement.
- **(INC.46)'s EXPORT FINGERPRINT IS **NON-TRANSITIVE BY CONSTRUCTION**, SO IT CAN NEVER BE A PER-HOP STOPPING SIGNAL — IT IS ONLY EVER A WHOLE-PROGRAM GATE, AND ITS OWN KDoc SAYS SO (`Checker.kt:57050`, 2026-09-01, (INC.91) censused and refused).** (INC.47)'s file-boundary cut is what makes the hash converge, and what it gives up is exactly that F's hash does not move when G's type changes; `incrementalDiagnostics` is sound BECAUSE a moved signature anywhere falls back to a whole-program build. **A walk that keeps the signal and deletes the fallback DROPS DIAGNOSTICS**: measured on a length-preserving three-file edit (`count: number` -> `count: string`, 106 bytes both ways) the only error is at HOP 2 while hop 1 is silent in every channel — fingerprint unmoved, 0 rows, `.d.ts` text unchanged — so the walk stops one hop early and reports **0 where the truth is 1**. Two corollaries: the `foreignKey` mixes `decl.pos`/`decl.end` so an importer's hash carries the offsets of what it imports, but that costs exactly ONE extra hop and does NOT cascade (an unedited importer's own declarations do not move); and the sound way to narrow a signature edit is the transitive importer CLOSURE from `Result.importEdges`, which uses no fingerprint and is sound for the reason a superset always is.
- **A STRUCTURAL HASH OVER A PROGRAM'S TYPE GRAPH MUST BE A CANONICAL *SERIALIZATION*, NEVER A RECURSION WITH A DEPTH CAP — THE CAP IS A **MISSED INVALIDATION**, WHICH IS THE ONE DIRECTION THAT COSTS A STALE ANSWER (2026-08-29, (INC.47)).** A path-recursive walk is exponential in DAG width (159 s on one build), memoizing CLOSED subtrees does not save it inside a strongly-connected component (`types.ts`: a budget stop at 2 M nodes AND at 12 M), and the depth cap that bounds it hashes everything below as one constant — so a moved type deeper than the cap reads as no change. Discovering each reachable type ONCE and naming it by its discovery INDEX fixes all three at once: linear in nodes plus edges, cycles need no special case, no SCC to canonicalise, and nothing to truncate (**122.52 ms for ONE export -> 6.21 ms for 871**). An index is id-free **only while the traversal is a function of the text** — exports and member tables iterated by SORTED name, positional lists in syntactic order — so a new child enumerated in map order silently makes the hash a function of resolution order (INV.6(6c0), (INC.19)).
- **A PIN ON THE VALUE ONE LAYER PRODUCES CANNOT SEE WHAT THE NEXT LAYER DOES WITH IT (round 872).** `ExitCodeParityTest` pinned the exit code the daemon puts IN its response — always correct — while `runAsClient` propagated only `XTSC_REFUSED` and dropped the rest, so a daemon-served FAILING compile exited **0** where the CLI and the in-process fallback exit 1, and CI branching on it read a failure as a pass. Silent by construction: the diagnostics print either way and nothing prints the code. When a value crosses a boundary, pin it where it is CONSUMED.
- **A CONNECT-AND-CLOSE IS THE CLIENT'S REACHABILITY PROBE, NOT A FAILED REQUEST — the daemon must recognise a frameless connection and stay silent (round MOD.5).** `isDaemonReachable` is polled every 25-500 ms while a client waits for a daemon it just spawned, and logging each one fills the daemon's log with alarming lines describing its own health check.
- **A DAEMON REQUEST CARRIES THE CLIENT'S *WORKING DIRECTORY* AND THE COMPILER RESOLVES AGAINST IT — DO NOT RE-ADD PATH REWRITING TO EITHER CLIENT (round 873, protocol version 2).** A JVM cannot change its own cwd, so `CompileServer` installs `CompileRequest.workingDirectory` into `SystemVfs.workingDirectory` for the duration of one request (install-and-restore on the one compile thread, exactly like the round-848 ledger); that single variable is the funnel, because `ProjectCompiler.build` absolutizes both the project path and a `--outDir` override through `Vfs.resolveAbsolute` before doing anything with either. **The rule both clients used to carry — "rewrite an argument that names something existing here" — is UNFIXABLE at that layer and its two holes were CI-grade**: a client does not parse the compiler's options, so it cannot tell a project path from the `4` in `--workers 4`, and it never sees the path a user did not type — `CliArgs.project` defaults to `"."`, so `xtsc --daemon --noEmit` in a failing project compiled the DAEMON's directory and exited **0**, while `--outDir out` (not yet existing, so "not a path") emitted the user's JavaScript into it. The version bump is load-bearing: a v1 daemon answers a v2 request against its own cwd, and `protocolProblem` is what refuses it — **but only since 2026-08-09; before that its answer was printed and discarded, see the next entry.** Sweep with `scripts/serve_parity.py`; contract in `docs/serve-parity.md`.
- **CONSULTING A GUARD IS NOT ENFORCING IT — `CompileServer` called `protocolProblem` on every request, PRINTED the answer, and then compiled anyway (2026-08-09).** So a v1 request was in fact served against the DAEMON's cwd (the round-873 hazard the version bump exists to stop), and since a mismatched client's documented answer is to fall back and compile in-process, every `xtsc --daemon` through a stale native client cost **TWO full compiles** — 7 mismatches to 14 served requests, 14.6 s against an honest 7.5 s. `respondTo` now answers `XTSC_REFUSED` with `elapsedMs = 0`. **Why it survived: every server pin built its `CompileRequest` POSITIONALLY, i.e. at the UNVERSIONED default, so the suite pinned the opposite behaviour** — daemon tests now go through `clientRequest(...)`, and a mismatch message printed by BOTH peers must be peer-neutral (it said "the daemon speaks protocol N, this client speaks M", which inverts every noun in a daemon log).
- **GraalVM PGO IS THE BIGGEST SINGLE LEVER MEASURED IN THIS ARC — -21.2% CHECK-ONLY AND -19.1% EMIT, 5/5 PAIRED IN BOTH MODES, 46 DIAGNOSTICS AND ALL 78 EMITTED `.js` BYTE-IDENTICAL (2026-08-10, `docs/perf/aot-native-image.md` § 10).** It needs **Oracle GraalVM** (`-graal` in SDKMAN); Community Edition (`-graalce`) has no PGO and its `native-image --help` does not mention the word. On this box, one session: CE 25.2.4 11,481 ms -> Oracle 25.0.4 10,222 -> **Oracle+PGO 8,057**, i.e. **~1.93x FASTER than tsc 6.0.3** where CI's CE arm is at 1.02x parity. **Train the `.iprof` on BOTH modes** - a check-only-only profile leaves the Transformer/Emitter on static heuristics, which is round 840(c)'s -932 ms trap one artifact over. The CE-vs-Oracle row CONFOUNDS distribution with Graal version (25.2 innovation vs 25.0 LTS, same JDK base 25.0.4, and Oracle ships no innovation build) - do not read it as a statement about either alone.
- **`javaTarget`'s CEILING IS GRAALVM, NOT KOTLIN — GraalVM ships NO JDK 26 build in EITHER distribution (Oracle's `graalvm-jdk-26` 404s; CE's newest is `graal-25.2.4` / JDK 25.0.4), and `native-image` cannot read a class file its own JVM rejects (2026-08-10).** Kotlin 2.4.10 reaches `JVM_26`, so the bump compiles and then silently costs the `nativeImage` task and both CI native workflows — i.e. the fastest artifact shipped. 25 is the pin; raise it only together with a GraalVM that has 26.
- **AZUL ZULU IS *NOT* FASTER THAN TEMURIN, AND THE DEV BOX'S DEFAULT `java` IS ZULU — SO A *COLD* NUMBER TAKEN LOCALLY RUNS ~13% PESSIMISTIC AGAINST CI (2026-08-10, `docs/perf/jdk-jit-vendor-comparison.md` § 1).** Same upstream build both sides (26.0.2+10, so vendor is the only variable): Temurin wins 3 of 4 cells, cold check-only by **+13.1%** (0/2); the one Zulu-favouring cell is mixed 2/3 with a range crossing zero. Warm numbers are close enough to compare across the two; cold ones are not.
- **THE GRAAL JIT IS A ~5-10% *WARM* LEVER AND AN 8-16% *COLD* PENALTY, WHICH IS EXACTLY WHAT `bench.yml`'s `JAVA_HOME`-ORDER COMMENT IS GUARDING (2026-08-10, § 2 of the same doc).** Against Temurin: Oracle GraalVM's JVM is **-5.4% check-only / -10.2% emit warm, 3/3 in every cell**, and **+8.2% / +4.6% cold**. Confounded with JDK 25-vs-26 (GraalVM has nothing on 26) in the direction that UNDERSTATES Graal. Free ~5-10% for the DAEMON; keep the bench on Temurin as the conservative reference.
- **THE BENCH'S PRIMARY xtsc COLUMN IS A *WARM* REBUILD SINCE 2026-08-10 AND THE SERIES RESTARTED AT `BENCH-ROWS-V2` — never compare a V2 row's xtsc cell to a V1 one (cold vs warm is ~4x on the same commit, so the boundary alone looks like a 4x win).** **Since the same date it publishes TWO warm arms — `temurin26` (primary) and `graal-oracle25` — on identical bytecode in one run, so the series never has to pick a JVM**; the second arm's delta IS the within-round Graal-vs-HotSpot number, and a GraalVM-only primary would have frozen the series on JDK 25. The AOT column re-baselined on the same date, CE -> Oracle BASE — **not PGO, which lives in `native.yml` (the shipped artifact) because bench.yml runs per push**, so that column reads ~14%/~20% slow against what ships. What this immediately showed: **warm xtsc is 2.05x FASTER than tsc check-only and 2.20x on emit** — tsc parity is behind us in the warm regime and the open gap is tsgo (3.09x / 2.71x); the cold arm's "2.00x SLOWER than tsc" is a JVM-startup story, not a compiler one.
- **A CRaC CHECKPOINT OF A WARMED JVM RESTORES IN ~30 ms AND ITS FIRST COMPILE RUNS AT FULL WARM SPEED — 6.8-7.3 s AGAINST 24-25 s COLD, SAME BINARY, SAME SESSION (3.4x, against the AOT cache's 1.64x), OUTPUT BYTE-IDENTICAL BAR THE `time:` LINE (2026-08-10, `docs/perf/crac-checkpoint.md`).** Three features get called "AOT/snapshot" and only one stores COMPILED CODE: the shipped JDK 25 AOT cache is JEP 483+515 (classes + method PROFILES, which is why it is worth nothing warm), Leyden's AOT code caching is NOT in this JDK (`-XX:+AOTCodeCaching` unrecognized), and CRaC needs a CRaC build (Zulu 25 CRaC; the default `warp` engine checkpointed with **no root and CapEff 0**, where the CRIU engine would have needed capabilities). **It is not shippable: the restored process keeps the CHECKPOINT's working directory** (measured - checkpoint in the repo, restore from `/tmp`, `user.dir` unchanged), i.e. round 873's defect one layer down, so a CRaC CLI must re-install the real cwd through `SystemVfs.workingDirectory` in an `afterRestore` hook. The 340 MB image was page-cache-hot in every restore here and a cold-disk one is UNMEASURED (dropping caches needs root). Suite on that JDK: 14,234 / 0.
- **A WARM A/B's DEFAULT WARM-UP WAS *2* AND THAT ALONE SPENT MORE THAN THE WHOLE +-1.0% BAND — MEASURED A/A, TWO IDENTICAL ARMS SIT **3.3%** APART AT `WARMUP=2`, 2.0% AT 3, **0.8%** AT 6 AND 0.9% AT 8 (2026-08-10; defaults now 6 in `ab-warm.sh` AND `BenchMain`).** The inflated median is NOT the problem - the ramp is common-mode and cancels in a paired delta - but it lands on the one quantity the verdict gates on, the spread BETWEEN process medians. Method worth reusing: run ONE ladder of 16 measured iterations per process (`BenchMain <proj> 0 16`) and re-slice it offline for every candidate setting, which makes the arms identical by construction and costs two runs instead of ten. **And no setting stabilises an ABSOLUTE number: the ladder still drifts -3.4% between iterations 9-12 and 13-16, sign-consistent in both runs**, so warm-up is not over at 16 and only within-round paired deltas are quotable.
- **THE WARM-UP LADDER IS HotSpot AND ESSENTIALLY NOTHING ELSE — 80,056 ms of JIT CPU during request 1, 28,995 during 2, 9,927 during 3, against a plateau of ~2-3 s (2026-08-09, `jcmd PerfCounter.print` between requests).** The ladder 25.1 / 11.2 / 7.6 / 7.3 / 7.2 / 7.0 s is bought by C1+C2 compiling **5,069 code blobs in the first request alone**; GC is flat at ~120 ms/request and the one cross-request cache that is NOT the JIT — `CrawlParseCache` — is worth **270 ms** at plateau (`--parseCacheOff`, blocked arms). Two consequences: **the plateau is reached at request 6-8, not 4** (request 4 is still ~5% high), and JIT never actually stops (127 fresh blobs at request 10), it just moves off the critical path onto the other cores.
- **BEFORE BANKING A GREEN 8-PROFILE GRID, COUNT WHETHER THE CHANGED PATH *FIRES* THERE — AND THE COUNT IS OFTEN LITERALLY ZERO, ON THE LIBRARIES TOO (2026-09-11, (CHK.124)).** Admitting a new position to the expando walker read `added=0 removed=0` on all eight profiles, and a positive control counting admissions read **0 on all eight AND 0 on cronstrue, marked and the 600-file generated project** — a file-level read of a top-level function's own member is a shape none of those corpora contains, so the grid was a statement about the corpora and not about the change. **The CORPUS was the gate and it caught a real defect on the first run** (`genericCallWithoutArgs` is `f<number,string>.`, whose dangling dot the parser gives a zero-width `Identifier("")` — the new position grew a `Property '' does not exist…` row beside pristine's TS1003). Two rounds in one day landed on opposite sides of this: (CHK.121)'s grid counted 78/152/116 accepts and was a real gate; this one counted 0. **One counter, one build, and the difference between a gate and a control is settled** — CLAUDE.md's (INC.73) "a green corpus under an ablation is not 'this is dead'" with the instruments swapped.
- **A tsgo BASELINE'S *LAYER* IS THE DIRECTORY ITS `.diff` LIVES IN, NEVER THE BASE BASELINE UNDER `submodule/` — EVERY CASE HAS ONE OF THOSE, SO `find … submodule/` ALWAYS 'PROVES' THE ROW IS ADOPTED (2026-09-16, (P18.116): both TS2751 rows read as targetable that way and are in fact `submoduleTriaged`, i.e. tsgo's own KNOWN BUGS — `testdata/submoduleTriaged.txt` says "known diffs that we intend to fix").** Check the `.diff`'s directory and the three `submodule*.txt` manifests before spending a round on a row. Same round: **`corpus-screen.sh --include` is a SUBSTRING, not a regex**, so an alternation silently compares the UNCHANGED population and reads as a clean pass — the tell is the subtest COUNT; and `rm -rf build/test-results/jvmTest` at the REPO ROOT deletes nothing (the XMLs are per-module), which once summed 1,574 stale tests and reported 0 failures for a run that had just failed one.
- **THE THREE-COMPILER ADJUDICATION IS `scripts/ref_matrix.py` — USE IT INSTEAD OF REBUILDING ONE IN A SCRATCHPAD, AND KNOW THE TWO WAYS IT USED TO LIE, BECAUSE BOTH PRODUCED *PLAUSIBLE* TABLES (2026-09-11, (P18.70)).** It runs a fixture through ours + tsgo 7.0.2 + pristine 6.0.3 and prints AGREE / OURS-ONLY / MISSING / **TEXT-DIFF** (right row, wrong message) / **SPAN-DIFF** / REF-SPLIT. **The three compilers do NOT share a row format** — the references print `t.ts(12,5): error TS2339:` and ours `/abs/t.ts:12:5 - error TS2339:` — so a parser matching only one reads the other's rows as ZERO and reports "missing everywhere", which looks like a finding; `assert_parsed` now REFUSES an arm whose output contains `error TS` while nothing parsed. **And a key of `(file, line, code)` COLLAPSES two diagnostics of one code on one line**: a fixture emitting TS2339 twice on line 3 read `agree=1`, losing the second row and a message divergence on the first. **A REF-SPLIT row is evidence about NOTHING** (the two references genuinely diverge in whole families, round 938) and must never enter a prize. **TEXT-DIFF is the only cheap instrument here that can see a DISPLAY defect at all** — (PARITY.1) says the 8-profile grid is structurally blind to every one of them.
- **A PRISTINE FIXTURE HAS TWO HALVES THAT LIVE IN DIFFERENT FILES, AND TAKING BOTH FROM ONE OF THEM IS A SILENT WHOLE-FILE LINE SHIFT: the `.js`/`.errors.txt` BASELINE echoes the source verbatim but with the `// @key: value` HARNESS DIRECTIVES REMOVED (tsc strips them before compiling), while `tests/cases` keeps them (round 941).** So an offline reconstruction must take the SOURCE from a baseline and the DIRECTIVES from the case — a source taken from the case is the baseline's line numbering plus the directive count, and every `(line, code)` row then reads as a divergence in BOTH directions, which is indistinguishable from a real finding (30.5% of round 940's sweep). `tests/cases` is also incomplete in this clone, so neither half is always present; a missing case leaves the baseline's `(target=es2015)` VARIATION SUFFIX as the only surviving record of the options. **The guard is an ALIGNMENT ORACLE** — compare the reconstruction line-for-line against the baseline's own `==== file ====` body — and `scripts/pristine_sweep.py` records `aligned`/`misaligned`/`unknown` per fixture for exactly this reason. Directives are then passed into the scratch tsconfig VERBATIM, because `TsConfigLoader` routes every `compilerOptions` key through the same `applyDirective` the corpus harness uses; re-deriving that mapping in Python is what loses `@jsx` and `@experimentalDecorators`.
- **A BASELINE'S SQUIGGLE LINES ARE INDENTED WITH THE *SOURCE'S* WHITESPACE, SO A TAB-INDENTED FIXTURE'S `~~~~` LINES SURVIVED `pristine_oracle.py --extract`'s `strip("~ ")` AND WERE WRITTEN INTO THE RECONSTRUCTED SOURCE — four extra lines, every row misaligned (2026-09-02, (CHK.75); fixed to `strip("~ \t")`).** Round 941's alignment oracle is the check that catches the next costume: compare the reconstruction line-for-line before trusting a divergence.
- **AN ABSENT DIRECTIVE IS EVIDENCE ONLY WHERE THE *CASE FILE* IS PRESENT — A MISSING ONE SAYS NOTHING, AND `pristine_sweep.py --tsc-strict-default` DELETED FOUR GENUINE FALSE POSITIVES BEFORE IT WAS GUARDED ON THAT (round 943).** That arm injects tsc's own `strict: false` default wherever a fixture names no strict-family directive, to size how much of a sweep is the (CHK.13) convention rather than a modelling gap; `strictPropertyInitialization` has NO case file in this clone and **20 TS2564 in its own baseline**, i.e. pristine plainly had the flag ON. Round 941's defect (c) one directive over. **Second control, needed whenever the case file is absent: does pristine's own baseline carry that CODE anywhere in the fixture** — zero over seventeen uninitialised class fields is conclusive, seven is a refusal. `docs/pristine-divergences.md` § 0b.
- **A PROJECT PATH THAT DOES NOT EXIST USED TO CRAWL THE WHOLE FILESYSTEM, AND UNDER `--serve` THAT WEDGES THE DAEMON PERMANENTLY (round 873).** `resolveConfigPath` takes `dirname` of a config nobody confirmed — for `xtsc /nonexistent` that is **`/`** — and an unreadable config used to fall back to the default everything-glob: >30 minutes of CPU, after emitting TS5083 and going walking anyway. **The general trap is the server one, and it is unchanged: `CompileServer` serves requests SEQUENTIALLY on one thread by design and neither peer has a timeout, so ANY request that does not return holds the daemon for good and every later client blocks with no diagnosis.** A config that does not EXIST now includes nothing; one that exists and does not PARSE keeps the default (its dir is real). When a parity/latency harness reports "the daemon stopped serving", look for a wedged compile before suspecting the transport.
- **A PARITY/ABLATION DRIVER NEEDS A *REACHED-THE-THING-UNDER-TEST* CONTROL, AND FOR THE DAEMON THAT IS THE SERVER'S OWN `request N served` COUNT (round 873).** `--daemon` falls back to compiling IN-PROCESS when no server answers, so a dead daemon makes both arms run the same code and agree — a perfect green sweep that tested nothing, the same class as round 853's frozen classpath. The driver requires the count to advance by exactly one per step AND the `no compile server on` message to be absent. Related and cheap: **a "this cell is allowed to differ" marker is an escape hatch that WILL absorb an unrelated defect** — round 873's ablation caught one swallowing an exit-code divergence into a documented cwd excuse.
- **A CLI FLAG THAT WRITES A PROCESS-GLOBAL MODE MUST GO THROUGH `modes.set(Obj::field, v)` IN `parseCliArgs` — A BARE `Obj.field = v` THERE COMPILES, WORKS IN THE ONE-SHOT CLI, AND SILENTLY RECONFIGURES EVERY LATER REQUEST ON A `--serve` DAEMON (round 848).** `runCli` restores the ledger in a `finally`, to the value the field HELD (`ArgNarrowGate.mode` defaults to ON, not to its zero value); `CliModeRestoreTest` reflects over every declared field of the 16 mode objects and is what sees the omission. **And do NOT read a green corpus or a green profile as "that arm is equivalent": all 14 probe arms are byte-identical on the compiler profile INCLUDING `--flowScanBogus`, whose job is to corrupt the scanner, and defaulting `FlowScan.bogus` true leaves the 13,902-test suite with exactly ONE failure — its own dedicated equivalence pin.**
- **THE COMPILE SERVER MUST RUN EVERY REQUEST ON *THE SAME* THREAD, AND `Dispatchers.IO` SILENTLY BREAKS IT WITH BYTE-IDENTICAL OUTPUT (round MOD.4b).** Symbol/Type id sequences are thread-local and `runWithDeepStack` hands them off from the CALLER's counters (INV.6(6c0)), so a coroutine that lands on a different carrier thread hands off from counters still at zero, restarts ids at 1 and collides with the singleton intrinsics — invisible to the corpus, to a `--listAll` diff and to `cost_gate.py` alike. `CompileServer.onCompileThread` (a single-thread executor) is the guard and `CompileThreadInvariantTest` the pin; its load-bearing case is the CONCURRENT one, which a pooled dispatcher fails while passing every same-caller assertion. Never `launch { }` per connection.
- **THE LAUNCHER'S DEV CLASSPATH IS `…-daemon/build/install/lib` (Gradle's `xtscLib` Sync), NOT a hand-listed set of jars (round MOD.4b).** The old form pasted known jar names plus `build/bench/cp.txt` — the COMPILER's dependency tail — so the commit that gave the daemon its own dependencies (`-api`, ktor, coroutines) omitted them and died with `ClassNotFoundException` at RUN time with a green build. Add a module dependency freely; never re-hand-list the classpath.
- **A GRADLE `-I` INIT SCRIPT APPLIES TO *EVERY* BUILD IN THE INVOCATION — INCLUDING `build-logic` — AND AN UNQUALIFIED TASK NAME NOW RUNS IN ALL THREE PROJECTS (round MOD.3).** The classpath-dump init script used by `bench-compile-tsc.sh` / `cost_gate.py` / `ab-*.sh` registered its task under `allprojects`: post-split the root throws (no `jvmRuntimeClasspath`) and **`-api` prints a SECOND, WRONG `XTSC_CLASSPATH=` line that the readers' `head -1` may pick** — a silently wrong benchmark, not an error. It must be `rootProject { findProject(":xemantic-typescript-compiler-core")?.tasks?.register(…) }` (`findProject`, since `build-logic` has no such project and a hard `project(":…")` fails the run during plugin resolution), invoked QUALIFIED, with the reader asserting exactly one line. `ab-interleaved.sh`/`ab-warm.sh` re-use the generated file and refuse a pre-split one by grepping it for the module name.
- **…AND THE CACHED `build/bench/xtsc-classpath.txt` OUTLIVES THE THING IT DESCRIBES: A HARNESS THAT READS IT WITHOUT THAT GUARD RUNS AN OLD COMPILER AT EXIT 0 WITH A PLAUSIBLE ERROR COUNT AND NO TELL (round 852).** The file found in the tree was PRE-MODULE-SPLIT — its only class directory was the ROOT project's `build/classes/kotlin/jvm/main`, which the split left behind but which A/B harnesses still WRITE (so its mtime is recent and a staleness check by date proves nothing): 582 classes against the core module's 649, missing `ModeLedger`/`LibTypeCensus`/`CcetSections`, i.e. a pre-round-848 binary. `scripts/grid838.sh` had no guard and every dashboard capture it produced since the split is unverified. **Grep any cached classpath for `xemantic-typescript-compiler-core/build/classes` before believing a capture**, and treat the root `build/classes` as an A/B scratch area, never as the compiler.
- **…AND THE FRESHNESS GUARD THAT *DOES* EXIST IS USUALLY POINTED AT THE WRONG FILE: A CACHED CLASSPATH IS A CLAIM ABOUT A BUILD, AND THE INPUT THAT INVALIDATES IT IS `gradle/libs.versions.toml` — NOT THE MODULE'S `build.gradle.kts`, WHICH A VERSION BUMP LEAVES UNTOUCHED (round 858).** `ab-warm.sh` guarded its `cp-warm.txt` cache with exactly that wrong comparison, which is how `build/bench/cp.txt` came to name kotlin-stdlib 2.4.0 / kotlinx-io 0.9.0 / serialization 1.9.0 a month after the build moved to 2.4.10 / 0.9.1 / 1.11.0 — **every jar it named still existed and the compiler still linked against them, so there was no error, no crash and no wrong answer, only a measurement of something other than what ships.** A guard aimed at the wrong input is indistinguishable from no guard; the ablation shows it, since dropping `libs.versions.toml` from the watched set reddens only the libs-versions pin while the module-build-file pin stays GREEN. Everything now resolves through `scripts/lib/dep-classpath.sh` (non-empty + newer than EVERY build-definition input + every named entry exists), `build/bench/cp.txt` has ZERO readers and `DepClasspathGuardTest` keeps it that way. **Also from that round: the documented "pre-split refusal" greps the INIT SCRIPT, not the cache, and sits in the resolve branch — so a cache HIT skips it entirely. Check that a refusal FIRES, never that it exists.** `docs/perf/classpath-readers-audit.md`.
- **THE JAR FILE NAME IS A CONTRACT: core's `base { archivesName }` is pinned to `"xemantic-typescript-compiler"`, NOT `project.name` (round MOD.3).** `scripts/aot-draw-variance.sh` and `scripts/aot-corpus-suite.sh` glob `xemantic-typescript-compiler-jvm-*.jar`; letting the jar become `…-core-jvm-*.jar` makes those globs match nothing. `-core` is an internal module boundary the distribution never learns about. (The LAUNCHER no longer globs by name — post-MOD.4b it takes the whole staged lib dir, see the entry below.)
- **THE THREE SHIPPED ENTRY POINTS DELIBERATELY DISAGREE, AND "MAKING THEM AGREE" IS A REGRESSION IN WHICHEVER DIRECTION YOU DO IT (MOD.7, 2026-08-10).** `scripts/xtsc` + `scripts/xtsc-aot` run `…compiler.server.XtscMainKt` (they have a daemon to reach); the **GraalVM image is built by `:xemantic-typescript-compiler-cli`** — `./gradlew :xemantic-typescript-compiler-cli:nativeImage`, binary at that module's `build/native/xtsc` — from `…compiler.cli.MainKt`, which REFUSES `--serve`/`--daemon`/`--socket` with exit 2; and `…compiler.MainKt` may be neither, because the compiler's argument loop ends in `else -> if (!a.startsWith("-")) o.project = a`, so it adopts a socket path as the PROJECT and exits 0 (round 840, measured, 173 files emitted). Pointing the image back at the dispatcher re-adds ktor-network + slf4j + `-api` to a binary that can never open a socket (23 → 15 runtime modules); `AotCacheGuardTest` refuses a `nativeImage` in the daemon module and `NativeImageEntryPointTest` refuses the other two classes. **kotlinx-serialization is NOT removed by that split and never can be at the entry point** — the core parses tsconfig.json with it. PGO passthroughs on the task: `-PnativeImageArgs="…"` and `-PnativeImageOutput=name` (Oracle GraalVM only; CE has no PGO).
- **THE AOT LAUNCHER'S DEV CLASSPATH IS A *STAGED* DIR THAT ONLY `assemble` CREATES, SO A FRESH CHECKOUT OR A `clean` LEAVES `scripts/xtsc` UNABLE TO START AT ALL — and post-split nothing had run it, so the launcher was dead for the whole MOD window (round 857).** `xtsc_resolve_classpath`'s dev fallback is `…-daemon/build/install/lib` (the `xtscLib` Sync); with it absent the script dies `cannot resolve a classpath … or build this repo first`. Fail-safe, but it means **`./gradlew assemble` is a prerequisite of every AOT measurement**, and a harness that assembles its own classpath instead (the old `<core jar>:$(cat build/bench/cp.txt)` form) is no longer a PREFIX of the trained one — the JVM then declines the cache in `AOTMode=auto` and **both arms run uncached, agree, and prove nothing**. Any harness carrying an AOT arm must read that staged dir with the launcher's own `find -maxdepth 1 -name '*.jar' | LC_ALL=C sort` AND assert a non-zero `shared objects file` class-load count.
- **THE AOT FINGERPRINT RECORDS EACH CLASSPATH ENTRY'S *BASENAME*, NEVER ITS PATH (`${entry##*/}`), SO A BYTE-IDENTICAL COPY OF THE LIB DIR AT ANOTHER LOCATION VERIFIES `USE` AGAINST THE SAME CACHE (round 857).** The guard binds the ARTIFACT, not the install location — which is what lets a distribution be relocated without retraining, and what makes a mutate-a-copied-lib-dir experiment a valid arm rather than an automatic miss. Do not "strengthen" it to full paths: that would refuse every relocated install for no correctness gain.
- **`xemantic { applyAllConventions() }` IS SINGLE-MODULE-ONLY AND MUST NOT BE RESTORED — its `applyJarManifests()` breaks any subproject two ways (round MOD.2).** Its `allprojects` callback calls `archiveBaseName.get()` while a Jar is being CREATED (for a subproject that runs BEFORE the Kotlin plugin sets it — callbacks fire in registration order and the root always evaluates first, so a `base { archivesName = … }` in the module does NOT fix it), and it reads `project.xemantic`, an extension that exists only on the root. The replacement is root build.gradle.kts's `afterEvaluate` jar-manifest block; the pin that a jar keeps its five `Implementation-*` attributes + `META-INF/LICENSE` is a manual `unzip -p build/libs/*jvm*.jar META-INF/MANIFEST.MF`.
- **A `types: ["node"]` bench measurement on a FRESHLY created profile dir is GARBAGE until `node_modules/@types/node` is copied in (round 730).** Only `build/bench/tsc-project-*` (the compiler profile) carries the real `@types/node` from an earlier online session; `bench-compile-tsc.sh` `rm -rf`s it from every profile it materializes without `--node-stub`. The tell is TS2591 staying at 43 instead of falling to 13 (plus a TS2688) — it looks exactly like a regression. Copy the dir across before measuring.
- **THE 8-PROFILE GRID'S TWO ARMS MUST BE PRODUCED BY THE SAME COMMAND — a `--listAll` patched into `bench-compile-tsc.sh`'s `NOEMIT_ARGS` is OVERWRITTEN by the next line (`[[ $NO_EMIT -eq 1 ]] && NOEMIT_ARGS=(--noEmit)`), so that arm's capture is TRUNCATED to the first 30 diagnostics and the diff reports a regression that does not exist (round 811 read `0 added / 16 removed` on all eight profiles from a byte-identical compiler).** The tell is `... and N more error(s)` in the capture; run both arms as a direct `java … --noEmit --listAll <profileDir>` and have the differ REFUSE any capture containing that string, alongside round 804's non-empty check.
- **…AND EVERY COMMITTED "8-PROFILE GRID" HARNESS IN THIS REPO WAS IN FACT A *ONE*-PROFILE GRID UNTIL ROUND 895 — THE COMPILER PROFILE IS `build/bench/tsc-project-<commit8>` (a historical name) WHILE THE OTHER SEVEN ARE `build/bench/tsc-<name>-<commit8>`, SO THE `tsc-project-*` GLOB THEY ALL USED MATCHES EXACTLY ONE DIRECTORY.** It fails SILENTLY and in the reassuring direction: the loop runs, both arms capture, the differ prints `added=0 removed=0` and the round records a green 8-profile grid it never ran — which is exactly what makes profile-unique files (harness/server/services, round 782's own lesson one entry up) invisible. **Enumerate profiles by the presence of a `tsconfig.json` and REFUSE a run that finds fewer than 8** (`scripts/round895-grid.sh` is the reference); treat any pre-895 round's grid claim as covering the compiler profile only.
- **A CAPTURE md5 IS A PROPERTY OF (OUTPUT x RECIPE), SO IT IS ONLY COMPARABLE ACROSS ROUNDS THAT USED THE SAME RECIPE — *FOUR* LIVE LINEAGES EXIST FOR THE SAME COMPILER-PROFILE OUTPUT, AND ROUND 853 REPRODUCED THEM ALL FROM ONE VERIFIED CAPTURE (round 841 + round 853).** The fourth is round 848's `4090b73e…` (`grep 'error TS' | sed 's#.*/src/#src/#' | sort`), which read like a stale-binary artifact for a whole round and was not one. `59d930db…` = `grep 'error TS' | sort`, 46 lines, absolute paths (rounds 826, 836-840); `4caacf24…` = the whole `--listAll` stdout minus `[`/`time:` lines, sorted, 54 lines (rounds 828-835); and **`scripts/grid838.sh`, the committed harness a next round will reach for, additionally `sed`s the project prefix away and prints `84bbe7f0…`, which NO round note records** — so its first user will read a `sed` as a regression. Quote the recipe with the digest, prefer `added=0 removed=0` against a rebuilt before-arm for anything cross-round, and keep the md5 for the within-round many-runs check. `docs/perf/aot-cache.md` § 11.
