- **`scripts/bench-compile-tsc.sh` stat parsing uses GNU `grep -oP` — on macOS (BSD grep) every parsed stat (self_ms, errors, files, emitted) silently logs as 0 in the bench TSV; `wall_ms` is real.** A TSV row with self_ms=0/errors=0 is a macOS parse artifact, not a 0-error run — recover the numbers from the run log it points at (or install GNU grep).
- **AN ABLATION IS EVIDENCE ONLY IF YOU COUNT HOW OFTEN THE DISABLED CODE ACTUALLY RAN (round 753 proved both directions in one session).** "Cut it out and the profile stayed byte-identical" is the standard deletion test and it is silently unsound: the objlit annotation fallback (`discriminantKeysOfMember`'s AST leg) ablates byte-identical on the compiler profile and is LOAD-BEARING off it — the profile simply carries no `p: typeof X` shape — while the `.kind` DOMAIN veto ablated byte-identical having FIRED 11,667 times, which is what made deleting it safe. So instrument the ablation to count suppressed verdicts (or classify per SIGHTING) before reading a green run as deletion evidence; a zero count means the run tested nothing. Same failure class as round 744's "100% artifacts, deletable" inventory and round 750's frozen-memo probe.
- TypeScript compiler tests are **generated** by `./gradlew generateTypeScriptTests` into `build/generated/typescript-tests/`; this task requires the TypeScript repo to be cloned first (done automatically via `cloneTypeScriptRepo` dependency). Never edit generated files manually.
- **The test suite is C = A − B, mirroring tsgo's EXACT harness (build.gradle.kts) — the full-suite count is NOT the raw tsc corpus.** **(A)** `cloneTypeScriptRepo` pins `typescript-repo` to `typeScriptCommit` = the **PRISTINE mainline** parent of tsgo's `_submodules/TypeScript` merge — **LOAD-BEARING: never pin to the tsgo submodule sha**, whose `tsgo-port` branch REGENERATES baselines to the Go compiler's DIVERGENT output (union member order, `'boolean' | 'number'` vs our/pristine `'number' | 'boolean'`); we diff char-by-char against ORIGINAL tsc (owner directive: don't chase tsgo divergences). **(B)** three skips, all defined in `generateTypeScriptTests`: `tsgoSkippedTests` (whole-FILE, tsgo's own `skippedTests`), `usesUnsupportedOption` (whole-CONFIG, tsgo's `SkipUnsupportedCompilerOptions` over the harness-PARSED options — es3/es5, amd/umd/system, node/node10/classic, `outFile`, `baseUrl`, and the three `false`-valued interop flags), and `tsconfigInTestUsesRemovedFeature` (whole-FILE, 2026-07-02 owner-approved and DELIBERATELY beyond tsgo: a tsconfig EMBEDDED in a test setting `module: amd/umd/system` or `outFile` bypasses the directive filter; deliberately NARROW — embedded `target: es5` / `moduleResolution: node10` / `baseUrl` are NOT skipped). tsgo's `skippedEmitTests` is deliberately NOT mirrored (Go parallel-emit nondeterminism; our harness is deterministic). `scripts/tsgo_relevance.py` is a retained safety-net reporter; see TSGO-RELEVANCE.md, and the per-flag enumerations are in build.gradle.kts.
- **…AND THAT IS WHY `effectiveTarget` AND `defaultedTarget` MUST BOTH SURVIVE: THEY ARE tsgo's OWN SPLIT, NOT A tsc-6 RESIDUE — its EMIT at a written es5 is byte-identical to es2015 (12 shapes of 12, no ES5 transformer exists) while its CHECKER honours the written value, and BOTH collapse directions were built and lose measured rows (2026-09-16, (P18.113): onto the written target the strict-reserved binding rows go silent, onto the emit target (j2)'s kept TS2318 gate opens and the module default stops being CommonJS).** Corollaries from the same round: the es5 DEFAULT lib reaches es2015 through `lib.dom.d.ts`, so a written es5 has the whole es2015 surface (an `effectiveTarget`-shaped numeric lib model is wrong there — ask the RESOLVED lib set); `ES3` is an INVALID ARGUMENT in TypeScript 7 (TS6046, then the target is UNSET) where `ES5` is a known-but-REMOVED value (TS5108), so they may never be deleted together.
- **A WRITTEN `target: es5` IS *HONOURED* BY tsgo's CHECKER (`GetEmitScriptTarget` returns the written value; 29 `languageVersion` reads) BUT ONLY **ONE** OF THOSE READS IS `< ES2015` — the rest-only binding pattern's iterable type, `checker.go:17879` — and `LanguageFeatureMinimumTarget` bottoms out at ES2016 (2026-09-16, (P18.111), 20 gates measured one by one).** So "tsgo ignores a removed target" is true of the EMITTER and the LIB and false of the checker, and a `defaultedTarget < ES2015` gate here is dead only if tsgo has no matching rule — of ours, seven were, four were LIB questions in disguise (the TS2461/TS2488 forks read `iterableExists`, now `uplevelIterationLib()`), one was wrongly suppressing (TS18027) and one is tsgo's own (TS2318, keep). A version gate can hide behind a TABLE compare (`< a.first`, the TS1501 regexp flags) and evade a `< ES2015` grep.
- **TS2802 IS *LIB*-GATED IN TypeScript 7, NOT TARGET-GATED: tsgo iterates through the protocol whenever the global `Iterable` type EXISTS, and a written `target: es5` loads `lib.d.ts` → dom → es2015, so at the default lib the row can never fire — while `lib: ["es5"]` makes it fire at es2015 too (2026-09-15, (P18.109), 16 cells).** Any `defaultedTarget < ES2015` gate on an ITERATION rule is therefore a tsc-6 residue; the remaining ones (`checkIntersectionNeverArrayDestructure`, `spineIterableOperandActive`) are (LEGACY.1)(j)'s, and a rule rebuilt for (j4)'s es5 lib set must be spelled in lib terms.
- **AN EMBEDDED tsconfig IS *LOADED* BY tsgo's HARNESS AND THE `// @directive`s THEN **OVERRIDE** IT (`GetConfigNameFromFileName` matches the BASENAME; `CompileFiles` seeds the options from the file and only THEN applies `SetOptionsFromTestConfig`) — SO A SKIP PREDICATE THAT READS THE EMBEDDED BODY ALONE DELETES CASES tsgo ACTUALLY ANSWERED (2026-09-17, (LEGACY.1)(g): an embedded-only rule drops `maxNodeModuleJsDepthDefaultsToZero`, whose embedded `"moduleResolution": "node"` is overridden by `// @moduleResolution: bundler`).** `tsconfigInTestUsesRemovedFeature` therefore exempts any option a directive names, leaving it to `usesUnsupportedOption`. It fails SILENTLY — a wrong skip does not fail a test, it REMOVES one — so the guard is the four `tsgoExpected*` bucket constants: a gradeable case caught by a bad rule shows up as **`adopted` falling**, where a pristine-pinned one moves only `keptTsc`.
- **tsgo's CLI SHORT-CIRCUITS AFTER A CONFIG ERROR, SO A PROJECT SETTING A TS7-REMOVED OPTION IS NOT GRADEABLE AGAINST IT AT ALL — with `baseUrl` (or `outFile`) set it prints the TS5102 row and **not one semantic diagnostic**, not even for an obvious `const bad: number = "s"` in another file (2026-09-17, (LEGACY.1)(g)).** So "the import is now unresolved" and every other behavioural claim about such a project has to be measured some other way (the harness, a `-project` pin, or the LSP — CLAUDE.md's own (LEGACY.1) entry already reaches for the LSP when the CLI stops at an options row). Reading its silence as agreement is the reassuring direction.
- **A TS7-REMOVED OPTION VALUE CAN HAVE *LIVE* CORPUS COVERAGE THROUGH AN **EMBEDDED** tsconfig, WHICH `usesUnsupportedOption` NEVER SEES — 17 active baselines carry `moduleResolution: node10`/`node` that way, so "every configuration is already skipped" is a claim about DIRECTIVES only (2026-09-15, (P18.106); two ablation arms moved exactly those 17).** Before a (LEGACY.1) family round, grep the CASE files (`// @Filename: tsconfig.json` bodies) for the value as well as the directive census, and treat the errors screen as a gate, not a control, whenever the grep is non-zero. Same round: TypeScript 7 has NO classic resolution — every removed `moduleResolution` value is treated as UNSET and derives Node16/NodeNext/Bundler from the module kind (`GetModuleResolutionKind`), and TS5070/TS2792 have no emitter anywhere in tsgo.
- **`usesUnsupportedOption` IS A PER-*CONFIG* SKIP, SO A FILE WHOSE BARE DIRECTIVES ARE UNSUPPORTED STILL GENERATES TESTS FROM ITS VARIATIONS — `@target: es5, es2015` drops the bare subtests and KEEPS the `(target=es2015)` ones (round 836).** Any offline replication of the generator (the M3.0 per-case survey in `docs/conformance-worklist.md` is one) that reads it as a whole-FILE skip silently loses ~10% of the corpus and reports the lost cases as absent rather than failing — 13 of 125 conformance cases on the first pass, including every `labeledStatements` `NoCrash` fixture. Only `tsgoSkippedTests` and `tsconfigInTestUsesRemovedFeature` are file-level.
- **An UNSET `@target` maps to `effectiveTarget = ES2024` (tsc `getEmitScriptTarget`: unset → LatestStandard), NOT ES2015 — so a no-`@target` test KEEPS native class fields / async / spread, and its baselines are internally consistent.** **THERE ARE THREE TARGET NOTIONS AND A NEW READ MUST PICK ONE DELIBERATELY (rounds 944/945):** `effectiveTarget` is **EMIT ONLY** (45 Transformer/Emitter sites; an explicit es3/es5 maps UP to ES2015); **`defaultedTarget` is EVERY CHECKER QUESTION** — tsc's own single `languageVersion` — covering BOTH lib availability (`libFeatureAvailable`/`libProvidesGlobalAt`/`bindRealLibs`) and the 23 `target < ES2015` DOWNLEVEL gates; and the RAW `options.target` survives at exactly THREE sites, all listed in `CompilerOptions.defaultedTarget`'s KDoc. **`effectiveTarget` is NEVER right for a checker question** — mapping an explicit `es5` up deletes that program's genuine TS2550/TS2583 and OPENS every downlevel gate tsc keeps shut. **Both halves were invisible rather than deliberate: 6,436 of the corpus's 6,573 case files name an explicit `@target`**, and no case reaching a lib-availability mechanism omits `@target`/`@lib` — so **a pin about a downlevel gate must name its target explicitly** (`CompilerTestSupport.DOWNLEVEL_ES5`); 21 pre-945 pins relied on the ES3 default to open a gate for them. Round 944's "the downlevel gates are UNCHANGED and that is a measured decision" is RETRACTED (see the archive for the misattributed evidence).
- **…AND THAT RAW READ IS RIGHT FOR *LIB AVAILABILITY* AND WRONG FOR A *SYNTAX-SUPPORT* GATE, BECAUSE `options.target` DEFAULTS TO **ES3** WHILE tsc's UNSET DEFAULT IS THE LATEST STANDARD (round 941, TS18028).** `if (options.target <= ScriptTarget.ES5)` made every `#private` field in a project with NO `target` an error; the predicate a syntax gate wants is the target the user ASKED FOR — `options.targetExplicitlySet && options.target <= ES5` — and NOT `effectiveTarget`, which maps an explicit ES5 up to ES2015 and would drop the true positive. **The corpus cannot see either side**: `usesUnsupportedOption` skips every explicit es3/es5 config, so no ACTIVE baseline exercises such a gate at all. The sibling `target < ES2015` dispatch gates (e.g. `checkBlockScopedFunctionDeclarations`, TS1250) are unaudited and inherit the same question.
- **Disabled test category**: `.errors.txt` error baseline tests are commented out in `build.gradle.kts` `generateTypeScriptTests` task — search for `TODO: Re-enable when type checker is implemented`. Re-enabling adds ~9,055 tests (see docs/history/PLAN.md item 4c).
- Kotlin 2.x disallows `.` in backtick-quoted JVM method names (error: "Name contains illegal characters: .."); sanitize test function names by replacing dots with underscores (e.g. `foo_ts` not `foo.ts`). Some TypeScript test base names contain dots beyond the extension (e.g. `accessors_spec_section-4.5_error-cases`), so the entire `nameWithoutExtension` must have dots replaced, not just the `.ts`/`.js` suffix.
- **Write `src/commonTest` so it would compile for Kotlin/Native — NOTHING CHECKS THIS ANY MORE (all native targets commented out 2026-07-25 for loop speed), so violations accumulate SILENTLY and are paid for in a cleanup session whenever a native target returns.** Kotlin/Native is stricter than the JVM on three axes, and round 682 cleared 169 errors accrued since round 610 — while `./gradlew jvmTest` stayed 100% green throughout, because it is a JVM-only gate. (1) Backtick names additionally reject `(`, `)`, `,`, `&`, `@` ("Name contains illegal characters") — keep common test names to letters/digits/spaces/`-`/`_`/`'`, rendering a parenthetical aside as ` - aside` (`src/jvmTest` names are unaffected — that asymmetry is exactly why the drift goes unnoticed). (2) `kotlin.assert` is `@ExperimentalNativeApi` there and unusable from common code (and `@OptIn` for a native-only annotation cannot be written in common) — use `com.xemantic.kotlin.test.assert(cond)`, which is multiplatform AND power-assert-enabled (build.gradle.kts `powerAssert.functions`), so it takes NO message: the failure diagram renders every subexpression value, which is why the library's `message` parameter is not meant to be used. It also always evaluates, unlike `kotlin.assert` (a silent no-op without `-ea`). (3) JVM-only stdlib extensions (`String.format`) don't exist. To CHECK: `./gradlew -PenableNativeTargets=true compileTestKotlinLinuxX64` is now runnable LOCALLY under the memory protocol below (~1m40s); CI still runs `.github/workflows/native.yml`. **Round 822 re-measured the drift after ~10 dark rounds and found only 17 violations, ALL of axis 1 and ALL the same character — `,`** — because axes 2 and 3 are structurally closed by the one-idiom `assert` rule and the no-JVM-API rule, which agents inherit by copying neighbouring code, while a COMMA is the one illegal character that occurs naturally in English prose and this entry's actionable advice is about parentheses. Expect the same distribution next time: grep for `,` in backtick names first.
- **THERE IS A FOURTH KOTLIN/NATIVE DRIFT AXIS AND IT IS A *RUNTIME* ONE, SO `compileTestKotlinLinuxX64` BEING GREEN PROVES NOTHING ABOUT IT: a `commonTest` that drives multi-thousand-frame RECURSION compiles clean and then HARD-CRASHES the test process** (`runWithDeepStack` is a pass-through on native and a native stack overflow is uncatchable, so `StackOverflowError` there is a never-thrown stub). Gradle reports `Test running process exited unexpectedly. Current test: <name>` and every alphabetically-LATER class then silently never runs — one crasher masks all the rest, so finding them is iterative (round 822 lost 4,414 of 13,689 tests to a single test). **Depth alone is not the predicate**: 30,000-term chains, a 3,000-statement flow chain and the corpus's 6,452-term `binderBinaryExpressionStress` all PASS because the walkers are iterative; what crashes is true deep recursion (a >2,000-level flow walk) or any pin asserting the JVM boundary guard's TS2589. **ROUND 827 MOVED THE BUDGET, NOT THE AXIS: `runWithDeepStack` now runs the native pipeline on a `pthread_attr_setstacksize(256 MB)` thread, so `CfaTooLargeBailTest` + `Inv4SpineAccessorModifierDeepChainTest` are back in `commonTest` and green — but an overflow is still UNCATCHABLE there, so a deep enough shape still kills the process and a TS2589-asserting pin is still unexpressible (`DeepExpressionChainTest` stays `src/jvmTest`).** A `none { code == 2589 }` assertion that DOES ride along is vacuous on native — say so in the KDoc rather than reading it as coverage.

- **`--noEmit` means the COMPILE CORE must not transform/emit either (round 738) — and the gate is `CompilerOptions.skipEmitOutputs`, NEVER `options.noEmit`.** `noEmit` is also a corpus DIRECTIVE that **440 generated tests set**, and their baselines were produced by a core that still emits; `skipEmitOutputs` is set only by `ProjectCompiler` from its own `noEmit` parameter. Do not "simplify" the Phase-3 gate to `options.noEmit` — `SkipEmitOutputsTest`'s negative control exists for exactly that. Round 738's follow-on benchmark claim ("every pre-738 xtsc-vs-tsc ratio compared our check+emit against tsc's check-only; the gap is ~2.15×") is **RETRACTED — round 739 read the scripts: the CI 3-way runs EVERY compiler with emit, so the 2.4× was already like-for-like and this gate does not move it.**
- **A PERF RATIO IS MODE-SCOPED, AND THE TWO MODES ARE DIFFERENT COMPILES (round 739, `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md` § 0.2 has the table).** `bench-3way.sh` (the published `bench-history/` ratio) runs xtsc, tsc AND tsgo **with emit**; `ab-interleaved.sh` and `cost_gate.py` — i.e. every perf number this arc produces — run `--noEmit`. Measured: the emit work is **8.5% of a check-only compile**, so the two are not interchangeable and **the check-only ratio was never measured on either side** (the bench never ran tsc with `--noEmit`; it does now). Always name the mode with the ratio, and never read ONE CI row as the ratio — xtsc is a single cold JVM run per row and the ratio ranges 1.87×–2.72× across 340 rows of an essentially unchanged compiler.
- **A SECTION-PROBE RUN ON A MEMORY-STARVED BOX INFLATES EVERY ROW BY ~270x WHILE THE RATIO BETWEEN ARMS SURVIVES, SO NO INTERNAL CONSISTENCY CHECK CAN CATCH IT (round 800).** A batch that built and then immediately measured — the build's own Kotlin daemon still resident, BUILD.1's trap — read 6,483 ms for a row whose true value is 5-11 ms and took 9.6 min per compile instead of 26 s. **The only tell is the ABSOLUTE ns/call against the previous round's table**; put `./gradlew --stop` plus a graceful bracket-pattern kotlin-daemon kill INSIDE the script, between the build and the runs, and note that DETERMINISTIC counters are immune and can still carry the round.
- **WATCHING A BENCHMARK RUN IS PART OF THE BENCHMARK — round 774's A/A read −6.70% on the SAME binary while the agent `tail`ed its log every few seconds, and −0.36% / −0.54% / +1.02% when left alone (process-median sd 278 → 48 ms).** One xtsc run already takes ~3.15 of this box's 4 cores, so the spare capacity your shell commands eat is the capacity the measurement needs: start a run in the background, then END THE TURN and react to the notification — do not poll. There are TWO A/B protocols with different bands (COLD `scripts/ab-interleaved.sh` ±2.0%, WARM `scripts/ab-warm.sh` ±1.0%, `docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md` § 6); a warm run whose printed per-arm sd exceeds ~1% was not measured on a quiet box and its verdict must be discarded, however clean the median looks.
- **`bench/` is GITIGNORED (.gitignore line 62) — `bench/*.tsv` is a LOCAL dashboard, not a shared baseline.** "Re-baseline the dashboard" cannot be done by committing TSV rows; the shared record is `bench-history/` (CI-written), STATUS.md and the round notes. A TSV row you add is invisible to the next agent on another machine.
- **The crawl's per-file read/pre-parse spans are ELAPSED-WITH-SUSPENSION, not CPU (round 738).** They bracket a `withContext(...)`, so a file's "parse" includes waiting for a `Dispatchers.Default` slot — which is why the pre-parse sum is ~10.7× the crawl's WALL. **Only the crawl WALL is a wall-clock price**; never add those sub-sums into a total. Race-freedom there is by construction (each element carries its own nanos back; the SINGLE-THREADED collector sums them) — a `+=` from the workers races exactly as `PassTiming.nodeKindHistogram` does.

- **A RECEIPT MATRIX WHOSE PROBES ALL SIT *INSIDE* THE SCOPE UNDER CHANGE CANNOT SEE A FIX THAT MOVES THE WRONG ANSWER *OUTSIDE* IT, AND IT WILL READ AS A CLEAN WIN — 36 in-block cells reported `9 ours-only / 19 missing -> 0 / 10` for a change whose honest net, with one after-block read added per cell, is `2 / 22 -> 0 / 20` (2026-09-11, (CHK.118), REFUSED on that number).** The leak is not benign in either direction: where the shadowed member exists on BOTH types the after-scope read becomes a **confident false positive on legal code**, and where it exists on neither it becomes a silence — so the in-scope matrix is biased toward the fix twice over. **Any receipt for a change to a SCOPED recording must probe at least one read PAST the scope's end**, and a probe population drawn only from the shape the fix targets is the round-902 dead-arm law one instrument over. Corollary measured in the same round: the PARENT may false-positive on the same lines, so state whether an FP class APPEARS or merely MOVES — 2 FP + 1 true row becoming 1 FP + 1 missing is a different claim from "the fix introduces a false positive".
### Measured dead-ends (full detail archived — do NOT re-attempt without reading the archive entry)

This section's title has been true only since 2026-09-21: the full text of every entry — 25 moved wholesale plus all 65 distilled below — now lives in `docs/history/CLAUDE-GOTCHAS-ARCHIVE.md` under `## Measured dead-ends + test assertion gotchas — moved from CLAUDE.md 2026-09-21`. **Nothing was deleted.** A distilled entry keeps the REFUSAL and its MEASURED NUMBER and ends in `(archive: <key>)`; the METHOD, the per-fixture detail and the derivation are one grep away — `command grep -n '<key or mechanism|round|(INC.n)>' docs/history/CLAUDE-GOTCHAS-ARCHIVE.md`. **GREP THE ARCHIVE BEFORE RE-ATTEMPTING ANYTHING HERE** — a shortened entry means "the measurement is in the archive", never "this was only a hunch", and an entry with no stub at all is a per-mechanism refusal you will reach by grepping that mechanism.

- **WIDENING AN OBJECT-LITERAL MEMBER'S ENUM TYPE BREAKS DISCRIMINATED-UNION SELECTION PROGRAM-WIDE (+7 rows per profile, +22 on harness); it ships at (CHK.91) only with a FRESHNESS gate plus a PULL-derived contextual keep, and a keep decided from the PUSH field alone loses those 22 sites.** An enum-member ACCESS is the ONE literal-like type that survives into a member. (archive: SUPERSEDED 2026-09-05 ((CHK.91) LANDED):)
- **`BinderResult.flowGraph` IS BUILT ON FIRST ASK, SO A NEW `binderResults` LOOP THAT TOUCHES IT SILENTLY RESTORES A 126 ms COST AND NO GATE SEES IT** (only the incremental floor moves). Keep the `lazy`: `CheckerPool`/`--shareBind` hand ONE `BinderResult` set to several threads. (archive: `BinderResult.flowGraph` IS BUILT ON)
- **A COST PRIOR DOES NOT TRANSFER ACROSS *REGIMES* — the identical candidate is 0.3% of a FULL build and 24% of an incremental FLOOR.** Before re-reading any refusal here, ask which regime it was measured in: full vs partitioned, cold vs warm, sequential vs `--workers`. (archive: …AND THE LAW IT MEASURES IS THAT A)
- **EVERY `BinderResult` FROM ONE `Binder` SHARES THAT BINDER'S `nodeToSymbol`/`moduleInstanceStates`, KEYED `(pos, end)` — THEY COLLIDE ACROSS FILES AND ARE LAST-WINS**, which makes per-file bind REUSE unsound as a drop-in and is invisible from `bind()`'s signature. (archive: EVERY `BinderResult` FROM ONE `Binder` SHARES)
- **THE INCREMENTAL FLOOR IS 75% PROGRAM-WIDE TAIL WALKERS AND ONLY 20% BIND** (of 1,219 ms: 400 tail walkers 806.7, `init:*` 112.2, bind 240.6, crawl 27.4, `checkSpine` 0.1) — **and the tail is FLAT**, largest pass 26.3 ms, so there is nobody to make cheaper. Round 880's "bind 515 ms" is a per-WORKER term and does NOT transfer. (archive: THE INCREMENTAL FLOOR IS 75% PROGRAM-WIDE)
- **A CONSERVATIVE JOIN CAN BE LOAD-BEARING FOR SOMEBODY ELSE'S BUG — making `narrowTypeFromFlowCore`'s `FlowLoopLabel` answer precise turned 5 reads in tsc's `emitter.ts` into `'length' does not exist on type 'never'` (REFUSED, 8 ours-only rows per profile).** Before making any conservative answer precise, ask what it is masking. (archive: A CONSERVATIVE JOIN CAN BE *LOAD-BEARING FOR SOMEBODY ELSE'S BUG*:)
- **A KILLED REBUILD LEAVES *NO* `Checker.class`, AND A SCRATCH-PROJECT CLI RUN THEN PRINTS **ZERO DIAGNOSTICS** — WHICH READS EXACTLY LIKE A CLEAN FIXTURE (2026-08-28).** Round 851's trap with a new trigger: the 10-minute tool timeout killed an ablation driver between `git checkout --` and its rebuild, and the next probe measured a classpath the compiler is not on. `scripts`/`build/chk*/ours.sh`-style helpers END IN A GREP, so their exit status is the GREP's and "no rows" and "no compiler" are both exit 1; the only tell is the raw output's `diagnostics: N error(s)` line. `md5sum` the class file before believing any scratch run.
- **A SOURCE-SCANNING ANALYZER OVER `Checker.kt` PARSES ITS OWN *KDoc EXAMPLE* AS REAL CODE AND RUNS AWAY OVER THE FILE, SO EVERY WALKER READS "no hazard" — IT FAILS TOWARD *SAFE TO CHANGE*, THE ONLY DIRECTION THAT MATTERS FOR A CLASSIFIER.** Round 809's stripper rule is necessary and nowhere near sufficient; control set below. (archive: A SOURCE-SCANNING ANALYZER OVER `Checker.kt` WILL)
- **GATING A TAIL WALKER ONTO THE PARTITION BANKS ~79-85% OF ITS ROW, NOT 100% — the rest reappears on OTHER passes, because the gated walkers drive shared MEMOIZED resolutions and the first later asker pays.** Any per-pass sum quoted as a prize needs that discount until re-measured. (archive: GATING A TAIL WALKER ONTO THE PARTITION)
- **`checkedResults` IS `binderResults` *EXACTLY* WHEN `assignedFileNames == null`, SO MOVING A PASS LOOP ONTO IT IS A STRICT NO-OP FOR EVERY FULL BUILD AND THE WHOLE CORPUS — IT CAN ONLY CHANGE A PARTITION.** A pure EMITTER is safe; a COLLECTOR is round 609's 1,174 TS2339 false positives. Detector `scripts/partition-equivalence.sh`, corpus second. (archive: AND `checkedResults` IS `binderResults` *EXACTLY*)
- **A BUILD-COUNTING PIN THAT COUNTS READS OF A FILE THE TEST *EDITS* READS ZERO BUILDS FOR A BUILD THAT HAPPENED — AN OVERLAID FILE IS SERVED FROM THE OVERLAY AND NEVER REACHES THE BACKING `Vfs`.** Count a file the test does NOT edit, with a control calibrating the unit. (archive: A BUILD-COUNTING PIN THAT COUNTS READS)
- **A WHOLE-SOURCE SUBSTRING SCAN FOR A CONSTRUCT WITH NO AST NODE TESTS FOR THE *WORDS* — `checker.ts` says `export as namespace` TWICE, both in `//` comments, and that ONE false positive moved a measured stability rate from 67% to 32%**, i.e. it presented as a plausible REFUSAL, not a defect. Anchor such a scan to a line start at minimum; no fixture finds this, a real edit corpus did. (archive: A WHOLE-SOURCE SUBSTRING SCAN FOR A)
- **HASHING A RESOLVED-TYPE GRAPH WITH A *PATH-ONLY* CYCLE GUARD IS EXPONENTIAL IN DAG WIDTH AND ITS SYMPTOM IS A HANG WITH NO DIAGNOSIS (159 s in one build).** Cache a COMPLETED subtree only when CLOSED — an open one's hash carries a path DISTANCE; a SELF-reference still counts as closed. tsc's graph is then one giant SCC, so the memo has nothing to memoize until the component completes. (archive: HASHING A RESOLVED-TYPE GRAPH WITH)
- **AN ID-KEYED HASH PASSES EVERY STRUCTURAL TEST AND THEN INVALIDATES EVERYTHING ALWAYS, SO A CROSS-BUILD FINGERPRINT NEEDS A "two builds of identical text agree" CONTROL FIRST** (`Type.id`/`Symbol.id` are per-build AND per-thread). Mirror hazard: read `Type.Intrinsic.intrinsicName`, since B58.1 renders `errorType` as `any`. (archive: AN ID-KEYED HASH PASSES EVERY STRUCTURAL)
- **AN EMPTY `build/classes/kotlin/jvm/main` *DURING* A KOTLIN COMPILE IS NORMAL — THE BACKEND WRITES ITS OUTPUT AT THE END — AND READING IT MID-BUILD MANUFACTURES A CONVINCING ROUND-851 "the build was killed and wiped the class dir" DIAGNOSIS (2026-08-29).** It cost a redundant rebuild and a concurrent second Gradle invocation, which is the one thing the one-build-at-a-time rule exists to stop. Check `ps` for a live `compileKotlinJvm` before reading an empty class dir as a wipe; round 851's trap is real but its tell is an output dir whose mtime is LATER than a log that says it compiled, not an empty dir while a compile is in flight.
- **A LANGUAGE-SERVICE CACHE MUST BE SERVED BY *CONTAINMENT OF WHAT WAS ASKED*, NEVER BY MEMBERSHIP OF THE FILE — AN ANSWER NEVER ASKED FOR IS ABSENT, AND AN ABSENT CAPTURE RENDERS NOTHING WITH NO ERROR ANYWHERE.** Decide it against the stored REQUEST's own spans; the pin is a caret on a NON-occurrence in a prepared file. (archive: A LANGUAGE-SERVICE CACHE MUST BE SERVED)
- **A MEMO BOUNDED BY ENTRY *COUNT* LETS A ONE-SPAN ENTRY EVICT A 125,289-SPAN ONE — bound a MIXED-population cache by WEIGHT, in lanes that cannot evict each other**, and never raise such a limit to make a latency case green. (archive: A MEMO BOUNDED BY ENTRY *COUNT* LETS)
- **A CAPTURE BUILD'S DIAGNOSTICS ARE NOT A PLAIN BUILD'S, SO NO CAPTURE CACHE MAY EVER ANSWER `diagnostics`/`diagnosticsOf`** (standing rule, `docs/language-service.md` § 3) — a capture types nodes the checker had no reason to type. The error half is `diagnosticsOf`'s memo, keyed by the PARTITION the build walked. (archive: AND A CAPTURE BUILD'S DIAGNOSTICS ARE)
- **NARROWING THE *CHECK* FOR AN EDITOR QUERY IS DONE AND CLOSED — A MEDIAN FILE'S OWN CHECKING IS 15 ms AGAINST A 1,092 ms FLOOR, i.e. THE FLOOR IS 99% OF A NARROWED QUERY.** An editor's question needs NO reverse-dependency closure, and **a partition's result must NEVER be adopted as a project's build** — its diagnostics are a subset. (archive: NARROWING THE *CHECK* FOR AN EDITOR)
- **THE *CHECK* PATH AND THE *CAPTURE* PATH DISAGREE UNDER A PARTITION — A TYPE REFERENCE INSIDE A FOREIGN FILE'S ANONYMOUS OBJECT TYPE LITERAL RENDERS `any` IN A CAPTURED TYPE WHILE THE SAME BUILD REJECTS THE ASSIGNMENT THAT DEPENDS ON IT** (45 of 381,666 spans; a LAZY MEMBER TABLE, and 5 of the 45 run the other way). **Do NOT infer a capture's correctness from a green diagnostics sweep — different resolvers.** (archive: THE *CHECK* PATH AND THE *CAPTURE*)
- **A PARTITION OR REPLAY CHANGE IS GRADED ON THE *CAPTURE* SWEEP, NOT ONLY THE DIAGNOSTICS SWEEP — a lost collector or type-parameter scope surfaces as a WRONG TYPE, never a missing error** (one run read `diagnosticRows=46` agreeing while 8 of 75 files diverged). Run BOTH `replay-differential.sh` and `capture-equivalence.sh`. (archive: SO A PARTITION OR REPLAY CHANGE IS)
- **A WRITE-ONCE INTERNED FIELD MAKES ITS VALUE A FUNCTION OF THE DISPATCH *ORDER*, AND `checkSpine`'s ORDER IS A FUNCTION OF THE *PARTITION*** — which is why all ~13k corpus baselines are blind to the family. The rule: resolve a type parameter's constraint in a SECOND loop INSIDE the scope install, never inside `typeParamInternCache.getOrPut { }`. (archive: A WRITE-ONCE INTERNED FIELD MAKES ITS)
- **THE OUTER-SCOPE CONSTRAINT RESOLUTION IS LOAD-BEARING FOR A SELF-REFERENTIAL ALIAS (`type Shared<I, D extends Shared<I, D>>`), SO "hoist it everywhere" IS A REAL STACK OVERFLOW.** **Read a TS2589 at `(0,0)` as `reportCheckerStackOverflow`, never as a depth diagnostic.** (archive: …AND THE OUTER-SCOPE RESOLUTION IS)
- **`aliasDisplayMap` IS AN *ID-KEYED GLOBAL* AND STRUCTURALLY CANNOT EXPRESS WHAT tsc DOES — tsc PICKS AN ALIAS BY THE REFERENCE's DECLARATION SITE.** Two synonymous aliases on one interned type are FIRST-WINS here; only a change of KEY closes it, so read such a divergence as a KEY problem and a logical-parity conversation, never a refactor. (archive: …AND `aliasDisplayMap` IS AN *ID-KEYED GLOBAL*, SO)
- **A PIN THAT INSTALLS THE MODE IT WANTS AND RESTORES IT LEAVES THE SHIPPED *DEFAULT* PINNED BY NOTHING, SO AN ABLATION RESTORING THE OLD BEHAVIOUR READS 0 RED AND LOOKS REDUNDANT.** Ship ONE pin asserting the default IS the intended configuration, with no mode install in it. (archive: A PIN THAT INSTALLS THE MODE IT WANTS)
- **"THE PASS WRITES A CHECKER FIELD" IS NOT A REFUSAL UNTIL YOU ASK WHETHER THE WRITE IS A PROPERTY OF THE *PROGRAM* OR OF THE *FILE* — 9 of 53 refused passes were installing a per-FILE ambient and were gateable all along.** Split a MIXED pass: the program-wide INDEX loop stays, the per-file EMITTING loop narrows. A retractor's `fileName = null` row SURVIVES the partition filter. (archive: "THE PASS WRITES A CHECKER FIELD" IS)
- **THE `srcScan` FAMILY MUST BE GATED ALL AT ONCE OR NOT AT ALL — `srcHas` BUILDS ITS PER-FILE n-GRAM FILTER *LAZILY*, SO THE FIRST CALLER IN PASS ORDER PAYS ~19.4 ms FOR ALL 78 FILES AND GATING ONE WALKER HANDS IT TO THE NEXT.** Read any "this walker got dearer" as "did it become the first ASKER of something lazy" first. (archive: THE `srcScan` FAMILY MUST BE GATED)
- **A WHOLE-PROGRAM GATE *ABOVE* A PER-FILE LOOP IS A QUESTION ABOUT THE PROGRAM AND MUST STAY ON `binderResults`, SO NARROWING THAT LOOP BANKS ESSENTIALLY NOTHING — WHAT BANKS IS A FILE-NAME PRE-GATE** (two passes went 2.5 ms -> 0.002). A pre-gate is unsound for a pass that wipes rows once its needle is found. (archive: A WHOLE-PROGRAM GATE *ABOVE* A PER-FILE)
- **A RATIO IS A PROPERTY OF WHAT *BOTH* ARMS WERE ASKED, AND A COST COMMON TO BOTH DILUTES IT WITHOUT TRACE** — a "decaying" 3.06x -> 1.68x lineage read 2.25-2.30x / 4.2x at the median query once a whole-file capture request was dropped from both arms. **Ask what the arms were asked, not only what regime they ran in.** (archive: A RATIO IS A PROPERTY OF WHAT *BOTH*)
- **A PROGRAM-WIDE `init` PASS MAY BE THE PROGRAM'S *FIRST-TOUCH ORDER* RATHER THAN ITS OUTPUT, SO BEFORE PRICING ONE ASK WHAT THE ORDER BUYS** — `init:buildFileLocalTypeMaps` was refused on three axes (deferral by PHASE moved 2,722 / 1,665 capture spans; the deferrable part is 1.13 ms of 66) and then SUPERSEDED by (INC.25), which partition-scoped it once the ONE truncated resolution behind its lost resolutions was fixed. (archive: A PROGRAM-WIDE `init` PASS MAY BE THE)
- **"A FULL BUILD IS UNCHANGED BY CONSTRUCTION" IS THE CLAIM MOST LIKELY TO BE TRUE OF THE CODE AND FALSE OF THE BINARY — MAKE IT A DIGEST, NOT AN ARGUMENT.** A structural argument alone is round 853's frozen classpath waiting to happen. (archive: "A FULL BUILD IS UNCHANGED BY CONSTRUCTION")
- **A MEMBER TYPE THAT DEGRADES TO `any` UNDER A PARTITION IS USUALLY A *TRUNCATED* RESOLUTION, NOT A CACHE-ORDER ONE — round 778's write gate is NOT the mechanism** (same `ambient=empty persisted=true` in both arms, differing only in `truncated`: 1 of 822 narrowed, 0 of 21,315 full). The lever is the CYCLE HANDLING. (archive: A MEMBER TYPE THAT DEGRADES TO `any` UNDER)
- **A LIB INTERFACE'S BODY IS NEVER SPINE-WALKED, SO A SHAPE THAT RESOLVES WHEN YOU HAND-WRITE IT CAN BE BROKEN FOR THE *LIB* TYPE OF THE SAME SHAPE** (`[Symbol.unscopables]` rendered `any` for every small project on the shipped build). **A failed repro of a lib-type defect is evidence about the SPINE, not the shape** — reproduce against the lib type; the 78-file tsc profiles HIDE it. (archive: A LIB INTERFACE'S BODY IS NEVER SPINE-WALKED, SO)
- **`getUnionType` PERFORMS NO SUBTYPE REDUCTION, SO A FLOW JOIN KEEPS A NARROWED SUBTYPE BESIDE THE DECLARATION'S CONSTITUENTS — A **FILTER** BUG, NOT A DISPLAY ONE: the extra member survives a later discriminant test.** `flowJoinUnion` reduces at the TWO joins in `narrowTypeFromFlowCore` and NOWHERE else — never inside `getUnionType`, where INV.5(a) interning and byte-pinned member ORDER make it a whole-corpus change. `subtypeRelation` has ZERO readers. (archive: `getUnionType` PERFORMS NO SUBTYPE)
- **A FULL-vs-NARROW DIFFERENTIAL SILENTLY ASSUMES THE *FULL* ARM IS THE REFERENCE, AND WHEN THE FULL ARM HOLDS THE DEFECT THE GATE REPORTS A FIX AS A REGRESSION.** The target is not "narrow agrees with full" but "neither arm has a wrong name to disagree about" — **refuse on sight any proposal to close it by making narrow match full.** (archive: A FULL-vs-NARROW DIFFERENTIAL SILENTLY)
- **A SOURCE-SCANNING ANALYZER'S FUNCTION SPANS ARE CUT BY A *MULTI-LINE PARAMETER LIST*, TRUNCATING A FUNCTION TO ITS HEADER SO EVERY FIELD WRITE READS AS ABSENT — IT CLEARS A PASS IT SHOULD REFUSE.** Working control set: raw `fun` count == stripped count with ZERO blanked, every `pass("name")` KDoc sample REFUSED, whole-file brace balance 0, spans non-overlapping, named body probes, **plus a KNOWN-REFUSED set used as an oracle**. (archive: A SOURCE-SCANNING ANALYZER'S FUNCTION)
- **…AND IN THIS CHECKER A `pass("…")`-REGISTERING HELPER IS NOT A CALLER: WITHOUT EXCLUDING THE 12 `initCheckPasses*` REGISTRARS FROM THE CALL GRAPH, *EVERY* WALKER READS AS "REACHED FROM ELSEWHERE" AND THE CLEAN SET IS **0** (2026-08-23).** The registrars mention every pass by name, so a naive reachability pass makes the analyzer answer "refuse everything" — which looks like conservatism and is in fact a dead instrument.
- **AND THE FIXTURE FOR "DOES NARROWING LOSE A DIAGNOSTIC" IS VACUOUS BY DEFAULT: AN ARGUMENT-POSITION ERROR (`use({ program: 1 })` AGAINST A FOREIGN TYPE LITERAL PARAMETER) IS NOT REPORTED BY THIS COMPILER AT ALL, SO BOTH ARMS AGREE ON AN EMPTY LIST AND THE PIN PASSES HAVING MEASURED NOTHING (2026-08-22).** The shape that DOES report is a read of the literal's member (`const n: number = make().program` -> TS2322). Any equivalence pin over two compilers, two modes or two arms needs a control asserting the reference arm is NON-EMPTY — round 790's "a verifier reads 0 both when the skip is sound and when the instrument is dead", one instrument over.
- **AN IMPOSSIBLE *ORDERING* IS THE CHEAPEST TELL THAT A WARM-UP RAMP IS BEING MEASURED, AND IT IS SHARPER THAN ANY SPREAD STATISTIC (2026-08-22).** A "floor" arm measured 1,632 ms where the median partition it is a strict SUBSET of the work of measured 1,107 — impossible, and the cause was position: the floor sat at slot 3 of the process while the partitions ran at slots 4-81, and the same process read a full build at 9,421 ms against a warm 4,818. **Put timing arms after whatever warms the JVM, rotate them, and check subset-vs-superset before quoting anything**; four rotated draws then spanned 0.7%. Round 869's law with a free consistency check attached.
- **52.3% OF THE FLOW NODES THIS COMPILER MINTS ARE NEVER READ AND IT IS WORTH 0.63% — DO NOT RE-OPEN "the flow graph builds too much".** Only 22.0% of the minting WALK is in containers nothing reads. **And the asymmetry is INVERTED from a side table: a missing FLOW NODE makes `flowAt` answer null and the compiler emits a FALSE POSITIVE** — any laziness here must defer-and-build, never omit. (archive: 52.3% OF THE FLOW NODES THIS COMPILER)
- **THE FLOW-NODE CLASSES MUST STAY PLAIN CLASSES — `FlowCensus` (and any future side table) keys an IDENTITY `HashSet<FlowNode>` on them, which is sound only while they are not `data` classes (round 865).** Adding `data` to `FlowBranchLabel` & co. turns every such set into round 471's deep-`hashCode()` hazard over a mutable antecedent graph, silently. `FlowNode.id` is NOT an alternative key: it restarts at 0 in every file, exactly as `nodeId` does (round 787).
- **THE INV.4 REACH MACHINERY IS CENSUSED AND CLOSED — 3.32 M edge evaluations, 1.91 M consultations per warm rebuild, every mechanism below 1%.** Three traps: the 41-119-arm `when (parent)` chains are **13.3 ns** each (106 arms costs the same as 49; whole population 44 ms = 0.67%), a push-based status is **11.1x MORE** work, and an 8% memo hit rate with 23 classifiers at 0% is CORRECT. Only the memo TRANSPOSITION is open, at <= ~0.8%. (archive: THE INV.4 REACH MACHINERY IS CENSUSED)
- **A PER-FILE, NAME-KEYED "could this root ever be narrowed" SET IS VACUOUS — it refuses 0 of 14,117 openings and costs 150-211 ms**, because every root DECLARED in a file is in that set by construction. Skipping a narrowing walk needs a path-and-position oracle, which is the walk. Pin such a predicate at the SET level — `diagnose()` is single-file, so the refusing population is unreachable from a fixture. (archive: A PER-FILE, NAME-KEYED "could this)
- **A PASS WHOSE PRODUCT HAS ONE READER MAY STILL BE UNDEFERRABLE, AND A PASS THAT LOOKS LIKE A COLLECTOR MAY BE PURE EMIT-ONLY WORK — CHECK THE READER'S *CALLER*, NOT THE PASS'S NAME** (`trackAllImportReferences` walked 78 times per checker to fill a set a `--noEmit` build cannot read). Prefer deferring to a static `skipEmitOutputs` return, since the corpus EMITS; a pin on the mechanism's OBVIOUS shape can be vacuous. (archive: A PASS WHOSE PRODUCT HAS ONE READER)
- **A PRODUCED-vs-CONSUMED CENSUS MUST BE KEYED ON THE *CALLS*, NEVER ON WHAT WAS STORED (round 829, a 4x under-read).** Keying the deletable population on map ENTRIES missed the largest population in the pass — **67.3% of its resolutions store nothing at all** (they answer `any`/`errorType`), so they can never be "read" and were structurally invisible; the tell is a classified population that does not sum to its own denominator.
- **A FALSIFIER CONTROL CAN BE DEAD ON THE VERY PROFILE THE PRIZE WAS MEASURED ON — RUN IT WHERE THE THING IT WATCHES HAPPENS** (`--ccetPreGateBogus` reports 14 on harness and **0** on the compiler profile). Check the control fires on the SAME run that reports the zero; if it cannot, say so and move the falsification to a fixture. (archive: A FALSIFIER CONTROL CAN BE DEAD ON)
- **A CALL WHOSE CALLEE IS A MEMBER OF AN *IMPORTED* NAMESPACE IS NEVER ARGUMENT-CHECKED — `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` does not run for it at all** (1,108 such calls on the compiler profile, 1,518 on services). So **a call-argument fix measuring ZERO firings on the profiles is probably not inert, it is invisible** — price it against a probe using a plain imported function. (archive: A CALL WHOSE CALLEE IS A MEMBER OF)
- **A PER-KIND SPINE DISPATCH TABLE IS CLOSED *COLD* AND IS A ~1% LEVER WARM — DO NOT RE-DERIVE IT.** One declining consultation is **2.286 ns**, so the whole prize is **73.2 ms = 1.06%** of a warm rebuild. Do NOT re-run `--dispatchGated` (it measures `G - R`, machinery +715 ms); the shape is decided — a per-kind `Long` BITMASK, never a 138-arm `when (kindId)` (over the 8,000-bytecode cliff). **Six handlers hold ~61-63% of the spine.** (archive: A PER-KIND SPINE DISPATCH TABLE IS)
- **BYTES IS A 10x-NOISY PROXY FOR CHECKING COST — CENSUS PER *NODE*, OR A SIZE LADDER MANUFACTURES A SUPER-LINEARITY THAT DOES NOT EXIST.** Per-byte is 76-739 us/KB and two 1.3-1.5 KB files differ 3.5x; per NODE the population is flat (p10 6.08 / median 9.71 / p90 19.63 us) and `checker.ts` sits at the p10. (archive: BYTES IS A 10x-NOISY PROXY FOR CHECKING)
- **A SECTION-PROBE'S OWN BOUNDARY COST MUST BE PRICED DIFFERENTIALLY — an in-situ EMPTY-SPAN calibration over-reads by 3.5-4.4x and misled three consecutive rounds** (306/391 ns read against a true ~86-92). Run the SAME code at N boundaries and at 1 and divide by the extra boundary count; `ArgSections`' coarse MODE is the shape to copy. Per-section nanos are sound for RELATIVE attribution only. (archive: A SECTION-PROBE'S OWN BOUNDARY COST)
- **WHEN THE THING BEING PRICED IS SMALLER THAN A TIMESTAMP PAIR, AMPLIFY THE SIGNAL INSTEAD OF SHRINKING THE INSTRUMENT** — `p(r) = cold + (r-1)*warm + boundary`, so **two values of `r` cancel the boundary algebraically**. **Falsify by ARITHMETIC, not timing**: the sink must be an exact multiple of the hit count at every `r`, and three independent pairs must agree on the slope. (archive: When the thing being priced is SMALLER)
- **AN AMPLIFICATION LADDER READ OFF THE WHOLE-REBUILD WALL MUST BE ABBA-ROTATED INSIDE EACH PROCESS — THE FIRST INSTRUMENTED REBUILD IS THE SLOWEST DRAW, WORTH UP TO 15%** — run `r16,r8,r0,r0,r8,r16` with >=8 rebuilds first. **AND ROTATION INSIDE ONE PROCESS IS NOT ENOUGH AT TWO DRAWS PER ARM: RUN THE MIRROR ROTATION AS A SECOND BATCH** (one binary read 53.6 vs 14.1 ms/rep, pooling to 33.9/32.6). A single 6-draw ladder is a batch, not a result. (archive: …AND AN AMPLIFICATION LADDER READ OFF)
- **THE REVERSE-REPLAY SCOPE MECHANISM HAS ONE HOME — `MapScopeStack<V>` / `SetScopeStack` (`ScopeStack.kt`) — AND A THIRD COPY FAMILY MUST INSTANTIATE IT, NEVER HAND-ROLL A FOURTH.** The scope is a `MutableMap`/`MutableSet` FACADE (helpers write through it) and `clear()` THROWS; a frame SHARING its parent's map is "open no scope", a flag the pop mirrors. (archive: THE REVERSE-REPLAY SCOPE MECHANISM)
- **A PER-SCOPE WHOLE-MAP COPY IS REPLACEABLE BY ONE LIVE MAP + AN UNDO LOG *EXACTLY* WHEN THE STACK IS STRICTLY LIFO, EVERY REMOVAL IS RECORDED AND NO READER MUTATES OR RETAINS THE MAP — and what decides whether it is worth it is `writes/entries`, never the copy count.** A local pointer swap is NOT a retention; a detached snapshot is NOT a disqualifier. **`currentLocalTypes`, `CtaFrame.localTypes` and `CtaFrame.varTypes` DO NOT satisfy all three — do not widen `AnnScopeStack` to them without new pins.** (archive: A PER-SCOPE WHOLE-MAP COPY IS REPLACEABLE)
- **WHEN A SCOPE'S MAP IS HANDED *OUT* TO HELPERS THAT WRITE INTO IT, THE SCOPE MUST BE A `MutableMap` FACADE (`put`/`putAll`/`remove` through the log), AND THE FACADE IS THEN THE ONLY PLACE THE FAMILY'S WRITES CAN BE COUNTED** — a census hooking 2 of 3 write paths was 6.3x optimistic with nothing to say so. **`clear()` must THROW.** (archive: …AND WHEN THE SCOPE'S MAP IS HANDED)
- **A SKIP'S POSITIVE CONTROL IS ITS COMPLEMENT POPULATION, WHICH COSTS NOTHING TO SHIP (round 790).** A verifier that keeps the OLD behaviour and counts violations over the skipped population (round 788's protocol) reads 0 both when the skip is sound and when the instrument is dead. Running the SAME comparison over the population the gate never skips answers that for free — there it must diverge — so no deliberately bogus baseline has to be injected. `--verifyLoopRetry` / `--verifyLoopRetryAll` are the reference pair.
- **THE FLOW-NARROWING TAIL IS EXTREME AND ITS THREE OBVIOUS CAUSES ARE ALL MEASURED FALSE — 394 of 70,037 walks (0.56%) cost 1,485 ms = 47% of all narrowing and 4.9% of the compile.** It does NOT trip, does NOT exhaust the visit budget, and is NOT memoizable (essentially every launched walk is a first sighting). The residue is arithmetic: 13x more arrivals AND 6.3x costlier arrivals. (archive: THE FLOW-NARROWING TAIL IS EXTREME)
- **A NARROWING ARM THAT ANSWERS A TYPE THE *DECLARATION* DOES NOT CONTAIN FLIPS EVERY JOIN IT REACHES ONTO `flowJoinUnion`'s QUADRATIC SUBTYPE REDUCTION, AND THAT COST IS PER-ARRIVAL, SO `cost_gate.py` IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO IT — measured 9.9 s of a 37.0 s check with `spine.nodes` bit-identical.** The only instruments are a bench row and `--narrowSections`. (archive: A NARROWING ARM THAT ANSWERS A TYPE)
- **tsc's UNION-ALIAS DISPLAY IS *IDENTITY PRESERVATION*, NOT STRUCTURAL MATCHING, AND INV.5(a) MAKES IT UNREACHABLE HERE BY CONSTRUCTION** — tsc keys its union cache by member list **plus alias**; round 545 interns ours by member-id list ALONE. **No id-keyed or member-set-keyed table can give three answers from one key** — do not attempt it in `unionAliasStructural` or `aliasDisplayMap`. (archive: tsc's UNION-ALIAS DISPLAY IS *IDENTITY PRESERVATION*, NOT)
- **A GENERIC ALIAS'S OWN TYPE PARAMETERS MUST BE IN SCOPE FOR ITS BODY — `type Box<T> = { v: T }` RENDERED `{ v: any; }` ON ORDINARY BUILDS** (its `T` answered `errorType`, and **`any` ABSORBS A UNION**, so the body vanished rather than degrading). **The fix is a forced SPLIT**: `getTypeOfSymbolWorker` answers the parametric form, `getDeclaredTypeOfSymbol` must NOT (two corpus false positives). `declaredTypes` has no write gate. (archive: A GENERIC ALIAS'S OWN TYPE PARAMETERS)
- **A FULL-vs-NARROW COMPARISON IS NOT A PIN WHEN BOTH ARMS AGREE ON THE *WRONG* ANSWER — the capture sweeps are differentials, so a defect in BOTH arms is invisible by construction.** A pin for a display defect must assert the VALUE; ground truth is obtainable, not guessable — `tools/tsgo-7.0.2/lib/tsc --lsp -stdio`. (archive: …AND A FULL-vs-NARROW COMPARISON IS)
- **A RECURSION BRAKE LIVES IN THE *ENCLOSING* DECLARATION, NOT THE REFERENCED ONE, AND ONLY A **FLIP CENSUS** (which decisions does the relaxation actually change?) NAMES THE MECHANISM** — the four flipped decisions were all NON-recursive aliases referenced from inside a self-referential one. **Census what a change FLIPS before deciding what a guard is for.** (archive: A RECURSION BRAKE LIVES IN THE *ENCLOSING*)
- **CACHING IN FRONT OF A RESOLUTION IS A CLOSED DIRECTION (the THIRD confirmation): the cacheable population is the CHEAP TAIL, so a keyed probe costs more than the work it avoids.** Priced three ways: the whole context-bypassed population is **68 ms = 0.35%**; widening the gate measures **+28% wall**; memoizing the fingerprint **+11.9%**; identity keying gets 4.1% hits. **tsc is not fast because it caches.** Measure the PRIZE first. Its fingerprint is also INCOMPLETE (1,269 shape-different serves under an identical key). (archive: CACHING IN FRONT OF A RESOLUTION IS)
- **CUTTING THE `getTypeOfExpression` RECOMPUTE FACTOR IS CLOSED — the x2.76 is REAL but the whole prize is 823 ms (2.9%) and the largest actionable handler-pair merge is 166 ms (0.58%).** It is 2.05x cross-handler x 1.34x recursion over 177 initiating sites, so no handler re-types alone. `typeOfExprNanos` DOUBLE-COUNTS (true cost 2,439 ms = 8.5% of checker-init), and **74.4% of the calls are OUTERMOST**. (archive: CUTTING THE `getTypeOfExpression` RECOMPUTE)
- **SKIPPING A *CACHED* RESOLUTION MOVES THE WORK, IT DOES NOT DELETE IT — so a SKIPPABLE population is not a RECOVERABLE one** (94.3% of calls skipped, four counters down 2.75-5.09%, yet the timed row fell only 109 ms of a 265 ms population and the downstream row rose by about as much). **Ask which of the skipped callees CACHE their result.** (archive: SKIPPING A *CACHED* RESOLUTION MOVES)
- **A WARM A/B IS MORE SENSITIVE IN ms AND *LESS* SENSITIVE IN PERCENT (round 788).** The warm rebuild is ~11.9 s against a ~27 s cold compile, so a saving whose code C2 has already compiled is a SMALLER fraction of the warm run — a change worth ~1.4% cold can read as ~0.5% warm and land inside the +-1.0% band. Quote the driver's own verdict line (round 788 got `NOISE-DOMINATED`: per-pair spread 270 ms against an 80 ms median delta, with both arm sds under 1%), never a median alone, and fall back to `cost_gate.py` counters — which is what the driver itself tells you to do.
- **POPULATION vs FREQUENCY — the arc's most expensive recurring error, now with three measured instances (round-758 audit of 57 claims, `docs/perf/claim-audit-round758.md`).** "N% of the nodes / N of the calls / N sightings" answers *how often a path is entered*, NEVER *how much work is behind it*, and here the two differ by 5–146×: `IDENTIFIER` is 44.5% of the nodes and **8.4%** of the spine's time (5.3×); `getCalleeType`'s "half its results are thrown away" is 50.6% of the calls and **8–10%** of the time (6.2×); 874 expression-bodied arrows held **six** block bodies (146×). So: **price the population before designing the fix; never multiply a count by a mean cost.** Corollary: a RESIDUAL (a time obtained by SUBTRACTION) is not a measurement of whatever you then name it — § 0's "dispatch + handler machinery, 42%" is the migrated passes' own checking work, and real dispatch is 100–300 ms.
- **BUT THE DIRECTION IS NOT ALWAYS "SMALLER", AND ASSUMING IT COST TWO ROUNDS THEIR PREDICTIONS (round 759, `docs/perf/claim-audit-round758.md` § 10).** "What you could skip cheaply is what was already cheap" **holds only when the exit predicate and the cost share a CAUSE** — a `getCalleeType` resolution is discarded *because* it failed fast. Inside `checkArgumentsAgainstSignature` it is FALSE: the 72% of arguments that never reach the assignability check carry **89%** of the argument-typing time (**22,604 ns vs 7,134 ns**), because the predicate reads the PARAMETER (`isSimpleCheckableType`, foreign TPs) while the cost belongs to the ARGUMENT, and complex parameters attract complex arguments. Rounds 758 and 759 both predicted "< 40%" from the prior and were out by ~2.5×. **Ask what SELECTS the population and what DRIVES its cost before invoking the law.** The CACHE form (line above, "stop proposing caches") is unaffected. Also from that round: *paying for a value is not wasting it* — the 89% is consumed by eleven downstream blocks, so a big share is not automatically a prize.
- **The flow-walk memo (round 664) is LIVE and DEPENDENCY-KEYED: key = (reference nodeId, fileHash, walkKind, inputId), value = the FlowGraph instance + the Type instances bound to the reference's ROOT NAME in currentLocalTypes / narrowedDeclaredTypes; it serves only while all three match.** Three invariants a change must preserve: a walk that TRIPPED (`flowDepthTripped`) is NEVER stored (its result is truncated and its TS2563 side effect must keep firing; a served hit runs no walk, so it consumes no visit budget and trips can only get rarer); the value is `Any?` + an unchecked cast, sound ONLY because `walkKind` is in the key (Boolean-returning `isAssignedAtFlow` must never share a key with the Type-returning walks); and a NEW `flowWalkWithTripCheck` call site must pass a `kind`, an `inputId` folding its starting type id with its path hash, and the path's ROOT NAME — omitting any of them silently mixes distinct walks into one entry (round 662 measured that collision as 165 wrong serves).
- **There are TWO narrowing memos at DIFFERENT scopes and they fail differently — the entry above is the INTER-walk one; `NarrowFlowMemo` is the INTRA-walk one, and round 736 found its serve condition was costing 631,585 recomputed flow-node arrivals per compiler-profile compile.** Its `served(id, depth)` guards ONE thing: a deeper entry has less budget before `NARROW_MAX_DEPTH` and might truncate where the stored computation did not. That is decidable, not approximable — `depth` reaches the result through no other channel, and only NON-truncated subtrees are ever stored — so an entry carries `hi` (the max depth its own subtree reached) and also serves when `depth + (hi - storedDepth) < maxDepth`. **THE NON-OBVIOUS INVARIANT: a served hit MUST fold `depth + memo.lastHitHeight` into the caller's `narrowWalkHiDepth`** — otherwise an ancestor records the memo shortcut's height instead of a fresh recomputation's and the disjunct goes unsound under nesting. Both walker mirrors carry it; `NarrowMemoDepthTest` + `IntKeyMapTest` pin it. Corollary for future rounds: when a cache measures as "cannot reach this population", check whether a DIFFERENT cache at a different scope is the one failing.
- **THE SEQUENTIAL SELF-COMPILE ANCHOR IS NOT STABLE ACROSS ROUNDS AT THE ~10% LEVEL, WITH IDENTICAL CODE AND AN IDENTICAL PROFILE — 23,183 (r826) / 25,299 (r823) / 26,145 (r824) / 26,518 ms (bench row, 2026-07-28) = a 12.8% spread, all four the same sequential path on the same 78-file profile.** Round 824 reconciled its seq arm with round 823's as "3.3% apart, well inside either arm's band"; the third point shows that was luck. **Only WITHIN-round paired deltas are quotable** — never compare an absolute ms figure to another round's.
- **A "SINGLE-THREADED" xtsc RUN CONSUMES ~4.17 OF THIS BOX'S 8 CORES (round 824; it was ~3.15 of 4 on the old box, round 740) — AND THAT TAX IS NOT A CONSTANT: IT SCALES WITH `nproc`, BECAUSE `CICompilerCountPerCPU` IS TRUE BY DEFAULT.** Doubling the cores took `CICompilerCount` 3 → 4 and `ParallelGCThreads` 4 → 8, and the JIT's CPU from ~21.7 s to ~43.8 s (`-XX:CICompilerCount=2` drops the run 4.20 → 2.55 cores). **So never express a core budget as "N cores NET of a fixed ~3.2"** — round 740's ">= 12 cores" unpark condition did exactly that and was miscalibrated in the conservative direction (the answer arrived at 8; free cores went 0.85 → 3.83, not → 4.85). Two consequences that did NOT change: **never run two compiles/benchmarks concurrently** (one copy already occupies half the box), and **this is not a single-thread lever** — self time is flat across baseline / JIT-starved / GC-starved (round 618's "JVM flag hunting is DEAD" stands; the JIT threads occupy cores a WORKER would want, they do not steal from the compile thread).
- **THE `--passTiming` PROBE LANDS ~100% INSIDE `checkSpine` AND NOWHERE ELSE, SO A `full`-TIER TABLE OVERSTATES THAT ONE ROW AND NOTHING ELSE — USE `--passTimingRows` (+0.25% cold, 0.0% warm).** A warm-up RATIO from a `full` table is compressed for that row (`checkSpine` really warms 3.46x, the tail 2.59x), and **the probe's OWN cost warms up**, so pricing it needs >=2 instrumented rebuilds per process. (archive: THE `--passTiming` PROBE LANDS ~100%)
- **A JVM THAT SHELLS OUT TO `jcmd` WITH *ITS OWN* PID IS ATTACHING TO ITSELF, AND IT HANGS — MEASURED 30 MINUTES, AFTER PRINTING A PLAUSIBLE *PARTIAL* TABLE AND THEN NOTHING (2026-08-25, the (INC.36) heap ladder).** It fails in the reassuring direction: a dead run reads exactly like a slow measurement. Take `GC.class_histogram`/`GC.heap_info` from an EXTERNAL helper process, asked through a file and with a HARD DEADLINE after which the measurement continues without it — `scripts/inc36-retention.sh` + `Inc36RetentionMain.histogram` are the reference pair, and a histogram is corroboration there, never the finding.
- **`jfr print` SILENTLY TRUNCATES EVERY STACK TO ITS TOP 5 FRAMES AND `scripts/aggregate_jfr.py` DOES NOT PASS `--stack-depth`, so its "inclusive time by method" table is inclusive time *within five frames of the leaf*** (`checkSpine` read 15.6% against the known ~74%). **Pass `--stack-depth 512` and assert the max stack length is below the cap.** LEAF numbers are unaffected but unstable across processes — attribute stdlib work to its nearest non-stdlib CALLER and filter to `xtsc-deep-stack`. (archive: `jfr print` SILENTLY TRUNCATES EVERY)
- A JFR leaf-frame self-% is NOT a wall-clock price (round 623): `computeLineStarts` showed 5.3% of samples; eliminating it (lazy) measured −0.3% = neutral — counted-loop safepoint bias inflates tight-loop self samples, and parallel-crawl worker savings don't move the serial-dominated wall. Price any JFR self entry by interleaved A/B before acting on it (the round-618 JFR shares this bias class).
- JVM GC/heap flag hunting on the self-compile: DEAD (round 618) — G1 vs ParallelGC vs +Xms4g vs +UseCompactObjectHeaders (JDK 25, 3 interleaved rotations) all land within the drift band (medians 30.2–30.8 s); the workload is not GC-bound at -Xmx4g. Perf levers are structural (docs/ARCHITECTURE-RETHINK.md), not flags.
- **DELETING THE LEGACY PASS TAIL FOR WALL TIME IS DEAD AS A LEVER — 417/440 tail passes emit, and 20 of the 23 census-silent ones are ALSO corpus-pinned; only 3 pure adders were deletable.** **Census-silence is deletion evidence ONLY for pure `diagnostics.add` passes** — wipe-and-pin walkers, rewriters, retractors and collectors are census-silent while load-bearing. A test touching fork-global PassLab state must SAVE-AND-RESTORE; `build/pass-lab.txt` is not a task input (force `--rerun`). (archive: Deleting the legacy pass tail for wall)
- Deleting the `checkGeneric*` pin-walker family as "superseded by the open generic gate": DEAD — 15/16 are still load-bearing (round 600 sweep: 21 corpus failures; their shapes are inference/constraint semantics the structural relation cannot decide). Only checkGenericFnTypeBipartition was deletable (its noStrictGenericChecks pin family is tsgo-skipped). Re-attempt a per-walker deletion only after generic INFERENCE (M3.1) lands, one walker at a time, suite-gated.
- A LIVE narrowing-walk fold at the two Type-returning entries (epoch-fenced + declaredType-identity + confirm-once): DEAD — gate-green but NOISE in the interleaved A/B (round 599; the confirm-once tax + 80k/111k fresh-epoch misses cap the served fraction); savableNanos measured 1.4s but unrealizable under this policy. Revive with INV.5 canonical types.
- **A LIVE PER-NODE `getTypeOfExpression` MEMO IS DEAD — gate-green but 1-3% SLOWER interleaved, and the whole servable population is worth 30 ms** (the servable calls are the CHEAP TAIL; the expensive ones are the non-instance-stable kinds the whitelist excludes). **Any estimate of a memo's value must price the SERVABLE population, never hits x a mean call cost** — that error inflated this by 35x. (archive: A LIVE per-node getTypeOfExpression)
- Global variance analysis wired into the relation engine: DEAD — ~263 regressions (round 336); strictFunctionTypes variance lives in dedicated walkers. Revisit only as part of a full M3.1 relation redesign.
- A general PropertyAccess-receiver TS2339 fallback via resolved interface members (B153): NOT viable — merged/cross-file interface member resolution is incomplete; only the class-AST-chain path + two tightly-gated exceptions (B527/B330) work.
- **`getTypeOfSymbol` PERSISTS INTO `symbolTypes` ONLY WHEN THE *CALLER'S* INSTANTIATION CONTEXT IS EMPTY** — without it a member typed by a type parameter froze globally as `T` or `any` depending on which file touched it first, invisible in every output diff until the crawl order changes. Read the gate at ENTRY. **Any NEW global id-keyed checker cache written during a resolution that consults ambient state needs the same gate.** (archive: `getTypeOfSymbol` PERSISTS INTO `symbolTypes` ONLY)
- **AN UNCONSTRAINED TYPE PARAMETER AND `any` ARE INDISTINGUISHABLE TO A READ PROBE — use a WRITE or an ARGUMENT probe (round 761).** `const s: string = x.v` is silent for both, which is how a whole queue item came to say "resolves to `any`" about a value that was really the unsubstituted `T`. `x.v = "s"` prints the actual target type; renaming each declaration's parameter (`TBOX`/`TFWD`) then names WHICH one survived, i.e. which hop failed.
- **AN "ACCEPTANCE-ONLY" RELATION LEG *CAN* ADD A DIAGNOSTIC, BECAUSE THE RELATION FEEDS NARROWING — a type that now relates gets SUBTRACTED and the reference collapses to `never`.** Round 784's "a second chance can only turn a rejection into an acceptance" is true of the RELATION and false of the COMPILER — price such a leg on the 8-profile grid, never on the argument. (archive: AN "ACCEPTANCE-ONLY" RELATION LEG *CAN*)
- **A TARGET TYPE'S MEMBER TABLE IS LAZY, SO ANY NEW READER OF `collectTargetPropertyNames` / `type.members` MUST `resolveStructuredTypeMembers(target)` FIRST — otherwise its verdict depends on whether an earlier line in the file happened to resolve that type** (the helper answers "bail" rather than failing). **A corpus baseline can MASK this exactly**, so only a hand-written pin whose fixture has ONE use of the target can see it. (archive: A TARGET TYPE'S MEMBER TABLE IS LAZY, SO)
- **`getPropertyOfType`'s UNION ARM ANSWERS AN ASSIGNABILITY QUESTION AND THEN RETURNS ONE CONSTITUENT'S SYMBOL, SO IT IS THE WRONG HELPER FOR EVERY "where/what is this member" QUESTION AND FAILS SILENTLY BOTH WAYS** (on one constituent -> null; on all N -> the first). Collect across `Union`/`Intersection` yourself; a PRIMITIVE's or bare type parameter's members need `getApparentType`, and a NAMESPACE's, MODULE's or ENUM's are on no type at all. (archive: `getPropertyOfType`'s UNION arm ANSWERS)

### Checker walker gotchas

The per-walker, per-diagnostic and per-fixture corpus of this section — 183 entries moved wholesale plus the full text of the 92 distilled below — now lives in `docs/history/CLAUDE-GOTCHAS-ARCHIVE.md` under `## Checker walker gotchas — moved from CLAUDE.md 2026-09-21`. **Nothing was deleted.** Moved wholesale: the per-TS-code rules, the per-walker receiver/relation/narrowing mechanisms, the Kotlin-externals and Kotlin/JS rendering rules, and the per-round instrument narratives. A distilled entry ends in `(archive: <key>)`; search it with `command grep -n '<key or walker|TSxxxx|fixture name>' docs/history/CLAUDE-GOTCHAS-ARCHIVE.md`. **GREP THE ARCHIVE FIRST** when you touch a named walker, a specific TS code, a corpus baseline or a receiver/narrowing shape — a missing entry here means "look in the archive", never "no such constraint exists".

- **UNWRAPPING `!` IS VALUE-PRESERVING FOR AN `inferReturnTypeFromBody` ARM THAT ANSWERS FROM *SYNTAX* AND WRONG FOR ONE THAT ANSWERS A *DECLARED TYPE* — FOR `return value!` STRIPPING THE NULLISH IS THE ENTIRE POINT (2026-09-22, (CHK.144)).** **And the 8-PROFILE GRID was the ONLY instrument that saw the 3 false positives this produced on tsc's own sources: the corpus screen read `0 of 8,725` and all 28 pins were GREEN on that same binary.** A return-inference arm is reached ~4,100-5,700 times per profile, so the grid is a real gate for this family and the screen is not — and an arm answering a declared type must decide the `!` policy explicitly and pin BOTH directions (a flow-narrowed `let` that only FLOW can prove non-nullish must still be refused).
- **A `currentLocalTypes` CONSULT GATED ON *OWNER IDENTITY* DOES NOT STOP A SCOPE LEAK, BECAUSE `getTypeOfArrowFunction`'s PUSH IS ITSELF CONDITIONAL ON THE TYPE BEING CONCRETE — so exactly the un-annotated parameter such a leg would serve is the one never pushed, and what it reads is the CALLER's binding of that name (2026-09-22, (CHK.144); measured `(r: any) => number` against tsgo's `any`, and the leg was removed rather than re-gated).** Resolve a name from the identifier's own PARENT CHAIN instead, with a shadow-stop that halts at every value-space binder — including ones you cannot type (function / class / enum / namespace / import), or the walk reaches past a shadow.
- **A CONTEXTUAL-`this` CARRIER IS DECIDED FROM THE FUNCTION EXPRESSION'S *IMMEDIATE* PARENT IN `spineItEdge`, AND THE THREE SHAPES tsgo STILL REPORTS FALL OUT OF THAT RATHER THAN NEEDING ARMS — an IIFE parents to the `CallExpression` (so the arm ABOVE decides it, answering false for a CALLEE) and a NESTED function expression parents to its own statement, so **an arm that walks ANCESTORS looking for an enclosing `=` silences all three** (2026-09-22, (CHK.35a); `thisBinding2` is the one active corpus baseline that would catch it).** Its sibling trap: **a type-keyed test there is inert wherever the target resolves to `any`**, which is why this one is syntactic — a contextual parameter type mentioning a FREE TYPE PARAMETER collapses to `any` ((CHK.35c)), so the `this:` a target really declares can be invisible to a test that asks the TYPE.
- **A FRAME HAS *TWO* TYPE-PARAMETER TABLES AND THEY ANSWER DIFFERENT QUESTIONS: `fnTpDecls` IS THE **AST** MAP (it is what emits TS2302) WHILE `fnTpScope`/`currentTypeParamScope` IS WHAT **TYPES A NAME** — FEEDING ONE AND NOT THE OTHER IS SILENT AND PRODUCES A *WRONG* TYPE, NOT AN `any` (2026-09-22, (CHK.140): a class type parameter that did not reach `fnTpScope` failed to SHADOW a file-level alias, so `type Q = boolean; class C<Q>` typed its body's `Q` as `boolean`; `any` is only the degenerate case where no outer name exists).** The tell is one binding answering two types in one body — the ccet and cta readers consult different tables — and no cache can do that. **And do NOT mirror `ccetEnterClassDeclaration`'s B74.5 exclusion of statics into the cta frame**: TS2302 comes from `fnTpDecls` and fires either way, so such a gate protects nothing and is LOSSY — tsgo reports TS2302 *and* types the reference as the parameter.
- **AN ELEMENT ACCESS'S KEY IS DECIDED BY THE INDEX *TYPE*, NOT ITS SYNTAX, AND THE READ AND WRITE ANSWERS DIFFER — A UNION OVER THE KEY'S LITERALS FOR A READ, THEIR **INTERSECTION** FOR A WRITE (2026-09-21, (CHK.139); `cheaKeyLiterals` is the one reader, and a SINGLETON literal counts).** ALL-OR-NOTHING is the binding constraint: an absent key makes the WHOLE access `any` in tsgo, so `getIndexedAccessType` must be delegated to PER LITERAL — **its own union arm is `.filter { it !== anyType }`, i.e. it DROPS an absent key**, which is the false-positive direction. **And `typeToString` parenthesizes an intersection member by its RENDERED FORM, never by its Type kind**: tsgo prints `A & U` for an alias-named union and `A & (B | C)` only for an anonymous one, so keying on `m is Type.Union` produces `A & (U)` and breaks display pins whose operands are union ALIASES — a change the corpus screen is structurally blind to ((PARITY.1)) and only the suite catches.
- **AN ASSIGNMENT THROUGH AN ELEMENT ACCESS IS DECIDED BY `getTypeOfElementAccess` ANSWERING A NON-`any` SLOT — THAT `anyType` *IS* THE FIREWALL — BUT AN **ARRAY LITERAL** AGAINST A TUPLE-BEARING SLOT MUST BE REFUSED OUTRIGHT (2026-09-21, (CHK.136)).** Round 459 recorded that this engine cannot decide array-literal → tuple (the Identifier site needs an AST-side helper and a declaration TYPE NODE this reader has not got); without the refusal tsc's own `builder.ts:1423` is an ours-only TS2322 on **all 8** profiles. **AND A GUARD NO ARM CAN DISCRIMINATE IS AS OFTEN *UNNECESSARY* AS IT IS UNPINNED — ASK WHAT IT COSTS**: the same round's UNION-RECEIVER refusal read 0 RED on 14 pins AND 0 of 8,725 on the screen because the ACCESSOR guard already reached every row of `divergentAccessorsTypes8` (`Two.prop3` is a get/set pair where `One.prop3` is a plain field, so the two were never the separable pair the sizing claimed), and measuring its COST showed it dropped two rows tsgo reports — so it was REMOVED rather than recorded as a redundant barrier. **And the four display/anchor divergences the new rows inherit are PRE-EXISTING AT THE *PROPERTY* TARGET, measured on a twin fixture** — tsgo re-anchors at the RHS with *Did you mean to call this expression?* when the RHS is callable and its RETURN relates, drills INTO a zero-parameter arrow's expression body, widens the literal SOURCE (`'boolean'` where we print `'true'`), and contextually types the RHS parameter (`(t: { type: string; })` where we print `(t: any)`). They are ONE shared family: fix them in both readers or neither.
- **THE KOTLIN-EXTERNALS / KOTLIN-JS RENDERING RULES ARE MEASURED TABLES AND THEY ARE *COMPILE-GATE* FACTS — a rule guessed from reading produces a declaration NO Kotlin compiler accepts, and the metadata gate CANNOT see it (it compiles the `external`-free variant).** Backtick package segments, `external class` ctor calls, override defaults, receiver function types: read the archive before designing any externals rendering rule. (archive: A BACKTICK RESCUES A KOTLIN *PACKAGE* SEGMENT)
- **`currentLocalTypes` IS A FLAT COPY OF THE ENCLOSING SCOPE, SO A PARAMETER NOTHING REGISTERS RESOLVES TO THE **OUTER** SAME-NAMED BINDING, NOT TO `any`** — every "we cannot type this parameter" exit needs the `ctaTypeParamsIntoLocals` PRE-PASS SEED, and a `currentParamBindingNames` registration is INERT for the cta-frame reader. (archive: AN EXPLICIT `: any` ANNOTATION WAS EXACTLY THAT UNTIL (P18.160))
- **TO FIND WHICH READER PRODUCED A ROW, RUN `--passTiming` OVER A MINIMAL SCRATCH PROJECT AND READ `emissions by pass`** — `checkSpine` means a spine handler under the cta frame, whose parameter seeder is `ctaTypeParamsIntoLocals`, not `populateParameterLocalTypes`. (archive: WHOSE PARAMETER SEEDER IS `ctaTypeParamsIntoLocals`)
- **A FIXTURE'S FILE-NAME SHAPE IS PART OF WHAT IT MEASURES — a specifier-RESOLUTION pin needs PATH-shaped `@Filename: /proj/src/…` names and a pin reproducing a CORPUS baseline needs FLAT ones.** When an arm reads 0 RED, re-shape the names before calling the guard redundant. (archive: A FIXTURE'S FILE-NAME SHAPE IS PART OF WHAT IT MEASURES)
- **A FLAT-NAMED FIXTURE MAKES EVERY MODULE-SPECIFIER PIN VACUOUS: `resolveModuleSpecifier` MATCHES `fileResults` *KEYS*, so `"./m"` matches `m.ts` by string alone and never reaches a directory-relative leg.** The 8 profiles and both libraries carry ZERO `import = require(` / `export = `. (archive: A FLAT-NAMED FIXTURE MAKES EVERY MODULE-SPECIFIER PIN VACUOUS)
- **AN `any`-ANNOTATED PARAMETER IS IN *NO* WALK-SCOPED TABLE (`populateParameterLocalTypes` SKIPS `any`), SO THE IDENTIFIER LADDER RESOLVES A NAME IT SHADOWS TO THE FILE-LEVEL DECLARATION** — invisible wherever that binding is also `any`. (archive: AN `any`-ANNOTATED PARAMETER IS IN *NO* WALK-SCOPED TABLE)
- **A PIN ASSERTING A *SILENCE* CAN BE SATISFIED BY A WRONGLY-RESOLVED TYPE THAT HAPPENS TO FIT, AND ITS ABLATION THEN READS 0 RED** — `const x: number = rel.num` is green on both binaries where `const x: string = …` discriminates. (archive: A PIN ASSERTING A *SILENCE* CAN BE SATISFIED BY A WRONGLY-RESOLVED TYPE)
- **NO GATED CORPUS HERE CONTAINS AN `export =` MODULE — 8 profiles, cronstrue and marked hold ZERO — SO A GREEN GRID ON ANY CommonJS-INTEROP CHANGE IS A CONTROL**; the ACTIVE corpus (40 such cases) plus the round's pins are the gate. (archive: NO GATED CORPUS IN THIS REPO CONTAINS AN `export =` MODULE)
- **A COPIED `*-grid.sh` REFUSES ON `Checker.class` BEING BYTE-IDENTICAL BETWEEN ITS ARMS, WHICH IS WRONG FOR ANY ROUND THAT EDITS A COLLABORATOR** (`NameResolver.kt`, `Relater.kt`, `MemberResolver.kt`, …) — point the identity check at the class that moved. (archive: A COPIED `*-grid.sh` REFUSES ON `Checker.class` BEING BYTE-IDENTICAL)
- **A PER-MEMBER `isTypeAssignableTo` OF TWO *FUNCTION* TYPES LOSES METHOD BIVARIANCE (`bivariantParams` reads both DECLARATIONS), SO A MEMBER-LEVEL VERDICT REPORTS LEGAL TYPESCRIPT** — ask the WHOLE-TYPE relation; the oracle is the same pair written as a VARIABLE DECLARATION. (archive: A PER-MEMBER `isTypeAssignableTo` OF TWO *FUNCTION* TYPES LOSES METHOD BIVARIANCE)
- **ROUND 807's "A SIGNAL WITH NO UNIQUELY-ITS-OWN FAILURE IS A REDUNDANT GUARD" HAS TWO MEASURED COUNTER-EXAMPLES; THE ESCALATION IS PINS -> SCREEN -> ASK WHAT SHAPE THE MECHANISM COULD BITE ON.** An undiscriminated arm is a statement about the INSTRUMENTS' POPULATION until screened. (archive: HAS *TWO* MEASURED COUNTER-EXAMPLES FROM ONE SESSION)
- **A REFUSAL HERE IS A MEASUREMENT TAKEN IN A *REGIME* AND A NEIGHBOURING ARC CAN DISSOLVE IT — WHEN A REFUSAL'S REASON NAMES A **COUNT** OF COLLATERAL BASELINES, RE-TAKE THE COUNT BEFORE INHERITING IT.** Record the number, not the verdict. (archive: A REFUSAL IN THIS REPO IS A MEASUREMENT TAKEN IN A *REGIME*)
- **A RECORDED REASON BUILT FROM A DIFF'S *FIRST DIFFERING LINE* DESCRIBES A SYMPTOM AND THEN READS AS A VERDICT ON THE MECHANISM — THREE TIMES IN ONE SESSION, ALL OVERSTATING THE WORK.** A sizing that calls a row MISSING must QUOTE the row. (archive: A RECORDED REASON BUILT FROM A DIFF'S *FIRST DIFFERING LINE*)
- **A BASELINE SERVED BY A WIPE-AND-PIN WALKER CANNOT BE CLOSED BY AN ENGINE RULE, SO A ROUND LANDING BOTH CREDITS THE ROW TO THE WRONG ONE UNLESS IT ABLATES THE **PIN** AND RE-SCREENS.** Such a ledger row is a RE-TRANSCRIPTION or a walker RETIREMENT, never an engine question. (archive: A BASELINE SERVED BY A WIPE-AND-PIN WALKER CANNOT BE CLOSED BY AN ENGINE RULE)
- **EVERY `*-grid.sh` COMPARES `grep 'error TS'` ROW SETS, i.e. HEAD LINES ONLY, SO THE 8-PROFILE GRID IS BLIND TO ANY CHANGE THAT ALTERS ONLY *CHAIN* LINES** — the eight profiles carry 0 chain lines between them; use a normalised FULL-capture diff. (archive: SO THE WHOLE 8-PROFILE GRID IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO ANY CHANGE THAT ALTERS ONLY *CHAIN* LINES)
- **AN EMIT FAMILY HAS EXACTLY ONE INSTRUMENT HERE, THE CORPUS *EMIT* CHANNEL — `--noEmit` SKIPS THE TRANSFORMER (round 738), SO THE GRID, `cost_gate.py` AND EVERY COUNTER ARE BLIND TO IT**; an `--outDir` run plus `diff -r` is the CONTROL, never the gate. (archive: AND AN EMIT FAMILY HAS EXACTLY ONE INSTRUMENT HERE)
- **A MAP BUILT BY SEVERAL COLLABORATING SITES IS DECIDED BY THE *LAST* WRITER, SO WIRING THE OBVIOUS SITE CAN CHANGE NOTHING AND READ EXACTLY LIKE A REFUTED HYPOTHESIS.** Enumerate every writer; when you cannot, put a marker in the SHARED consumer. (archive: A MAP BUILT BY SEVERAL COLLABORATING SITES IS DECIDED BY THE *LAST* WRITER)
- **TypeScript 7 GIVES A JS FUNCTION NO IMPLICIT `...any[]` REST AT ALL AND THIS COMPILER STILL CARRIES tsc-6 ASSUMPTIONS THAT IT DOES** — suspect any `...any[]` / `hasRest` / `bodyMentionsArguments` gate in the JS parameter path, and note **THE INSTRUMENT IS ARITY, NOT DISPLAY**. (archive: TypeScript 7 GIVES A JS FUNCTION NO IMPLICIT `...any[]` REST AT ALL)
- **A RETENTION MEASUREMENT MUST HOLD ITS SUBJECT IN A *SLOT*, NEVER A LOCAL: `local = null` IS DEAD CODE TO A WARNING-CLEAN BUILD, THE LOCAL KEEPS THE GRAPH REACHABLE, AND THE ARM READS `+0.0 MB`.** Check the arm's positive and release controls before believing a zero. (archive: A RETENTION MEASUREMENT MUST HOLD ITS SUBJECT IN A *SLOT*)
- **A `ForStatement`'s INITIALIZER IS A `VariableDeclarationList` WHOSE PARENT IS THE **LOOP**, NOT A `VariableStatement`, AND BOTH `spineArithLeaveNode` AND `spineCtaM3StatementAnchor` TEST THAT PARENT** — `for-of` and a plain `let` always worked, so a probe written either way reads as healthy. (archive: A `ForStatement`'s INITIALIZER IS A `VariableDeclarationList` WHOSE PARENT IS THE **LOOP**)
- **`spineIsJsLike`/`spineIsDts` ARE SET PER FILE *INSIDE* `checkSpine` AND NEVER CLEARED, SO AFTER THE SPINE THEY HOLD THE **LAST** FILE'S VALUE** — ask the FILE NAME (`isJsLikeFileName`) anywhere outside a spine handler. (archive: `spineIsJsLike`/`spineIsDts` ARE SET PER FILE *INSIDE* `checkSpine` AND NEVER CLEARED)
- **A STRUCTURAL / SPLIT PIN OFTEN PICKS A CONVENIENT *BEHAVIOURAL VEHICLE*, AND THE VEHICLE IS WHAT A SEMANTIC ROUND BREAKS.** Repair by RE-SELECTING the vehicle — ideally a shape the new rule REFUSES — never by weakening the assertion. (archive: A STRUCTURAL / SPLIT PIN OFTEN PICKS A CONVENIENT *BEHAVIOURAL VEHICLE*)
- **A PENDING ROW WHOSE RECORDED REASON NAMES A *MODEL GAP* MAY BE A *REACH* ROW — FIVE OF NINE DID NOT SURVIVE CONTACT.** The discriminator is one scratch run: rewrite the SOURCE union's order — if the output moves, the display reads NODES and the fix is a sort. (archive: A PENDING ROW WHOSE RECORDED REASON NAMES A *MODEL GAP* MAY BE A *REACH* ROW)
- **MORE THAN ONE EMITTER CAN OWN A DIAGNOSTIC CODE, SO A VALUE PIN CAN ASSERT THE RIGHT ANSWER FROM THE WRONG SITE — AND IT READS AS COVERAGE.** Only the ablation separates them; `emissions by pass` names which pass emitted a row. (archive: MORE THAN ONE EMITTER CAN OWN A DIAGNOSTIC CODE)
- **A TEST CLASS *NAMED* AFTER A PRISTINE DIVERGENCE IS ITSELF A COUNTDOWN AND, UNDER THE tsgo-ONLY DIRECTIVE, A PRE-DECLARED RED SET.** Before starting a family, grep the test SOURCES for `pristine` / `does not chase` / `divergence` / `residue`, and re-MEASURE each re-point against tsgo. (archive: A TEST CLASS *NAMED* AFTER A PRISTINE DIVERGENCE IS ITSELF A COUNTDOWN)
- **tsgo's `GetStrictOptionValue` IS `options.Strict != TSFalse`, SO EVERY STRICT SUB-OPTION DEFAULTS **ON** UNLESS `strict:false` IS EXPLICIT — *AND AN EXPLICIT SUB-OPTION `false` WINS*.** Spell it `X || (!options.XExplicitlyFalse && !options.strictExplicitlyFalse)`; 28 sites still carry the old idiom deliberately. (archive: tsgo's `GetStrictOptionValue` IS `options.Strict != TSFalse`)
- **A `.js` FILE IS IN THE PROGRAM UNDER `allowJs` AND *CHECKED* ONLY UNDER `checkJs`, AND A JS-FILE GATE KEYED ON ANYTHING ELSE IS INVISIBLE TO THE CORPUS — SUCH A FIXTURE HAS NO `.errors.txt` BASELINE AT ALL.** Any change to one needs an `allowJs`-without-`checkJs` pin. (archive: A `.js` FILE IS IN THE PROGRAM UNDER `allowJs` AND *CHECKED* ONLY UNDER `checkJs`)
- **`emitDeclarationOnly` TAKES THE `declarationOnly` WHITELIST PATH, WHERE `checkSpine` NEVER RUNS — SO A SPINE-FAMILY FIX IS SILENTLY INERT THERE, AND A RUN GATE ASSIGNED *INSIDE* `checkSpine` READS ITS DEFAULT.** `checkDeclarationOnlySpineFamilies` is the driver. (archive: `emitDeclarationOnly` TAKES THE `declarationOnly` WHITELIST PATH)
- **THE ACTIVE CORPUS RUNS AS A ~70-SECOND SCREEN *OUTSIDE* GRADLE OVER *BOTH* CHANNELS — `bash scripts/corpus-screen.sh`, with `--filter`, `--include` for `@Ignore`d rows and `--diff N`** — turning a round's blast radius into a measurement. **IT IS NOT THE GATE**: it sees no hand-written pin and its floors are PER CHANNEL. (archive: THE ACTIVE CORPUS RUNS AS A ~70-SECOND SCREEN *OUTSIDE* GRADLE)
- **A PassLab ABLATION RUN FROM A DIRECTORY OTHER THAN THE REPO ROOT READS A **DEAD LAB**, AND A DEAD LAB IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A REDUNDANT PASS** (`build/pass-lab.txt` loads from the process CWD) — run from the repo root with an ABSOLUTE project path and keep a control arm whose count MUST move. (archive: A PassLab ABLATION RUN FROM A DIRECTORY OTHER THAN THE REPO ROOT READS A **DEAD LAB**)
- **A PIN WALKER THAT LOCATES THE DIAGNOSTIC IT REWRITES BY ITS *ANCHOR POSITION* FAILS SILENTLY WHEN A GENERAL EMITTER MOVES THAT ANCHOR**, and the general wrong answer leaks through reading as a general-path defect; locate by the CALL's span. A pin walker is not deletable merely because the general path emits the same CODE. (archive: A PIN WALKER THAT LOCATES THE DIAGNOSTIC IT REWRITES BY ITS *ANCHOR POSITION*)
