- **tsc's STABLE TYPE ORDERING IS AN *INTERNING* ORDER, NOT A DISPLAY ONE (`addTypeToUnion` inserts by `binarySearch`), SO SORTING ONLY AT `typeToString` LEAVES EVERY OTHER READER ON THE UN-SORTED LIST.** TypeScript 7 reordered `TypeFlags` (`StableTypeOrdering.NEW_BIT`), and an interning-order change MOVES `typeNode.bypassed`. (archive: tsc's STABLE TYPE ORDERING IS AN *INTERNING* ORDER)
- **`currentLocalTypes` IS WRITTEN BY THE *LEGACY* STATEMENT-LIST WALK AND READ BY THE *SPINE* ANCHOR, WHICH RUNS LATER, SO A SAVE-AND-RESTORE AROUND A STATEMENT LIST CANNOT SCOPE ANYTHING IT RECORDS** — that boundary closes before both reads, and an "inert" result there is the expected one, not a broken instrument. (archive: `currentLocalTypes` IS WRITTEN BY THE *LEGACY* STATEMENT-LIST WALK)
- **A RECEIVER IS RESOLVED TWICE IN THIS CHECKER (`getTypeOfIdentifierCore` TYPES IT, THE `cmam*` FAMILY RE-RESOLVES FROM THE FILE-KEYED `lookupPerFileForNode`), SO A RULE ADDED TO ONE PRODUCES A CORRECT DIAGNOSTIC AND A FALSE ONE ON THE SAME LINE.** The `?:` ORDER is the rule, not a detail. (archive: A RECEIVER IS RESOLVED TWICE IN THIS CHECKER)
- **A HERITAGE BASE IS RESOLVED AT *THREE* SITES AND `NameResolver.resolveHeritageBaseSymbol` IS THE ONE THAT DECIDES THE LEAST** — members come from `getTypeFromBaseTypeExpression` and the `implements` verdict from `checkImplementsClauses`' own probe. Land a name-resolution fix, then MEASURE IT INERT before looking for the next resolver. (archive: A HERITAGE BASE IS RESOLVED AT *THREE* SITES)
- **A BARE TYPE NAME IS RESOLVED AT *THREE* SITES, NOT ONE, AND A CONSULT ADDED TO THE OBVIOUS ONE IS ABSENT FROM THE OTHER TWO** — the failure is a name resolving to the OUTER declaration, silent in every diagnostic channel here. (archive: A BARE TYPE NAME IS RESOLVED AT *THREE* SITES, NOT ONE)
- **MAKING A B83.5 NAME RESOLVE IN *VALUE* POSITION COSTS 19-20 OURS-ONLY ROWS ON EVERY PROFILE, SO THE SHIPPED RULE IS OVERRIDE-ONLY — REPLACE A CONVENTIONAL ANSWER, NEVER SILENCE.** Before making any silent name real, price it on the GRID, not on a fixture matrix (9 cells fixed vs +19 per profile, same build). (archive: MAKING A B83.5 NAME RESOLVE IN *VALUE* POSITION IS CORRECT AND COSTS)
- **`true` AND `false` ARE RESERVED WORDS THE PARSER RENDERS AS AN `Identifier`, SO A BOOLEAN LITERAL REACHES THE `Identifier` ARM OF EVERY EXPRESSION/CALLEE DISPATCHER AND RESOLVES TO NOTHING** — invisible next to `(1)()` and `("x")()`, which go through their own node classes. (archive: `true` AND `false` ARE RESERVED WORDS THE PARSER RENDERS AS AN `Identifier`)
- **A CORPUS BASELINE SERVED BY A *WIPE-AND-PIN* WALKER CANNOT CONTRADICT A HAND-WRITTEN PIN — THE TWO CAN ASSERT DIFFERENT ANSWERS INDEFINITELY WITH BOTH GREEN.** Before trusting a hand-written pin as the reference for a shape, check whether a `pinDiag`/wipe walker owns that file, and read the baseline itself. (archive: A CORPUS BASELINE SERVED BY A *WIPE-AND-PIN* WALKER CANNOT CONTRADICT A HAND-WRITTEN PIN)
- **A BUDGETED "COULD THIS BE LEGAL?" CONSULT MUST ANSWER *DIFFERENTLY* AT A NARROW ROUTE AND AT AN EMISSION FUNNEL, AND THE DIRECTION IS MEASURED RATHER THAN ARGUED** — at a funnel pass `refuseOnExhaustion = false`, so the change suppresses only on a POSITIVE finding. (archive: A BUDGETED "COULD THIS BE LEGAL?" CONSULT MUST ANSWER *DIFFERENTLY*)
- **A `Checker.class` NEWER THAN ITS SOURCE PROVES THE *BUILD* LANDED, NEVER THAT THE *ROUND* IS FINISHED** — an interrupted session's `grid-after` can be older than its own final fix. Compare every receipt's mtime against the SOURCE's, not against the previous receipt. (archive: A `Checker.class` NEWER THAN ITS SOURCE PROVES THE *BUILD* LANDED)
- **A `Type` REACHED THROUGH `getTypeFromTypeNodeBypassed` IS *SHARED* VIA INV.5(c)'s `state.mappedNodeTypes`, SO MUTATING IT IN PLACE FREEZES IT FOR EVERY OTHER INSTANTIATION** — MINT, never mutate, and clone a signature's own type parameters when the outer mapper moves a constraint. The gate is a BOTH-ORDERS pin. (archive: A `Type` REACHED THROUGH `getTypeFromTypeNodeBypassed` IS *SHARED*)
- **A PROBE WHOSE TARGET IS A *UNION* SAYS NOTHING ABOUT THE NON-UNION OBJECT CASE — `canUseTypeEngine` ADMITS "Anything → Union target" BY ITS OWN LINE.** Vary the TARGET's shape (union vs named object) before locating an assignability gap at a position. (archive: A PROBE WHOSE TARGET IS A *UNION* SAYS NOTHING ABOUT THE NON-UNION OBJECT CASE)
- **A NARROWING PROBE FOR AN ENUM-MEMBER LOCAL MUST TARGET A SAME-FLAVOUR LITERAL *MEMBER* (`const w: K.B = k`, `takeB(k)`, `k === K.B`) — THE PRIMITIVE MIS-ASSIGNMENT `const p: string = k` PRINTS `Type 'K'` ON BOTH SIDES, because tsc's `reportRelationError` generalizes `K.A` to `K` at a target that cannot hold a singleton and so does ours (2026-09-05, (CHK.85)(b) recon, 49 rows).** The "target a PRIMITIVE" rule above is for a union of primitives; an enum member is the one literal kind it blinds.
- **THE 8-PROFILE GRID IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO EVERY *TYPE-DISPLAY* CHANGE: EVERY ROW OF EVERY PROFILE IS `Cannot find name …`, SO NOT ONE NAMES A TYPE AT ALL.** The only instrument for a display rule is the ACTIVE `.errors.txt` corpus subtests — and since the corpus is GREEN, a display change can only turn one RED, never fix one. (archive: THE 8-PROFILE GRID IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO EVERY *TYPE-DISPLAY* CHANGE)
- **THERE ARE *FOUR* WAYS A LOCAL CAN SHADOW A SAME-NAMED OUTER BINDING AND A SHADOW REDUCTION MUST SEPARATE ALL FOUR** — top-level decls, two decls in ONE body, a GLOBAL/file-level collision, and a block-scoped declaration inside a NESTED function shadowing an enclosing function's local. Three are silent. (archive: THERE ARE *FOUR* WAYS A LOCAL CAN SHADOW A SAME-NAMED OUTER BINDING)
- **`resolveFlowCalleeDecl` DOES NO OVERLOAD SELECTION (`valueDeclaration ?: declarations.firstOrNull()`), SO EVERY CONSUMER READING A *RETURN ANNOTATION* OFF IT ANSWERS ABOUT THE **FIRST** SIGNATURE** — a DIRECT read of the call is right while a local INFERRED from it is wrong, so a direct-call fixture is vacuous for this class. (archive: `resolveFlowCalleeDecl` DOES NO OVERLOAD SELECTION)
- **`CtaFrame.narrowedDeclared` IS *SHARED* DOWN THE FRAME CHAIN AND HAS NO UNDO LOG, SO A WRITE INTO IT ESCAPES THE BLOCK, THE FUNCTION **AND THE FILE**.** A new writer must take its own copy first and write FIRST-WINS; `localTypes` is undo-logged only for a frame whose `localScoped` is true. (archive: `CtaFrame.narrowedDeclared` IS *SHARED* DOWN THE FRAME CHAIN)
- **A NARROWING PIN WHOSE SOURCE IS A *NULLISH* UNION AND WHOSE TARGET IS A *PRIMITIVE* IS VACUOUS IN BOTH ARMS — `canUseTypeEngine` refuses that pair wholesale ((CHK.63)), so the un-narrowed read is silent too (2026-08-28).** The round's own inherited repro (`build/chk61b/n1`) was silent on our compiler AND on tsc for exactly this reason; a NON-NULLISH union (`number | string` narrowed by `typeof`) makes the same gaps appear on the shipped binary with no patch. Corollary: truthiness narrowing only ever removes `null`/`undefined`, so the `!`-guard family is *structurally* unobservable at those readers until that gate opens.
- **`typeofTypeGuardToType` ANSWERS `anyType` FOR `"object"` AND `"function"`, SO A `typeof x === "object"` "NARROWING" IS A ***WIDENING*** — AND `any` IS ASSIGNABLE TO EVERYTHING, SO INSTALLING IT DELETES TRUE POSITIVES SILENTLY.** Any new consumer of `extractNullNarrowing` must drop an `anyType` answer. (archive: `typeofTypeGuardToType` ANSWERS `anyType` FOR `"object"` AND `"function"`)
- **A HANDED-DOWN DIAGNOSIS NAMES AN AXIS THE REPRO HAPPENED TO HAVE — 2 OF 4 WERE WRONG IN ONE ROUND, BOTH TOWARD A PLAUSIBLE-BUT-INNOCENT FEATURE. RE-BISECT BEFORE DESIGNING THE FIX**, and when a narrowing or relation diagnosis names a SYNTACTIC shape, vary the participating types' OPTIONALITY before believing it. Add a rule to BOTH halves of a filter/emitter pair. (archive: A HANDED-DOWN DIAGNOSIS NAMES AN AXIS THE REPRO HAPPENED TO HAVE)
- **tsc's WEAK-TYPE CHECK LIVES INSIDE `isRelatedTo` AND OURS LIVES IN THE B482 WALKERS, SO ANY DECISION PORTED FROM tsc THAT ASKS "IS X RELATED TO Y" MUST ADD `weakParamRefusesArg` BY HAND** — `checkTypeRelatedTo` alone answers "assignable" for EVERY non-nullish source against an all-optional target. (archive: tsc's WEAK-TYPE CHECK LIVES INSIDE `isRelatedTo` AND OURS LIVES IN THE B482 WALKERS)
- **OVERLOAD SELECTION (`signatureAcceptsArgs`) AND THE TS2769 MATCHER (`allArgumentsMatch`) MUST ASK THE SAME QUESTION, AND WHEN THEY DIVERGE THE TWO SYMPTOMS LOOK LIKE UNRELATED DEFECTS** (a wrong type with no diagnostic; a false TS2769). Add any new argument-acceptance rule to BOTH. (archive: OVERLOAD SELECTION (`signatureAcceptsArgs`) AND THE TS2769 MATCHER)
- **`resolveCallOverload` FALLS BACK TO `arityMatches[0]` WHEN NOTHING ACCEPTS, SO A PIN ABOUT A *REFUSAL* IS VACUOUS WHENEVER THE REFUSED OVERLOAD IS DECLARED **FIRST**.** Declare the overload under test SECOND, so a wrong refusal picks the other one and a write probe moves. (archive: `resolveCallOverload` FALLS BACK TO `arityMatches[0]` WHEN NOTHING ACCEPTS)
- **A STRUCTURAL RELATION AGAINST A *MEMBER-LESS* `Type.Object` PASSES VACUOUSLY, SO ANY NEW LEG THAT *ACCEPTS* AGAINST AN OBJECT TARGET MUST DEMAND THE TARGET DECLARE SOMETHING** — a members/signatures/index-infos test taken BELOW `resolveStructuredTypeMembers`, since both tables are lazy. (archive: A STRUCTURAL RELATION AGAINST A *MEMBER-LESS* `Type.Object` PASSES VACUOUSLY)
- **A RECEIVER THAT REPORTS NOTHING IS USUALLY TYPED CORRECTLY — THE PROPERTY-ACCESS FAMILY HAS ITS OWN CONSUMERS AND THEY BAIL ON `anyType` PER RECEIVER SHAPE.** Probe the TYPE (a write probe) before reading a missing diagnostic as a typing gap. (archive: A RECEIVER THAT REPORTS NOTHING IS USUALLY TYPED CORRECTLY)
- **A UNION TS2339 HAS TWO VERDICTS: *PARTIAL* COVERAGE CARRIES ITS OWN WITNESS, *ALL-MISSING* HAS NONE AND NEEDS A PER-MEMBER TRUST GATE.** Removing that gate is `added=0 removed=0` on all eight profiles AND moves ZERO corpus baselines and still costs false positives on a real library — read a green grid+corpus there as a control. (archive: A UNION TS2339 HAS TWO VERDICTS WITH DIFFERENT SOUNDNESS REQUIREMENTS)
- **BEFORE ATTRIBUTING A RECEIVER-SHAPED GAP TO *WHERE THE RECEIVER IS DECLARED*, RUN THE IDENTICAL SOURCE AT ALL THREE SITES — parameter, file-level `const`, body-local — BECAUSE THREE CONSECUTIVE ROUNDS DID NOT AND ALL THREE MIS-ATTRIBUTED IT.** (archive: BEFORE ATTRIBUTING A RECEIVER-SHAPED GAP TO *WHERE THE RECEIVER IS DECLARED*)
- **A `getTypeOfSymbol` CALL MADE IN ORDER TO *WARM* A CACHE IS A NO-OP WHEREVER THE AMBIENT INSTANTIATION CONTEXT IS NON-EMPTY (round 778's write gate)** — the resolution succeeds and is thrown away, and only a hover moves. A symbol a materializer MINTS must have its type written AT MINT TIME, ungated. (archive: A `getTypeOfSymbol` CALL MADE IN ORDER TO *WARM* A CACHE IS A NO-OP)
- **A STATEMENT NESTED IN A FUNCTION BODY IS EMISSION-OWNED BY THE *LEGACY* WALK — THE SPINE'S ANCHOR RUNS `recordOnly` AND TRUNCATES EVERY DIAGNOSTIC — SO A TYPE WRITTEN ONLY INTO THE SPINE FRAME IS CORRECT AND COMPLETELY INVISIBLE.** Such a fix needs BOTH `checkFunctionBody` and the frame. (archive: A STATEMENT NESTED IN A FUNCTION BODY IS EMISSION-OWNED BY THE *LEGACY* WALK)
- **NARROWABILITY IS DECIDED PER CONSUMER, NOT BY THE MACHINERY — every flow-reading call site carries its own NODE-KIND gate, so a kind the whole narrowing stack supports can still read the declared type everywhere.** Never conclude "this shape is not narrowable" from the shape: ask whether the READ happened. (archive: NARROWABILITY IS DECIDED PER CONSUMER, NOT BY THE MACHINERY)
- **NEVER GATE AN EMISSION ON A `@ts-*` COMMENT; EMIT, AND LET THE FUNNEL DROP IT** — the directives are filtered once, at `Checker.getDiagnostics()`, so a suppression written at an EMITTER marks nothing and the directive reads as unused (a false TS2578). Both halves of that funnel are scoped to `checkedResultsAll`, not the program. (archive: A SUPPRESSION WRITTEN AT AN *EMITTER* IS INVISIBLE TO THE DIRECTIVE ACCOUNTING)
- **AN ABLATION THAT RESTORES THE *SOURCE* LEAVES THE *CLASS DIR* HOLDING THE LAST ARM, AND THE NEXT THING YOU RUN MEASURES THAT ARM — A REPRODUCIBLE "FRESH DEFECT" IN CODE THAT IS FINE.** `git status` is clean, so every standard check passes; rebuild in the driver and verify with `javap -p -cp <class dir>`. (archive: AN ABLATION THAT RESTORES THE *SOURCE* LEAVES THE *CLASS DIR* HOLDING THE LAST ARM)
- **EVERY GATE IN THIS REPO IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO ANYTHING DECIDED BY A FILE THAT IS NOT A PROGRAM INPUT** — the 8 profiles hold ZERO `package.json` and the corpus harness materialises NO DIRECTORY (2,478 FPs on one library from one absent lookup). **The instrument for that class is a `-project` fixture through `ProjectCompiler` + a `Vfs`**; a `diagnose()` pin is vacuous by construction. (archive: THE EIGHT DASHBOARD PROFILES HOLD **ZERO** `package.json` FILES)
- **A FLOW-NARROWED REFERENCE READ IN AN *OBJECT LITERAL* AND READ AT A *VAR DECL* ARE TWO DIFFERENT INSTRUMENTS, AND THE WRITE PROBE CLAUDE.md RECOMMENDS IS THE **BLIND** ONE FOR AN ASSIGNMENT NARROW (2026-08-27, (CHK.62b)).** At one flow point `const q: T = p` was SILENT on a broken binary while `return { p }` was a TS2322 — so a pin written as a write probe measures nothing. The instrument that names the TYPE is a deliberately WRONG object-literal target (`return { p }` against `{ p: string }`): the message then prints the member type the checker actually built (`{ p: T | undefined; }` vs `{ p: T; }`), which is a VALUE pin and not an absence pin.
- **A DASHBOARD-PROFILE ZERO BOUNDS A HAZARD'S FREQUENCY, NEVER ITS EXISTENCE — THE 13k-BASELINE CORPUS IS THE ONLY INSTRUMENT THAT SEES A SHAPE THE PROFILES DO NOT CONTAIN.** A pre-gate measuring 0 emitting calls in a 22,187-call skip set still killed 7 corpus baselines; any new suppression keyed on "the property resolves" inherits both causes. (archive: A DASHBOARD-PROFILE ZERO BOUNDS A HAZARD'S FREQUENCY, NEVER ITS EXISTENCE)
- **A TS2322/TS2345 MESSAGE NAMES THE DECLARED TYPE, NOT THE FLOW TYPE, AND ITS ELABORATION CAN NAME A CONSTITUENT THE RELATION NEVER REJECTED — SO REDUCING FROM THE MESSAGE IS HOW A REAL DEFECT GETS THREE WRONG DIAGNOSES IN A ROW.** Probe the actual flow type with a deliberate mis-assignment, and build the model from the REAL declarations, deleting ingredients one at a time. (archive: A TS2322/TS2345 message names the DECLARED type, not the flow type)
- **TO FIND OUT WHAT A PASS IS REALLY DOING, ABLATE IT WITH THE PassLab INSTEAD OF DELETING IT: write `build/pass-lab.txt` with `disable <passName>` and re-run — ZERO recompile.** Remove the file afterwards; it silently applies to every later run from that directory. (archive: To find out what a pass is really doing, ABLATE it with the PassLab)
- **THERE IS NO FRESH-LITERAL EXPRESSION TYPE: `getTypeOfExpression` ANSWERS THE BASE PRIMITIVE FOR A LITERAL NODE, AND A `Type.StringLiteral` IS MINTED ONLY BY THE AST-BASED `literalTypeOfExpression`.** Any rule needing a literal type must read the AST; a name's type at every read site comes from `currentLocalTypes`, which is where a per-declaration typing rule belongs. (archive: THERE IS NO FRESH-LITERAL EXPRESSION TYPE)
- **`getTypeOfExpression` NEVER FLOW-NARROWS — NARROWING IS OPT-IN PER EMISSION SITE VIA `getNarrowedTypeForReference`.** A new rule that reads an argument/operand type and means to respect narrowing must call it itself; prefer a SECOND CHANCE on the rejecting path, which can only turn a rejection into an acceptance. (archive: `getTypeOfExpression` NEVER flow-narrows)
- **AN ARITY MUST COME FROM `Checker.signatureDeclaredArity` WHEREVER `minArgumentCount` IS READ, AND THE FIXTURE NEEDS A *UNION* RECEIVER OR IT IS VACUOUS (2026-09-21, (CHK.33)).** A MAXIMUM from `parameters.size` beside a MINIMUM from `minArgumentCount` states an IMPOSSIBLE range — `Expected 1-0 arguments, but got 1.` on legal code, 8 rows in `marked` — and round 446 fixed exactly that at the property-access reader while (CHK.97)'s union reader reintroduced it, because the SAME method on a plain receiver is already correct. Two traps beside it: a REST parameter whose own name is a binding pattern is dropped too, so `sigHasRestParameter` (it reads the list's LAST entry) answers FALSE and the signature reads as ZERO-arity; and **`require(minArgumentCount <= parameters.size)` is UNAVAILABLE as a constructor assertion** — every binding-pattern signature violates it by construction — so pin the inversion as a MESSAGE invariant over a whole diagnostic list instead.
- **`Signature.parameters` IS NOT THE DECLARATION'S PARAMETER LIST — `getParameterSymbols` DROPS EVERY BINDING-PATTERN PARAMETER AND THE BUILDER ZIPS ANNOTATIONS ONTO THE SURVIVORS **POSITIONALLY**.** Anything rendering, counting or aligning from `sig.parameters` is one short AND shifted; read `sig.declaration`'s own list when it is longer. (archive: `Signature.parameters` IS NOT THE DECLARATION'S PARAMETER LIST)
- **A NARROWING PROBE IN CALL-ARGUMENT POSITION READS THE DECLARED TYPE, NOT THE FLOW TYPE — use an ASSIGNMENT (round 777, five pins measured inert).** Flow narrowing is opt-in per emission site and the argument gate performs the read only on its special arms: a `never` parameter (rounds 441/462/766) and a bare-enum argument (round 764). So `declare function take(s: string); take(x)` after a guard chain reports the DECLARED type on a WORKING build — five pins written that way produced byte-identical output on a fixed and an ablated binary. `const probe: string = x` (TS2322) reads the real flow type; `assertNever(x)` works precisely because `never` is one of those arms.
- **…BUT A PRIMITIVE TARGET IS INERT AT THE RETURN PATH, WHICH GATES ITS WHOLE NARROWING BLOCK TO AN OBJECT-ISH/UNION TARGET (round 784).** `checkReturnAssignabilityCore` only attempts flow narrowing when `targetType` is `Type.Interface`/`Reference`/`Object`/`Union`/`Intersection`, so `function f(): string { … return x.p }` reports the UN-narrowed type on a fully working build — the probe reads as "narrowing is broken" when nothing is. Use a UNION target (`string | boolean`) there. General rule: **a probe shape validated at one site is not portable to another site's gate — re-validate it against a control whose answer you already know.**
- **A TS2339 FIXTURE IS VACUOUS UNLESS ITS RECEIVER IS A UNION AND THE MEMBER IS ON *SOME* CONSTITUENT** — `c.nope` on neither is silent for a block-scoped local whatever the compiler does, and `A | undefined` plus an EXISTING member reports TS18048, not TS2339. Run a control probe per NEGATIVE shape before recording a refusal as pinned. (archive: A TS2339 FIXTURE IS VACUOUS UNLESS ITS RECEIVER IS A UNION)
- **THE AXIS IS *DECLARED IN A BLOCK*, NEVER local-vs-parameter: A FILE-LEVEL `const`/`let` IS CHECKED AND ALWAYS WAS** (B83.5 is the cause). **A resolution experiment must not name its subject after a lib global** — the probe that said otherwise named its receiver `top`, and three follow-ups inherited that one mistake. (archive: AND THE AXIS IS *DECLARED IN A BLOCK*, NEVER local-vs-parameter)
- **A MODULE-LOCAL `interface` WHOSE NAME MATCHES A LIB GLOBAL IS **MERGED INTO** THE LIB SYMBOL, BOTH WAYS** (the second direction — answering the lib's members — is the dangerous one). **Known colliders to keep out of a fixture: `top`, `name`, `files`, `Text`, `Event`, `Body`.** (archive: A MODULE-LOCAL `interface` WHOSE NAME MATCHES A LIB GLOBAL IS **MERGED INTO** THE LIB SYMBOL)
- **A "DOES THE RECEIVER HAVE THIS MEMBER" PIN IS VACUOUS FOR EVERY RECEIVER WITH AN `extends` — THE AXIS IS **HERITAGE**, NOT "LIB".** Still silent: a program interface with heritage, a class instance with a base, any numeric-index receiver, a bare function type. Census the shape ONE CASE PER FILE before writing such a pin. (archive: A "DOES THE RECEIVER HAVE THIS MEMBER" PIN IS VACUOUS FOR EVERY RECEIVER WITH AN `extends`)
- **A NULLISH UNION SOURCE IS REFUSED AGAINST A *PRIMITIVE* TARGET BY `canUseTypeEngine`, SO `T | undefined` IS SILENTLY ASSIGNABLE TO `T` AT A DECLARATION, AN ASSIGNMENT AND A RETURN — AND ANY CHANGE THAT *ADDS* `| undefined` TO A TYPE THEREFORE **DELETES** DIAGNOSTICS RATHER THAN ADDING THEM (2026-08-27, (CHK.61)(b)/(CHK.63)).** ARGUMENT position and a UNION target are the two that still fire, which is why a probe written either way reads as working. Opening the gate is 11 ours-only rows on the 8 profiles, all flow-narrowing gaps in the ASSIGNMENT/RETURN readers ((CHK.64)) — so the gate and the narrowing must move together, in that order.
- **AND A NARROWING GAP THAT LOOKS OPTIONALITY-DEPENDENT REPRODUCES WITH AN *EXPLICIT* `| undefined` MEMBER — NO PATCH, NO BUILD (same round).** Declare `p: number | undefined` instead of `p?: number` and the shipped binary shows the same five gaps the queue had recorded as reachable only under a probe patch. The mirror rule: the FLOW WALK handles `&&` correctly and the LEGACY if-arm helper (`extractNullNarrowing`, one `(name, type)` pair) does not, so **what varies across "does `&&` narrow here" fixtures is the READER, not the condition** — check a member access and an argument before concluding narrowing is broken.
- **THE CALL-ARGUMENT READER IS LIVE ONLY FOR A **PARAMETER** SOURCE, SO A NARROWING FIXTURE GRADED THROUGH `take(localVar)` READS ZERO ROWS ON A BROKEN *AND* A FIXED BINARY — AND A NULLISH-UNION ONE IS VACUOUS A SECOND TIME, BECAUSE IT SITS UNDER (CHK.63)'s OWN `canUseTypeEngine` GATE (2026-08-28).** `const r: string | number = mk(); take(r)` is silent here and TS2345 in tsc; only `function f(r: string | number) { take(r) }` reports. The instrument that always works is a deliberate MIS-ASSIGNMENT (`const p: boolean = r`), which PRINTS the flow type in its message; grade a loop/flow change on that, over `string | number` rather than `T | undefined`.
- **A SUPPRESSION-ONLY SUBSTITUTION MUST REFUSE A `never` ANSWER, BECAUSE `never` RELATES TO EVERYTHING AND AN UNREACHABLE NODE'S FLOW TYPE *IS* `never` — SO ADOPTING IT DELETES THE DIAGNOSTIC.** Every reader that narrows a source "only when it makes the assignment relate" inherits it. (archive: A SUPPRESSION-ONLY SUBSTITUTION MUST REFUSE A `never` ANSWER)
- **A NARROWING probe must target a PRIMITIVE, never an object/branded type (round 762; round 760 lost an arc to the same class of error).** Every enum-flavored type is a member-LESS `Type.Object`, so `K`, `K.A` and a washed `never` are ALL silently assignable to any object-shaped probe target — silence conflates "narrowed", "not narrowed" and "collapsed". `declare function probe(x: string)` (or `number`) fires for all of them and NAMES the narrowed type in the message. Corollary: `const y: K.A|K.B = x` cannot detect a widened `x: K` either, because enum → MEMBER relates vacuously — that is (REL.2)'s leniency, so such a pin can never show the positive.
- **A per-file checker PASS loop iterates `checkedResults` (the INV.6 partition view); a COLLECTOR loop (populates cross-file maps/sets, no `diagnostics.add`) MUST iterate `binderResults`** — a gated collector starves partition workers of program-wide suppression context (round 609: 1,174 TS2339 partition FPs). The `--partitionCheck 2` harness on the 8 profiles is the gate for any new pass loop's choice; the sequential path is identical either way.
- **Symbol/Type id sequences are THREAD-LOCAL (INV.6(6c0)) and `runWithDeepStack` hands them off: capture the caller's counters, seed the compile thread, WRITE THE ADVANCED VALUES BACK on join.** Removing the write-back restarts ids at 1 and collides with the singleton intrinsics — 51 corpus failures whose `--listAll` output stays IDENTICAL. A parallel worker rebases instead; ids must never cross a worker boundary. (archive: Symbol/Type id sequences are THREAD-LOCAL (INV.6(6c0), round 607))
- **Any NEW full-tree expression walker MUST handle `BinaryExpression` iteratively (worklist/right-spine), not recursively** — `binderBinaryExpressionStress` is a deeply-nested `a+b+c+…` chain that StackOverflows a naive `walk(left); walk(right)`, and a crash mid-check ABORTS JS emit, so the regression shows up as a JS-EMIT output diff (NOT in the `find_candidates` EXTRA bucket — only the failing-set diff catches it). B98b's `walkThisTypeExpr` learned this the hard way. Reference iterative patterns: B98b's worklist (`ArrayDeque` frontier) and `getTypeOfBinaryExpression`'s left-spine fold; the old right-spine reference `checkArgCountInExprCore` is deleted (round 535 — its depth-cap geometry lives on in `spineArgDepth`/`spineArgEdge`).
- **Do NOT add ANY `try/catch(StackOverflowError)` in the checker — fix the recursion instead.** The checker's overflows are eliminated (0/suite-run): cycles are guarded at the resolvers by in-progress sentinels (`symbolTypeResolutionInProgress`/`nodeTypeResolutionInProgress`/`memberResolutionInProgress`/`relationComparisonStack`/`functionElaborationStack`) plus `collectInheritedPropertyNames`' `visited` set, and the full-tree binary walkers iterate. A new overflow means a missing guard or a recursive walk that should be iterative — add the guard / iterate, don't swallow. All ~213 inline catches were REMOVED (2026-07-02); **the ONLY remaining catch is the `init` boundary guard (`reportCheckerStackOverflow` → TS2589), and it is LOAD-BEARING — do NOT remove it** (a "zero catches" attempt let a raw SOE escape to the test harness, and the guard had been silently absorbing a left-spine recursion whose TS2589 was invisible in a js-only subtest). A guardless full run doubles as the corpus overflow census (1 crash = 1 overflow site); ~218 `is BinaryExpression ->` sites remain unaudited. On Kotlin/Native the guard is inert (a native stack overflow is a hard crash — see StackOverflowError.kt). **The whole pipeline additionally runs on a 256 MB deep-stack thread** (`runWithDeepStack`, DeepStack.kt, wrapping `compileParsed`; virtual-reserved and lazily committed on BOTH backends since round 827, so it costs nothing on a zero-swap box — measured max RSS 107-111 MB) — but that is a SAFETY MARGIN, not a licence: new walkers still iterate.
- **Defensive `try/catch` in the checker/parser/Vfs uses `catch (_: Exception)`, NEVER `catch (_: Throwable)`** (the whole codebase was swept to Exception on 2026-07-04 — 135 sites). `Throwable` swallows `Error` subtypes too, which defeats the SOE doctrine above: a `StackOverflowError` caught by a `catch (Throwable) { null }` returns a silently-wrong default INSTEAD of reaching the `init` boundary guard (→ TS2589), and an `OutOfMemoryError`/`AssertionError` gets masked into wrong output rather than surfacing. `catch (Exception)` still catches the genuine recoverable cases these guards exist for (NPE / ClassCast / IllegalState are `RuntimeException ⊆ Exception`), so narrowing is behavior-preserving on the corpus (byte-identical) EXCEPT that `Error`s now propagate — which is the point. The narrowing is validated by the full suite (exercises all catch paths) + a byte-identical self-compile; `DeepExpressionChainTest` pins the invariant that a deep chain surfaces TS2589 through the boundary guard rather than being swallowed. Removing the defensive `catch (Exception)` ENTIRELY (to surface NPEs from incomplete modeling as crashes) is a separate, per-site root-cause effort — do not do it blind.
- **The whole checker runs inside `Checker`'s `init` block, so a `private val` PROPERTY declared BELOW that block is still null while any pass executes (round 726).** A debug helper gated on such a property therefore prints NOTHING and reads as "wrong code path" when the path is right — put instrumentation state in a top-level `private val`, or hook `Diagnostic`'s constructor (a filtered `init` + `Throwable().stackTraceToString()`), which names the true emission site in one run.
- **A NEW `Checker` FIELD THAT ANY PASS CONSULTS MUST BE DECLARED BEFORE THE `init` BLOCK** — Kotlin initializes properties in DECLARATION order, so a `private val m = HashMap<…>()` written next to its own helper further down the ~110k-line file is still **null** throughout every init-dispatch pass, and the tell is an impossible-looking NPE on a non-nullable `val` (`because "this.m" is null`). Declare checker state beside the existing cache fields (`canonicalEnumSymCache` & co, ~:5582), never beside its consumer.
- **A `Checker.kt` stack-trace line number WRAPS modulo 65536** (the JVM LineNumberTable is u2; the file exceeds 90k lines): `Checker.kt:26287` is really line 91823 — add 65536 before trusting a frame that seems to point at unrelated code. **This bites EVERY reader of a `LineNumberTable`, not only a stack trace** — `javap -l`, and so `scripts/method_bytes_by_line.py`, report a method past line 65,536 at its WRAPPED line, and that range is usually a plausible-looking part of the file (round 820 read `tryInferSingleTypeParamFromArgs` as 49967–51030, i.e. inside the `companion object`). Add 65,536 back and ASSERT the corrected span contains the function, as `scripts/tisp_split_analyze.py --bytes` does — and note the third possibility, **+131,072**, plus INLINED stdlib appearing at SYNTHETIC lines PAST EOF, which makes a per-line JFR/`javap` attribution three guesses of which one is unfalsifiable (round 868): use line data to SHAPE a hypothesis, never to report a number.
- **A PER-NODE HOOK ON THE SPINE SEES *NONE* OF THE CHECKING AMBIENT — AT AN ARBITRARY NODE `currentLocalTypes` IS THE FILE-LEVEL MAP AND A BODY LOCAL ANSWERS WITH A SAME-NAMED GLOBAL'S TYPE.** Anything asking "what is the type HERE" must reproduce `ctaM3StmtAnchorCore`'s prologue and then `withCtaFrameLocals(ctaFrames.last())`. (archive: A PER-NODE HOOK ON THE SPINE SEES *NONE* OF THE CHECKING AMBIENT)
- **THAT DOES *NOT* GENERALISE TO NAME RESOLUTION — `currentLocalTypes` IS `String -> Type` AND HOLDS NO SYMBOL AND NO DECLARATION.** The walk-scoped input for that question is `spineCurrentScope`, already correct at an arbitrary node; but `spineScopeClear` nulls the chain per file, so post-hoc a body local resolves to a FILE-LEVEL binding. Refuse a MEMBER name rather than scope-looking it up. (archive: AND THE ABOVE DOES *NOT* GENERALISE TO NAME RESOLUTION)
- **GROUND TRUTH FOR A LANGUAGE-SERVICE ANSWER IS OBTAINABLE DIRECTLY — `tools/tsgo-7.0.2/lib/tsc --lsp -stdio` IS AN LSP SERVER** (client: `scripts/lsp_hover.py`). Do NOT hand-write expected values for an (API.\*) pin while this exists. (archive: GROUND TRUTH FOR A LANGUAGE-SERVICE ANSWER IS OBTAINABLE DIRECTLY)
- **A GRADLE `--tests` PATTERN CONTAINING A BACKTICK MATCHES *NOTHING* FOR A KOTLIN BACKTICK-NAMED TEST, AND THE RUN STILL EXITS 0** (the JVM method name has no backticks). Assert coverage from the RESULT XMLs — one `testcase` per intended family — never from the exit code. (archive: A GRADLE `--tests` PATTERN CONTAINING A BACKTICK MATCHES *NOTHING*)
- **AN AT-RISK SWEEP THAT SELECTS HAND-WRITTEN TEST CLASSES BY *NAME* CANNOT SEE A *FIXTURE*, AND ON THIS SUITE IT MISSES ~70% OF THE RELEVANT ONES.** Grep the test SOURCES, run every class, and assert every intended class appears in the result XMLs; a name-based filter is a convenience, never the at-risk set. (archive: AN AT-RISK SWEEP THAT SELECTS HAND-WRITTEN TEST CLASSES BY *NAME* CANNOT SEE A *FIXTURE*)
- **A REAL PROJECT'S SOURCES MAY BE CRLF — THE BENCH PROFILE'S tsc SOURCES ARE — AND PYTHON'S DEFAULT UNIVERSAL-NEWLINE TRANSLATION COLLAPSES `\r\n`, SHIFTING EVERY OFFSET.** It never errors: the oracle answers a real type for the WRONG name. Open with `newline=""` in any script mapping our offsets onto a file. (archive: A REAL PROJECT'S SOURCES MAY BE CRLF)
- **`Node.end` IS NOT THE END OF THE NODE — IT IS THE END OF THE TOKEN *FOLLOWING* IT, FOR EVERY NODE BUT `SourceFile`, SO SIBLING SPANS **OVERLAP** AND `[pos, end)` IS NOT A CONTAINMENT TEST (round 910).** `Parser.getEnd()` is `scanner.getPos()` (`Parser.kt:746`), read after the parser's one-token lookahead: in `const abc = 1;\nlet d = abc;`, `abc` reads `[6,11)` where its text is `[6,9)` (past the ` =`) and statement 1 reads `[0,18)` where its text ends at 14 (past `let`). **So `end - pos` is not a length, a caret on the `=` tests as inside the preceding identifier, and a caret on a statement's first keyword tests as inside the PREVIOUS statement** — any position→node lookup must bound each end by the next sibling's `pos` (or by a `Scanner` re-scan). `SourceFile` is exact only because EOF is zero-width. **And `Node.pos` is the mirror trap in the other direction: it is tsc's `getStart()`, NOT tsc's `pos`** — `Scanner.scan` sets `tokenPos` AFTER `scanLeadingTrivia()` (`Scanner.kt:331-333`), so trivia is already skipped and leading comments hang off `leadingComments` BELOW the node's span; a routine ported from tsc that adds a `getStart()` skip double-skips past the node's own first token. Pinned by `NodeSpanSemanticsTest` in the `-project` module.
- **A HAND-WRITTEN FIXTURE FOR A LEXICAL OR POSITION API DOES NOT CONTAIN WHAT REAL SOURCE CONTAINS, AND NINE GREEN ROUNDS PROVED IT.** Such a feature is gated by `TokenIndexInvariants` over REAL source with the parse as the ORACLE, not by pins; the local arm REFUSES with exit 2 rather than skipping. (archive: A HAND-WRITTEN FIXTURE FOR A LEXICAL OR POSITION API DOES NOT CONTAIN WHAT REAL SOURCE CONTAINS)
- **TWO SILENT TRAPS FOR `-project` CONSUMERS: a `Parameter` list may contain `isCommentPlaceholder` entries (`f(/* nothing */)` grows a PHANTOM parameter), and a FILE-FINAL token is unreachable by every position lookup when the file lacks a trailing newline.** (archive: TWO SILENT TRAPS FOUND BY THE FIRST PHASE-18 CONSUMERS)
- **A RAW `(pos, end)` PAIR DOES NOT ALWAYS IDENTIFY ONE NODE, AND THE CASE WHERE IT DOES NOT IS EXACTLY THE ONE A COMPLETION IS ASKED IN** (a dangling `.` at EOF makes the access and its receiver one span). Any span-keyed recorder must let a DESCENDANT overwrite its ancestor. (archive: A RAW `(pos, end)` PAIR DOES NOT ALWAYS IDENTIFY ONE NODE)
- **EVERY OFFSET→LINE CONVERSION IN THIS COMPILER GOES THROUGH `lineBreakWidthAt` (`LineStarts.kt`) — `\n`, `\r\n` AND A LONE `\r` EACH END EXACTLY ONE LINE — AND A PRIVATE COPY IS A SELF-INCONSISTENCY NOTHING HERE CAN SEE.** Add a converter only by delegating to `computeLineStarts` or `lineAndCharacterAt`. (archive: EVERY OFFSET→LINE CONVERSION IN THIS COMPILER GOES THROUGH `lineBreakWidthAt`)
- **NO CORPUS OR `diagnose()` FIXTURE *CAN* CARRY A LONE `\r`: `parseMultiFileSource` OPENS WITH `.replace("\r\n", "\n").replace("\r", "\n")`, SO A LINE-TERMINATOR PIN WRITTEN THE NORMAL WAY IS VACUOUS AND PASSES.** Use `diagnoseVerbatim`; anything about source TEXT SHAPE is corpus-invisible and `-project`-visible. (archive: AND THE REASON THAT FAMILY WAS INVISIBLE IS NOT "no fixture has a lone `\r`")
- **A DOC SECTION THAT SUMMARISES BEHAVIOUR IS NOT EVIDENCE OF IT, AND MEASURED HERE IT DECAYS AT ~ONE FALSE CLAIM PER THREE ROUNDS** — re-READING the source confirms a claim rather than testing it. A behavioural claim a test can express belongs in a test the page NAMES; one no test can express must be MARKED as such. (archive: A DOC SECTION THAT SUMMARISES BEHAVIOUR IS NOT EVIDENCE OF IT)
- **Every AST node class extends `NodeBase` (INV.2(a), round 495): `nodeId`/`parent` are stamped by `indexSourceFile` at the end of `Parser.parse()`; data-class `copy()` / Transformer-synthesized nodes stay UNINDEXED (−1/null) — never rely on nodeId/parent after a transform.**
- **EVERY NODE CLASS STAMPS `NodeBase.kindId` VIA `init { kindId = NodeKind.X }` (IT SURVIVES `copy()`, UNLIKE nodeId/parent) AND THE HOT DISPATCHERS SWITCH ON IT — KEEP `NodeKind` IDS DENSE.** A NEW node class needs `NodeBase(), ` FIRST in its supertype list plus a `forEachChild` arm; a new NODE-TYPED property must be walked there too (`ForEachChildOracleTest` is the oracle). LUB trap: annotate the return type of a `when` mixing node classes. (archive: M0.2 (round 621): every node class ALSO stamps `NodeBase.kindId`)
- **Every option-derived `Parser(...)` constructor argument MUST flow through `ParserFlags`/`computeParserFlags` (INV.1(e), round 494)**: the project crawl pre-parses each file with those flags and the core's multi-file site REUSES the tree when recorded content+flags match (`ParsedSource.preParsed`) — a parse-affecting parameter computed inline at a parse site is INVISIBLE to the match, so the core silently reuses a tree parsed WITHOUT it (a wrong tree, not just lost perf). Extend `ParserFlags` + `computeParserFlags` instead; `Inv1PreParseReuseTest` pins the gate.
- **A DISPLAY GENERALIZATION THAT MATCHES tsc CAN *BLIND* A WHOLE FAMILY OF NARROWING PROBES THAT READ THE NARROWED TYPE OUT OF THE MESSAGE AT A *PRIMITIVE* TARGET — 47 ASSERTIONS IN 13 CLASSES WENT BLIND RATHER THAN RED ON THE ENUM ARM, WHICH NO GATE HERE CAN SEE (2026-09-04, (PARITY.1), refused for this reason).** The (REL.2) arc pins `'K.A'` vs `'K'` deliberately, and generalizing collapses `K.A`, `K.A | K.B` and an un-narrowed `K` to one string. **The remedy is a `never` PROBE TARGET** — tsc suppresses the generalization there, so such a pin becomes tsc-verifiable for the first time (measured: nine narrowing shapes byte-identical to tsgo and pristine).
- **`lookupPerFileForNode` IS KEYED BY THE *FILE*, SO IT ANSWERS FOR A FILE-LEVEL DECLARATION HOWEVER DEEPLY THE REFERENCE IS NESTED, AND B83.5 KEEPS THE SHADOWING BLOCK-SCOPED DECLARATION OUT OF THE BINDER TABLES.** A consumer resolving a NAME must ask `lexicalScopeSymbol` first; a walker with its own hand-written shadow test is a second, silent copy of this trap. (archive: `lookupPerFileForNode` IS KEYED BY THE *FILE*)
- **"BLOCK-SCOPED" UNDERSTATES B83.5: `Binder` RECURSES INTO STATEMENTS FROM EXACTLY TWO PLACES, SO A `class` AT THE VERY TOP OF A FUNCTION BODY IS EQUALLY UNBOUND** — a repro need not nest anything. The scope-space ascent has ONE home (`LexicalScopeResolver`) and its axes are PARAMETERS: a new consumer gets a new method with its axes stated, never a widened existing one. (archive: AND "BLOCK-SCOPED" UNDERSTATES IT)
- **BLOCK-SCOPED DECLARATIONS ARE NOT BOUND (B83.5): any `class`/`interface`/`type`/`enum`/`function` inside a `Block`/`If`/`While`/`For`/`Try`/`Switch` is in no `locals`/`globals`, so every such lookup answers null.** The failure is not always a null: a UNIQUE name degrades to `any` SILENTLY, a SHADOWING one resolves to the OUTER symbol. CLOSED for `enum` only; a name-based enum reader must consult all THREE scopes. (archive: Block-scoped declarations are NOT bound** (B83.5, 2026-05-27))
- **THE INV.4 CHECK SPINE (`checkSpine`) IS THE WALK FOR MIGRATED PER-NODE CHECKS — A NEW HANDLER CARRIES ITS OWN OPTION/FILE-KIND GATES AND SEES ALL NODES; PREFER IT TO A NEW FULL-TREE PASS.** `spineWalkFile` visits every node of every file, so per-frame costs are the whole game (do not "simplify" the primitive phase stack). It also maintains `spineCurrentScope` (INV.4(c)(i)). **The ~25 per-SHAPE migration recipes are in the archive — grep `INV.4 check spine` BEFORE migrating a pass.** (archive: The INV.4 check spine (`checkSpine`, round 514) is THE walk for migrated per-node checks)
- **INV.4(c)(iii): THE checkUnresolvedNames FAMILY IS ON THE SPINE AND ALL ITS RECURSIVE WALKERS ARE DELETED — A NEW POSITION IS AN EDGE OR A MARK, NEVER A WALKER CALL (adding one double-emits).** Every emission routes through `spineUResEmit`; `checkUnresolvedNames` is the declarationOnly-ONLY driver; a new scope boundary must be mirrored in `spineUResEnter`; and `--listAll` raw order is not position-sorted, so A/B by SORTED lines. (archive: INV.4(c)(iii) (rounds 524–528): the checkUnresolvedNames family is ON THE SPINE)
- **Every init-dispatch pass is wrapped `pass("checkFoo") { checkFoo() }` (INV.0, PassTiming.kt — the opt-in `--passTiming` table): wrap a NEW init-level pass the same way, keep every PassTiming hook additive-only behind `PassTiming.enabled` (false must stay behavior-free — the corpus suite + listAll A/B pin it), and `pass` stays NON-inline (~514 inline expansions of its try/finally body would push the ctor toward the JVM 64 KB method limit).**
- **A PROBE'S `if (mode != ON) return` GUARD CANNOT PROTECT ITS OWN ARGUMENT — KOTLIN EVALUATES ARGUMENTS STRICTLY, SO `census(lazyThing.size)` DOES THE WORK ON EVERY PRODUCTION COMPILE.** Pass the OBJECT and derive the value AFTER the guard; pin a lazily-materialising type with a PRODUCTION-mode compile asserting its materialisation counter stays 0. (archive: A PROBE'S `if (mode != ON) return` GUARD CANNOT PROTECT ITS OWN ARGUMENT)
- **A BACKGROUNDED `sleep` IS PREEMPTED BY YOUR NEXT COMMAND, so "I waited 25 minutes" is usually false (round 722).** Launch a wait and then END THE TURN; real time passes only while you are idle awaiting its notification. Rounds 718–721 recorded 16-, 25- and 40-minute compiles that were really ~3-minute ones, and built a whole theory (contention, a competing bench loop, failing hardware) on the broken clock — the tell is a process whose `/proc`-verified age is far younger than your supposed wait. **Also launch long builds DETACHED (`nohup … &`), log gradle output to a FILE (a `| tail` pipeline shows nothing until it ends), and stamp the script with `date` before/after so a restart is visible.**
- **A FROM-SCRATCH `compileKotlinJvm` needs 5 GB, and gradle.properties now pins it (BUILD.1, owner-approved, rounds 717–720). Do NOT lower `kotlin.daemon.jvmargs`, and do NOT `pkill -9` the Kotlin daemon.** The measured ladder on this module: 2g GC-thrashes forever (350% CPU, RSS pinned at the ceiling, ZERO class files — it looks exactly like a hang, because Kotlin's backend only writes output at the end), 3g dies mid-compile, 4g fails in ~6 min with the explicit `Not enough memory to run compilation`, 5g succeeds in ~6.5 min. `org.gradle.jvmargs` was cut 2g → 1g to make room (Gradle's own daemon peaks ~1.1 GB; 5g + 2g oversubscribed the 7.7 GB box and the kernel killed the compile daemon mid-run with nothing in any log). Why one module needs this: Checker.kt is ~110k lines in a SINGLE file plus the generated real-lib sources' 3.71 MB of embedded string constants (566 KB before round 731 shipped the DOM set), so the unit is all resident at once. **An INCREMENTAL compile takes ~2 min and needs none of it — what forces the expensive path is invalidating the caches, above all `pkill -9` of the Kotlin daemon, which is exactly what the memory-freeing ritual below tells you to do.** Prefer a graceful `pkill` (no -9), and read a suddenly-slow build as "I invalidated the caches", not "my change broke something". **AND: an IDLE daemon from the previous build squats on its heap for its 2-hour idle timeout — measured 4.33 GB held while the next build tried to start, leaving 297 MB free and starving it (round 721).** Before a build that will compile from scratch, stop the previous daemons (`./gradlew --stop`, or `kill <pid>` for the Kotlin one) — otherwise the second build fights the first one's corpse for RAM. **A `pkill -f <name>` whose pattern appears in your own waiting tasks kills THOSE too** (round 721 killed its own timers with `pkill -f gate721`); use a bracket pattern or kill by PID.
- **NATIVE BUILDS ARE RE-AUTHORIZED ON THIS BOX (owner, 2026-08-04, on a 8-core / 15.6 GB VPS) — SUPERSEDING the round-775 "never run a Kotlin/Native or GraalVM build here" directive, which was issued for a 4-core / 7.7 GB host after one froze it for ~2 HOURS.** What has NOT changed and is still the whole hazard: **swap is ZERO** (`free -m` → `Swap: 0`), so memory over-commit does not degrade into paging — the kernel reclaims file-backed pages including running processes' text, and the box stalls instead of OOM-killing cleanly. The konan/LLVM backend and the linked native executable are separate processes outside every JVM heap, so no `-Xmx` bounds them. **Therefore, before any native task: `./gradlew --stop` + a graceful bracket-pattern `pkill -f 'KotlinCompile[D]aemon'` (an idle Kotlin daemon squats on ~3.8-5 GB for its 2-hour timeout), then watch `free -m`; run native tasks ALONE, never beside a suite run or a bench.** K/N compiles inside the GRADLE daemon, so its heap is `org.gradle.jvmargs` — see the round-772 entry below. Native is opt-in via `-PenableNativeTargets=true` (the linuxX64 target does not register without it, so a plain `./gradlew build` never sees it); CI still runs it in `.github/workflows/native.yml`. The JVM gates (`./gradlew jvmTest`, `compileKotlinJvm`, `scripts/cost_gate.py`) are routine and unaffected — one at a time, as always.
- **KOTLIN/NATIVE COMPILES INSIDE THE *GRADLE* DAEMON, SO ITS HEAP IS `org.gradle.jvmargs` — NOT `kotlin.daemon.jvmargs`, AND THERE IS NO `kotlin.native.jvmArgs` (round 772, after round 771 concluded the opposite and was WRONG).** [Kotlin's own tips page](https://kotlinlang.org/docs/native-improving-compilation-time.html) states it: "Kotlin/Native runs from the Gradle daemon … the same JVM process is used". BUILD.1 cut `org.gradle.jvmargs` 2g → 1g to make room for the 5 GB *Kotlin* daemon, **so the JVM-compile memory fix is exactly what starves Kotlin/Native** — `compileKotlinLinuxX64` OOMs at the inherited 1g no matter what you pass to the Kotlin daemon. With `-Dorg.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx6g` it SUCCEEDS in **2m04s**, and `compileTestKotlinLinuxX64` in a further 23s. **BUT `-Xmx6g` ON THIS BOX WILL FREEZE IT, AND THE OWNER FEELS IT — THIS MACHINE HAS ZERO SWAP (`free -m` → `Swap: 0`), so memory over-commit does not degrade gracefully into paging: the box stalls, becomes unresponsive for minutes, and the kernel OOM-kills something.** Before any ≥5g experiment: `./gradlew --stop` AND `pkill -f 'KotlinCompile[D]aemon'` first (an idle Kotlin daemon squats on **3.76 GB** measured, for its 2-hour timeout, so 6g + 3.76g on 7.7g is an instant stall), then watch `free -m`. The OOM-killer leaves NOTHING greppable in a build log — its signature is `Gradle build daemon disappeared unexpectedly` or a shell that simply dies — and `dmesg` is not readable as non-root here, so process forensics (`ps -eo rss --sort=-rss`) is the only diagnosis. Round 771's "K/N cannot compile this module on a ~8 GB box, a HARDWARE limit" is **retracted**: it tuned a property nothing reads (`-Pkotlin.native.jvmArgs`) and misread the resulting 1g failures as a 5–6g ceiling. **The LINK is no longer unmet (round 822 supersedes this half): on the 15.6 GB box `linuxX64Test` compiles, LINKS and RUNS the whole suite green in 25m33s — 13,689 tests, 0 failures.** What made it fit is heap going DOWN, not up: 6g was never the floor, `gradle.properties` now pins the measured **4g**, and the link gets the difference. Round 772's ~22-min `Gradle build daemon disappeared unexpectedly` was the OS OOM-killer (not a Java OOM — nothing greppable in the log) and remains the signature to expect if that headroom is spent again. Also true and independently useful: **do NOT pass `--no-daemon`** — it forces the compile into Gradle's own JVM and fails differently again.
- **The generated real-lib sources MUST stay split across `RealLibFilesPart*.kt` (~250 KB each) — merging them back into one file breaks the BUILD, not the tests (round 731).** All 3.71 MB of `src/lib` in one generated file with one `buildMap { }` lambda fails `compileKotlinJvm` with the BUILD.1 message `Not enough memory to run compilation` after 7m34s at the pinned 5 GB; split, a COLD compile takes 2m25s — so the single big file was itself the memory cost, and 3.71 MB in pieces is CHEAPER than the old 566 KB in one. The facade cuts the concatenation apart by recorded CHAR lengths, so `libNames`/`libLengths` must stay declared BEFORE `files` (object properties initialize in source order), and the generator wipes its output dir first (a shrinking pin would otherwise leave a stale part file that still compiles in).
- **Running a `-Xmx4g` self-compile (`MainKt --noEmit`) MANUALLY (not via the bench script) while the Gradle daemon (~1.8 GB) + KotlinCompileDaemon (~2.7 GB) are up exceeds the 7.7 GB box → swap → apparent "hang"/timeout that looks like a code perf bug but is NOT.** Free memory first: `./gradlew --stop && pkill -9 -f KotlinCompileDaemon` (verify with `free -m`, want ≥5 GB free), THEN run. A stubbed-to-no-op helper that STILL times out is the tell it's memory, not your code.
- **Do NOT run `compileKotlinJvm` while a background self-compile A/B (`MainKt` on the bench project) is in flight** — the recompile rewrites class files the running JVM lazily loads → `ClassNotFoundException` mid-run (looks like a compiler crash). Concurrent CLI RUNS are safe; only recompiles clobber.
- **`Type.Reference` interning**: Construction goes through `getOrInternReference(target, args)` — since (INV.0) step 1 a delegation to `TypeInterner` (`TypeInterner.kt`, held in CheckerState, keyed on `target.id|arg1.id,arg2.id,...`). Identical instantiations share an instance and `Type.id`, which is what the id-based cycle detection in `relationComparisonStack` relies on. Do NOT call `Type.Reference(...)` directly in new code.

### Test assertion gotchas

The per-round narratives behind 25 of this section's entries now live in `docs/history/CLAUDE-GOTCHAS-ARCHIVE.md` under `## Measured dead-ends + test assertion gotchas — moved from CLAUDE.md 2026-09-21`. **Nothing was deleted and nothing was moved wholesale**: the write-time conventions — the one-idiom `assert`/`have` rule, the power-assert AST hazard, the backtick-name rules, the narrowing-probe fixture conventions, the countdown-pin rule and the build/results-dir traps — are resident BYTE-IDENTICAL, because they are consulted while WRITING a test and their detail IS the invariant. A distilled entry ends in `(archive: <key>)`; search it with `command grep -n '<key or test class|round|(P18.n)>' docs/history/CLAUDE-GOTCHAS-ARCHIVE.md`.

- **THE TWO tsc-6 *MIRROR* CLASSES COMPARE AGAINST TypeScript 6's BASELINES OF LEDGERED TS-1 CASES, SO EVERY TypeScript-7 CHANGE STILL TO LAND IS A COUNTDOWN INSIDE THEM — GREP THE MIRRORED BASELINE *FILES* (the 21 names in `tsgoSelfCheckBaselines`) BEFORE A FAMILY ROUND, NEVER THE CLASS SOURCE, WHICH NAMES NO CODE.** The repair is a COUNTED substitution of exactly the rows tsgo's baseline shows, never a weakened compare. (archive: THE TWO tsc-6 *MIRROR* CLASSES (`TsgoHarnessSelfCheckBaselinesTest` and)
- **THERE ARE *TWO* `build/generated/typescript-tests` TREES AND THE ONE AT THE REPO ROOT IS A PRE-MODULE-SPLIT LEFTOVER — a census taken off it reads a plausible-but-WRONG corpus.** The live one is `xemantic-typescript-compiler-core/build/generated/typescript-tests`. Tell: an ignore count of 0 cannot be right while `tsgoPendingBaselines`/`logicalParityDivergences` is non-empty, and the generator spells it FULLY QUALIFIED (`@kotlin.test.Ignore`). (archive: THERE ARE *TWO* `build/generated/typescript-tests` TREES)
- **A CORPUS-SCREEN ZERO IS *BLIND* FOR ANY BASELINE THAT IS `@Ignore`d, SO AN ABLATION ARM WHOSE DAMAGE LANDS ON A **PENDING** ROW READS `0 mismatch(es)` WHILE ADDING FALSE ROWS (2026-09-17, (P18.131) arm a4: two invented rows on legal code, screen clean).** Only `--include <name>` reaches such a row. When an arm targets a mechanism whose ledger entry is still pending, run the screen WITH `--include` for that baseline, and record the plain zero as blind rather than green.
- **A MULTI-LINE `$interpolation` INSIDE A `"""…""".trimIndent()` SILENTLY DISABLES THE DE-INDENT — the interpolated continuation lines carry no indentation of their own, so the common prefix is 0 and NOTHING is stripped (2026-09-17, (P18.130): six span pins read 12 columns too high, which looks exactly like a span defect in the compiler).** Spell a shared prelude out per fixture, or keep the interpolation on one line.
- **A VALID `\uXXXX` ESCAPE WRITTEN INTO A FIXTURE THROUGH THIS TOOLING IS SILENTLY DECODED BEFORE THE COMPILER SEES IT, WHILE AN INVALID ONE SURVIVES — so three sibling pins passed and only the fourth failed (2026-09-13).** A test about ESCAPE handling, an invalid-character span, or any per-CODEPOINT column must therefore assert on bytes it controls: write the character through a Kotlin escape in the fixture string rather than as literal `\uXXXX` text, or verify the fixture round-trips before reading the result as evidence about the compiler.
- **A DIVERGENCE FAMILY ASSIGNED BY A ROW'S *FIRST DIFFERING LINE* IS NOT A FAMILY — 13 of 53 "wording" rows were wording; the rest were span/width, type display and duplicate-identifier.** The first differing line names the SYMPTOM's position, not the mechanism. And **fixing the named difference does not always close the row**, so a family's "landed" count must come from the suite, never from the edits made. (archive: A DIVERGENCE FAMILY ASSIGNED BY A ROW'S)
- **A REFERENCE'S OWN `.diff` LAYER CLASSIFIES A *FILE*; A RED TEST CLASSIFIES WHAT **WE** GOT WRONG — SO A FAMILY RANKING TAKEN FROM tsgo's `submodule*/` DIFFS IS A RANKING OF *ITS* DIVERGENCES.** Re-derive it from the RED SET, with two cheap controls: every red comes from the NEW baseline root, and every red carries a diff layer. **A bucket count in a three-way fallback is part of the PIN** — a wrong arm REMOVES a test, and a shrunken corpus reads like a green one. (archive: A REFERENCE'S OWN `.diff` LAYER CLASSIFIES)
- **A `tsgo-pending` ENTRY MEANS "IMPLEMENT IT AND IT CLOSES", SO CHECK WHAT THE BASELINE *IS* BEFORE FILING ONE — A ROW WHOSE BASELINE IS PRISTINE's CAN NEVER CLOSE, AND IMPLEMENTING tsgo's ANSWER MOVES YOU FURTHER FROM IT.** `pendingByBaseline` enforces uniqueness only, never that the baseline is tsgo's. **The four `tsgoExpected*` buckets count BASELINES, NOT CASES.** (archive: A `tsgo-pending` ENTRY MEANS "IMPLEMENT)
- **A PENDING LEDGER ROW CAN HIDE A *SECOND* DEFECT BEHIND THE ONE ITS ENTRY NAMES, BECAUSE A BASELINE COMPARISON STOPS BEING EVIDENCE ABOUT EVERY OTHER ROW THE MOMENT IT MISMATCHES ONCE.** Re-read the diff after closing the named mechanism, and name emitters with `--passTiming`'s `emissions by pass` rather than grepping for the code. **The ledger entry's own SIZE claim is the part to distrust.** (archive: A PENDING LEDGER ROW CAN HIDE A *SECOND*)
- **A CENSUS OF AT-RISK PINS BY DIAGNOSTIC *CODE* IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO EVERY PIN THAT DEPENDS ON A CODE **NOT** BEING EMITTED — that was 29 of 41 failures in one round** (nine classes reach a shared directive constant purely to keep an option row OUT, so they carry the code nowhere). **Census the OPTION, the DIRECTIVE and the shared test constants as well as the code**; when the repair is a shared fixture constant, give it the explicit value rather than weakening the assertions that read it. (archive: A CENSUS OF AT-RISK PINS BY DIAGNOSTIC)
- **A `tsgo-pending` BASELINE IS NOT A `LogicalParityDivergence`: a pending row is a tsgo-TARGET answer we do not produce YET (implement it), a divergence is one we DECIDED not to follow (pin the logic)** — hence no `pinnedBy` on the former, and the build fails on a baseline in BOTH lists. Both emit the subtest `@Ignore`d, so **the suite's SKIPPED number is load-bearing**: one that moves without a ledger entry is the tell. (archive: A `tsgo-pending` BASELINE IS NOT A)
- **A UNION *CONTEXTUAL-TYPE* FIXTURE WRITTEN AS `declare const zf: A | B; zf((p) => …)` NEVER REACHES THE CONTEXTUAL-SIGNATURE ARM — THE ARGUMENT IS HANDED THE *COMBINED CALLEE* SIGNATURE'S PARAMETER (stage 1's `combineUnionSignatures`), SO IT MEASURES THE UNION CALLEE AND BOTH REFERENCES PRINT TS7006 + TS2345 THERE WHATEVER `callableSignaturesForCtx` DOES (2026-09-12, (CHK.97) D3).** Probe a union contextual type through `declare function take(cb: A | B)` or an annotation (variable, object-literal property, return position). And a `Type.Reference` member's OWN signatures must be read, never its TARGET's: the target fallback reads `Cb<string> | Cb<number>` as identical and hands the arrow a bare `T`.
- **A NUMERIC `enum` IS A *UNION*, SO `let x: E = null!` IS ASSIGNMENT-NARROWED TO `never` BY BOTH REFERENCES AND THE PROBE GOES SILENT — INCLUDING AT A BOUND, FILE-LEVEL CONTROL (2026-09-10).** It reads exactly like the defect under test, and it was caught only because the FILE-LEVEL control cell failed too. Probe an enum-typed binding through a non-narrowing shape (`let a: E[] = []; const p: string = a[0]`), and treat a control cell that fails as evidence about the PROBE before it is evidence about the compiler.
- **WHEN THE TWO REFERENCES PRODUCE A DIFFERENT *ELABORATION SHAPE* FOR A FIXTURE THEY CANNOT ADJUDICATE THE RENDERING UNDER TEST — CONSTRUCT A SIBLING FIXTURE THAT FORCES THEM INTO THE MESSAGE SHAPE YOU ARE MEASURING** (a FRESH object literal makes both drill to the offending MEMBER; a NON-fresh source puts them back on the whole-object form). **A reference that answers a different question is not evidence either way.** (archive: WHEN THE TWO REFERENCES PRODUCE A DIFFERENT)
- **A FULL-TEXT PIN IN AN *UNRELATED* FAMILY SILENTLY DEPENDS ON EVERY DISPLAY RULE, SO A RENDERING CHANGE REDDENS NARROWING / ASSIGNABILITY / INFERENCE PINS WHOSE SUBJECT IS NOT DISPLAY — AND ONLY THE FULL SUITE SEES IT.** After a rendering change, grep the test sources for the substring under change and update the EXPECTATION only, never the test name, when the pin's own subject has not moved. (archive: A FULL-TEXT PIN IN AN *UNRELATED* FAMILY)
- **A PIN THAT KEYS ON A *DISPLAY STRING* IS SILENTLY DISARMED BY THE NEXT DISPLAY CHANGE — IT JUST STOPS BEING ABLE TO FAIL, AND NOTHING GOES RED** (three "negative controls" keyed on a substring that became unproducible would have passed a binary with the whole chain deleted). **Assert the CODE, not the message, wherever the message is not itself the thing under test.** (archive: A PIN THAT KEYS ON A *DISPLAY STRING*)
- **COUNT THE SILENCE-ASSERTING PINS BEFORE TRUSTING ANY OF THEM — one class held TEN COUNTDOWNS, SIX genuine controls and ONE structurally vacuous pin, and its own names did not distinguish them.** Convention: a pin asserting today's WRONG answer is named `residue - …` and its KDoc carries the exact reference row and the item that owns it. (archive: …AND IN THE SAME CLASS, COUNT THE SILENCE-ASSERTING)
- **A PIN *PAIR* THAT DOCUMENTS AN ASYMMETRY BETWEEN TWO SPELLINGS OF THE SAME SHAPE IS RECORDING AN IMPLEMENTATION DEFECT, NOT A LANGUAGE RULE — CHECK BOTH AGAINST THE REFERENCES BEFORE TREATING EITHER AS A CONTROL.** **A section header, class KDoc or comment naming a "quirk" / "asymmetry" / "we do not reach X" is the same countdown** — when you close one, grep for the others. (archive: A PIN *PAIR* THAT DOCUMENTS AN ASYMMETRY)
- **A MESSAGE-TEXT MARKER IS A *CONFOUNDED* POSITIVE CONTROL WHEREVER A REWRITING PIN WALKER MATCHES ON MESSAGE TEXT — the marker itself reddens the baseline on the BEFORE arm, so a working binary reads as broken.** Use a marker that is not part of the message, and settle such a question through the CORPUS HARNESS on both arms: a scratch CLI run uses the REAL libs where the corpus uses the embedded one. (archive: A MESSAGE-TEXT MARKER IS A *CONFOUNDED*)
- **A SEQUENCE OF `assert(<local>)` CALLS REPORTS ONLY THE *FIRST* FALSE ONE, SO A SUITE FAILURE LIST SYSTEMATICALLY UNDERCOUNTS WHICH EXPECTATIONS A CHANGE BROKE** — fixing exactly the reported locals then fails again on the next line. **Sweep the file and the module for sibling expectations sharing the substring you are about to change.** (archive: A SEQUENCE OF `assert(<local>)` CALLS)
- **`typeToString` HAS AT LEAST THREE CONSUMER FAMILIES BESIDE DIAGNOSTICS, SO A RULE SPECIFIC TO *RELATION ERRORS* MUST LIVE ON `relationErrorTargetDisplay`/`relationErrorSourceDisplay` AND NEVER IN THE GENERAL RENDERER — NEEDING A SECOND PER-CONSUMER BYPASS IS THE SIGNAL IT IS MISPLACED.** The three: the EXTERNALS generator's markers (another module, which no core-scoped `--tests` run touches), `Project.quickInfoAt`, and message-matching corpus PIN WALKERS. **A display change needs a pin in EACH consumer it can reach**; hover ground truth is `tsc --lsp -stdio`. (archive: `typeToString` HAS AT LEAST THREE CONSUMER)
- **THE `diagnose()` MULTI-FILE HARNESS CANNOT RESOLVE `import * as Mid from './mid'` WHERE `mid.ts` IS `export * from './lib'` — a type-position `Mid.Far.X` reads an ours-only TS2694 there while the identical shape through `ProjectCompiler` is clean on ours AND both references.** Use a DIRECT import or the `-project` module: when a shape involves a directory, a barrel or a package, `diagnose()` is not the instrument. (archive: THE `diagnose()` MULTI-FILE HARNESS)
- **A `Vfs` WRAPPER THAT COUNTS READS IS A *CONCURRENT* INSTRUMENT — THE CRAWL READS FROM 16 WORKERS AT ONCE — SO A PLAIN `reads++` OR A `HashMap` PUT IN IT IS ROUND 825's RACE IN THE TEST HARNESS**, whose symptom is a count pin that reads 0 once and is green everywhere else. Count with `kotlin.concurrent.atomics` (`-project`'s `CountingVfs` is the reference); treat a one-off red as a race before an order. (archive: A `Vfs` WRAPPER THAT COUNTS READS IS)
- **A LOOP-NARROWING FIXTURE WHOSE SUBJECT IS AN *IDENTIFIER* IS VACUOUS IN **BOTH** DIRECTIONS — `currentLocalTypes` ANSWERS IT AND THAT MAP IS STATEMENT-ORDERED WITH NO LOOP NOTION AT ALL (2026-08-28, (CHK.69)).** Round 784's gate sends the DECLARATION/ASSIGNMENT/RETURN readers there for a primitive target, so `if (typeof x === "string") { while (c) { x = 1 } const p: string = x }` is silent on a working binary AND on one with the loop arm deleted (it is also a shipped false negative — tsc emits TS2322). Use a **PROPERTY PATH** (`h.req`), which is the only subject that reaches the flow walk.
- **AN ABLATION ARM THAT *ADDS* THE OLD BEHAVIOUR BESIDE THE NEW ONE READS `0 RED` AND LOOKS LIKE A REDUNDANT GUARD, AND ROUND 922's `cmp`-AGAINST-ITS-OWN-SNAPSHOT CHECK PASSES FOR IT (2026-08-28).** The (CHK.69) `for-of` arm re-inserted the pre-fix `joinAntecedent(postLoop, currentFlow)` without REMOVING the new one, so the label stayed reachable: a real diff, a clean build, a dead arm. A diff proves the edit LANDED, never that it removes the thing under test — say per arm which line the mistake DELETES.
- **A CONTROL THAT GOES VACUOUS BECAUSE THE COMPILER *IMPROVED* IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM ONE THAT WAS ALWAYS DEAD (2026-08-28, `LoopEntryRetryGateTest` / `UnionRetrySubstitutionTest`).** Both assert the plain walk DIVERGES from the `FollowLoopEntry` mirror over a loop-crossing population; (CHK.69) made the two agree on those shapes and `typeDiff` read 0. The tell is that ONLY the controls move while every functional pin in the class stays green — repair the FIXTURE (here: a loop that ASSIGNS the guarded reference still washes), never the assertion.
- **A MEMO OVER A BUILD CAN ONLY SERVE A STALE ANSWER WHEN ITS KEY IS BYTE-IDENTICAL ACROSS THE CHANGE — SO A STALENESS PIN THAT EDITS THE QUERIED FILE IS VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION, BECAUSE THE EDIT MOVES THE SPAN AND THEREFORE THE KEY (2026-08-22, (INC.12)).** Keep the caret's own file untouched and edit something ELSE: another file's content (the answer must move), and — the direction CLAUDE.md already names as the dangerous one — an edit that ADDS A FILE to the program, where a mis-keyed hit is a MISSING FILE and not a wrong type. `ProjectCaptureMemoTest` is the reference pair.
- **TWO LANGUAGE-SERVICE FEATURES CAN BUILD THE *IDENTICAL* `TypeCaptureRequest`, WHICH IS WHY THE REQUEST IS THE RIGHT MEMO KEY AND THE CARET IS NOT** — hover and go-to-definition at one caret name the same span and read different channels of one answer. It also means **a build-COUNT pin is measuring the memo unless it re-dirties between rows.** (archive: AND TWO LANGUAGE-SERVICE FEATURES CAN)
- **A BUILD-*COUNT* PIN CANNOT TELL "SERVED FROM A LIVE CHECKER" FROM "ANSWERED NOTHING" — (INC.40)'s a3 ABLATION MADE THE CACHED PATH RETURN AN EMPTY DIAGNOSTIC LIST AND THE COUNT PIN STAYED **GREEN**, i.e. a count-only suite ships a language service reporting no errors at all, at full speed (2026-08-24).** Pair every cost pin with a VALUE pin asserting the answer equals a fresh build's — the count grades the mechanism, only the value grades the ANSWER; and an edit-sees-it pin is the third leg, since a handle that never invalidates is also count-green.
- **A COUNT OF *ALL* Vfs READS IS NOT A COUNT OF BUILDS — SOME COMPILER CACHE WARMS ACROSS BUILDS INSIDE ONE JVM AND TAKES A SOURCE READ WITH IT, SO A SIX-BUILD LOOP READ **29** TOUCHES ONCE AND **30** ON EVERY RERUN (round 914).** The stable unit is reads of the project's `tsconfig.json` — exactly one per `ProjectCompiler.build`, cached nowhere across them — and a control pin (three dirty builds -> three) is what makes that a measurement rather than an assumption. A sum-based build counter is order-dependent, i.e. the pin that reddens for a reason the round cannot name.
- **A MULTI-CASE FIXTURE THAT REUSES A VALUE NAME ACROSS `namespace`s IS SILENTLY COLLAPSED INTO *ONE* CASE, IN BOTH ARMS, SO A BEFORE/AFTER READING OF IT IS UNINTERPRETABLE (2026-09-22, (CHK.147)).** A namespace-local VALUE is not namespace-scoped here — the FIRST `declare let s` wins program-wide and the behaviour is order-dependent — so the five `Example` namespaces of `assignmentCompatWithDiscriminatedUnion` were all comparing Example1's types, and a naive reading of that file said "ours 14 -> 3" where the honest one-namespace-per-file reading is **14 -> 6**. **Split such a fixture per namespace before reading it**; it fails in the reassuring direction, and no gate here prints it.
- **A PIN WHOSE FIXTURE NAMES A LIB UTILITY TYPE IS VACUOUS IN THE `diagnose()` HARNESS — ITS EMBEDDED LIB DECLARES NO `Omit`/`Pick`/`Partial`/`Record`, SO THE REFERENCE RESOLVES TO `errorType` *BEFORE* THE ARM UNDER TEST AND THE PIN MEASURES NOTHING IN EITHER DIRECTION (2026-09-22, (P18.169): the first pin set read **6 of 18 RED on a WORKING binary**).** Declare the alias locally in the fixture; cover the lib spellings with a CLI matrix against tsgo instead, or `@useRealLibs`.
- **A `.tsx`/`.jsx` FIXTURE CANNOT PIN THAT `computeParserFlags` WAS CONSULTED, AND NEITHER CAN A TOP-LEVEL `await` — BOTH MEASURED, BOTH VACUOUS** (`Parser.isJsxFile` reads the EXTENSION whatever flags it is handed). **The one option-derived GRAMMAR difference is `forceJsx` on a plain `.js` file** — pin against that or the assertion measures nothing. (archive: A `.tsx`/`.jsx` FIXTURE CANNOT PIN)
- **AN IN-MEMORY FIXTURE PINNING MODULE RESOLUTION MEASURES NOTHING UNLESS ITS tsconfig SETS AN ES `module` KIND AND THE PROGRAM CARRIES >= 2 FILES — AND IT FAILS SILENTLY, AS AN EMPTY DIAGNOSTIC LIST.** Two gates: the region returns early on `binderResults.size <= 1` (**the real libs bind through their own path and do not count**), and the relative-specifier leg demands `options.module in ES_MODULE_KINDS` with `moduleResolution`/`paths`/`baseUrl`/`rootDir(s)`/`moduleSuffixes` unset. **Any import pin needs a NEGATIVE CONTROL.** (archive: AN IN-MEMORY FIXTURE PINNING MODULE)
- **A PassLab ABLATION CANNOT REACH A FIXTURE BUILT BY A DIRECT `Checker(options, results)` CONSTRUCTION — `PassLab.ensureLoaded()` lives in `runWithDeepStack`, which that harness never crosses, so the pin runs UN-ABLATED and reads GREEN against a binary whose pass is disabled**, and it is ORDER-dependent besides. Wrap the multi-file harness in `runWithDeepStack { … }`. (archive: A PassLab ABLATION CANNOT REACH A FIXTURE)
- **IN A SMALL FIXTURE ONLY A BARE EXPRESSION-STATEMENT PROPERTY ACCESS (`x.p;`) HAS A *FIRST* NARROWING WALK — in an argument or a `return`, an earlier pass already walked that reference, the round-664 inter-walk memo serves it, `narrowWalkLaunches` does not move, and every round-790-style "did the walk really run" bracket answers UNKNOWN (round 794).** Six candidate fixtures read `loopFree = 0` before that was understood, which would have made a whole pin population EMPTY and its verifier assertions vacuously true. Assert the population is non-empty (`assert(loopFree > 0)`) in any pin that measures over such a bracket.
- **A `const k = <expr> as unknown as E` SUBJECT IS INVISIBLE AT THE ARGUMENT GATE — for a FILE-LEVEL enum too — so a narrowing pin written that way passes on a broken build for a reason unrelated to the fix (round 782, two pins).** Make the subject a PARAMETER (`function inner(k: E)`). General form: validate a probe SHAPE against a control whose answer is already known before using it to measure anything; a silence-asserting pin on an unobservable shape measures nothing.
- **A PRICE OR RESIDUAL MEASURED ON A SUBSET OF THE 8 PROFILES IS A CLAIM ABOUT THAT SUBSET ONLY (round 782).** Rounds 777-781 each quoted a scaffolded price as `compiler N -> M, services N -> M` and concluded "the residual is empty"; the 16th site lived in `src/harness` and was structurally invisible to all four. Run the full 8-profile grid before declaring a family closed — profile-unique files (harness/server/services) are exactly where a last straggler hides.
- **A NARROWING pin whose EXPECTED union happens to equal a type ALIAS declared in the same file reads the ALIAS NAME, not the members (round 764).** Unions are interned and carry `aliasDisplayMap`'s name, so with a `type U = K.A | K.B | K.C` in the prelude, `k !== K.D` reports `Argument of type 'U'` — the pin then measures the alias table instead of what it was written for. Choose a subtraction whose survivors match no declared alias (removing a MIDDLE member also tests member ORDER, which removing the last one cannot).
- **A NARROWING probe must assign to an INCOMPATIBLE type and read the narrowed type out of the error message — "no error here" cannot tell correct narrowing from a reference washed to `never` (round 760).** `never` is assignable to everything, so an over-narrowing is SILENT at every use except a call argument, and its symptom surfaces far away as the DECLARED type ("Argument of type 'Node' …"), which reads like no narrowing happened at all rather than too much. `const s: string = x` after the guard prints `Type 'Node'`/`Type 'Ident'`/nothing-at-all (= `never`) and discriminates all three in one run; the same trick reads any resolved member type (`const s: string = m.kind`).
- **SINCE THE MOD MODULE SPLIT THE TEST XMLs ARE PER-MODULE — `build/test-results/jvmTest/*.xml` AT THE REPO ROOT MATCHES NOTHING, SO THE DOCUMENTED COUNTING SNIPPET PRINTS `0 0 0`, WHICH IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A SUITE THAT NEVER RAN (round 846).** Glob `*/build/test-results/jvmTest/*.xml` and SUM the four modules (core + api + client + daemon); core alone is ~99.5% of the count, so a core-only read looks plausible and is short by ~75 tests.
- **WIPE THE WHOLE `build/test-results/jvmTest` DIR, NEVER JUST `/binary` — the documented incantation was changed to the whole dir; do not "restore" the `/binary` form (rounds 638 + 690).** `/binary` clears the binary results but NOT the per-class XMLs, so a script globbing `build/test-results/jvmTest/*.xml` sums the PREVIOUS run's files too. That is harmless only while the test SET is stable, and it lies silently the moment a change adds or removes tests — a removed test keeps "failing" from its stale XML, and a COUNT that should have moved and did not means the results dir, not the change. Worse for an AWAITED run (round 638): gradle deletes the stale XMLs only when the test task starts, i.e. after the multi-minute compile, so a count-based wait "succeeds" on stale results while the new suite is still compiling. The whole-dir wipe makes any XML seen later fresh by construction.
- **Hand-written test conventions (round-437 sweep, 2026-07-07) — new local tests MUST follow them**: compile via the shared `diagnose(source, directives = "// @strict: true", fileName = "t.ts")` helper (CompilerTestSupport.kt; trimIndents the source and prepends the directives line) — never a per-file `TypeScriptCompiler().compile(...)` helper (EXCEPTION: a test asserting SYMBOL IDENTITY may construct `Checker(options, binderResults)` directly — cf. Inv3PerFileLookupTest — but its fixture fileNames MUST be path-shaped (`/proj/c.ts`): flat corpus-style names silently defeat directory-relative module-specifier resolution, so every import resolves null); assert via the `should`/`have` idiom (`diagnose(...) should { have(none/any { it.code == NNNN }) }`, com.xemantic.kotlin.test) or power-assert `assert(actual == expected)` — never `assertEquals`/`assertTrue(d.isEmpty(), msg)` (round 682 converted 792 `assertEquals` call sites; `assert` and `have` are the ONLY power-assert-transformed functions per build.gradle.kts, and they take NO message — the diagram renders every subexpression value, which beats any hand-written one). TWO traps when writing `assert(a == b)`: an empty-collection comparison must be `assert(xs.isEmpty())`, since `== emptyList()` gives the compiler no way to infer the element type; and an AST-valued subexpression must stay on `assertEquals` (next entry); sources are multiline templates (not buildString), class-shared TS preludes are a trimIndented `private val` concatenated by the caller (`diagnose(prelude + """…""")`), test names are backtick sentences (dots replaced — see the Kotlin backtick-name rule above; use `negative control - …` phrasing for negative tests).
- **Never put an AST node / node list / SourceFile subexpression inside a power-assert-transformed assertion (`have`/`assert` from com.xemantic.kotlin.test)** — on failure the diagram toStrings every captured subexpression, which for a data-class node dumps (or, on deep chains, STACK-OVERFLOWS rendering) whole subtrees, masking the real failure as SOE/OOM. Compute ints/booleans into locals first, or use plain `kotlin.test.fail` with a hand-built message (cf. ForEachChildOracleTest). This is why the round-682 `assertEquals` sweep DELIBERATELY left 27 sites on `assertEquals` — every site in Inv2NodeIndexTest / Inv2LexicalScopeTest / ForEachChildOracleTest whose compared value is a node, a `.copy()`, a `preorder(...)` element or a lexical scope. A `List<Diagnostic>` or a `CompilationResult` is SAFE to render (strings + diagnostics, no AST); a `SourceFile`/`NodeBase`/`Symbol`/lexical scope is not. **This is the ONLY sanctioned exception to the one-idiom rule above** — the 65 surviving `kotlin.test` calls are ALL in the six AST-centric files (Inv2LexicalScopeTest, Inv2NodeIndexTest, Inv3NodeKeyedLookupTest, Inv3PerFileLookupTest, RealLibSnapshotTest, ForEachChildOracleTest); a `grep assertEquals src/` hit outside those files is drift to convert, not precedent to copy.
- **ONE assertion idiom in tests: power-assert `assert(<boolean expr>)` (com.xemantic.kotlin.test) — do NOT reintroduce `kotlin.test.assertEquals`/`assertTrue`/`assertFalse`/`assertNull`/`assertNotNull`/`assertSame`/`assertContains` (round 682 converted 1,009 call sites; only `assert` and `have` are power-assert-transformed per build.gradle.kts `powerAssert.functions`).** They take NO message — the diagram renders every subexpression value, which strictly beats a hand-written one (a broken pin prints the operand value, the `false` verdict AND the receiver's contents). Translations: `assertEquals(exp, act)` → `assert(act == exp)` (ACTUAL first); `assertTrue(c)` → `assert(c)`; `assertFalse(c)` → `assert(!c)` (parenthesise if `c` has a top-level operator, else `!` mis-binds); `assertNull(x)` → `assert(x == null)`; `assertSame(exp, act)` → `assert(act === exp)`; `assertContains(coll, e)` → `assert(e in coll)`; `assertNotNull(x)` → `assert(x != null)` — and in VALUE position (`val v = assertNotNull(expr)`) split into `val v = expr` + `assert(v != null)`: `assert` carries `contract { returns() implies actual }`, so `v` SMART-CASTS to non-null afterwards (no `!!` needed). FOUR traps: an empty-collection compare must be `assert(xs.isEmpty())` (`== emptyList()` gives the compiler no element type to infer); `kotlin.assert` is a different function (see the Kotlin/Native entry above); an AST-valued subexpression must stay on `assertEquals`/`assertNotNull` (next entry); and **a SCRIPTED conversion may re-flow the argument EXPRESSIONS but must NEVER re-flow their string literals** (round 684: whitespace-normalising every rewritten argument collapsed the needle `"…\n    // second comment line"` — whose four spaces ARE the emit indentation the pin exists to check — into one space). Audit any scripted sweep by diffing the multiset of string literals before/after and accounting for EVERY disappearance; the only expected ones are the dropped messages.
- Avoid partial `assert("x" in result)` — always assert the full expected output.
- **A GREP-DERIVED "which pins does this display change blind" POPULATION IS STRUCTURALLY INCOMPLETE, BECAUSE AN EXPECTATION CAN NAME THE THING WITHOUT SPELLING IT.** The sound census is to BUILD THE ARM and RUN every class whose fixtures could reach the mechanism, then read the failures off the XMLs — grep said 13 classes, the run said 14 / 45. **And ask what the NEW target kind does to the READER.** (archive: A GREP-DERIVED "WHICH PINS DOES THIS)
- **A PIN ON AN ARM'S OWN DIAGNOSTIC CODE CANNOT SEE A DROPPED *SUPPRESSION* — when the failure mode ADDS a diagnostic rather than removing one, the seam pin must assert a DIFFERENT code that must NOT appear (round 812).** Both discriminating seams of the `checkDuplicateDeclarations` split fail by letting a SUPERSEDED check run: the arm's own count pin (TS2395 x2 / TS2813 x2) stays green under the ablation and only `none { it.code == 2300 }` moves. Ask what the dropped signal was PROTECTING, and pin that.
- **A SUPPRESSION SEAM CAN BE PROVABLY UNOBSERVABLE, WHICH IS A STRONGER ANSWER THAN A FAILED RETRY (round 812).** If the predicate that reaches an early exit is the term-by-term COMPLEMENT of every guard below it, no input can observe the exit — read the guards before building a purpose-built retry, and record the pin as an ARM pin.
- **A NARROWING pin that asserts a TS2339 APPEARS discriminates NOTHING — it passes on a working AND a broken build, for opposite reasons (round 751).** `case K.Alpha: return x.b` errors both when narrowing correctly REMOVED `B` (missing on `A`) and when narrowing never ran at all (missing on `A | B`); two such pins passed on the pristine build they were written to fail on. Assert the NEGATIVE branch instead — `default: return x.b` with `none { it.code == 2339 }` — which only a build that actually subtracted the constituent can satisfy. Same rule for any "the filter removed something" pin: assert what becomes LEGAL, not what stays broken.
- **A MEMO WHOSE DECISION READS MUTABLE STATE CANNOT BE VALIDATED BY INSPECTING ITS ANSWERS — A FROZEN-WRONG ENTRY LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A CORRECT ONE** (a first touch before an enum's values exist returns the symbol itself, indistinguishable from a genuinely-canonical one). The only probe that sees it recomputes the decision with the memo BYPASSED and diffs. Applies to any order-dependent memo in the checker. (archive: A memo whose decision reads MUTABLE)
- **A SEAM PIN IS ONLY A SEAM PIN ONCE IT HAS FAILED AGAINST THE MISTAKE IT NAMES — AN AMBIENT REINSTALL UPSTREAM CAN MAKE A WHOLE CLASS OF SEAMS UNOBSERVABLE** (the per-statement anchor sandwich restores `currentLocalTypes`, so a scope restore dropped inside a walker's arm is ERASED at the statement boundary; the leak is observable only WITHIN one statement). **And a shape validated with the project CLI is not automatically a valid pin.** (archive: A SEAM PIN IS ONLY A SEAM PIN ONCE)
- **DO NOT PIN A KNOWN-OPEN GAP AS A "control" — it is a countdown, not a guard (round 765, one suite failure).** Round 764 pinned "a switch `default:` still answers the whole enum" so the gap would read as measured; round 765 closed the gap and the pin failed by construction. Record an open gap in the SESSION NOTE instead.
